True click-to-world resolution would require inverse-projecting the SVG click point back through `_trajProj3`/zoom/anchor into world km and then bisecting the sample array by distance — the inverse of a non-invertible orthographic projection (multiple world points along the view ray project to the same screen point) without an extra depth cue. Nearest-to-view-time is deterministic, cheap, and matches what the user is already looking at (the "current" vehicle-position dot on the polyline).
- **Handle geometry**: local-frame axes (rHat, vHat, hHat) come straight from `physAimBurnState`'s returned basis (non-Gram-Schmidt-orthonormalized — same basis 565 actually applies dv against, so the gizmo can't drift from what will be flown). Each handle direction is `_trajProj3(hat) → normalize in SCREEN space` (`_trajGizmoScreenDir`, pure/gate-tested); handle LENGTH is a constant 22 RENDER units (not km, not px) — because render-space geometry is already `km·zoom` baked into a fixed ±200 viewBox (the two-layer design's whole point), a constant render-unit length maps to an approximately constant on-screen px length at ANY zoom without the gizmo needing to know the current zoom factor at all. The normal handle's screen-projected length shrinks toward zero at top-down (el=90°) camera angles by construction (h⊥ecliptic, camera looking straight down the same axis); below a 0.05 (render-space) length threshold it swaps to a diamond glyph pinned to a fixed fallback screen direction rather than collapsing to an unusable point-sized knob.
- **Drag → Δv mapping** (`_trajGizmoPxToDv`, pure/gate-tested): **gain = 2 m/s per screen pixel, dragged along the handle's own screen direction (dot product of the mouse delta with the handle's unit screen vector — not raw x/y)**, 0.2 m/s/px with Shift held (fine control). Both constants are HAND-TUNED, chosen so a ~100 px drag (a comfortable mouse gesture) produces a ~200 m/s burn — the right order of magnitude for a LEO-to-transfer-orbit-class maneuver — and are explicitly ZOOM-INDEPENDENT (screen px in, m/s out; no reference to `cam.wKm`/zoom anywhere in the mapping), matching the plan's "px-to-m/s gain ~ zoom-independent" instruction. Dragging past the handle's zero point flips the sign naturally (the along-handle projection goes negative), authoring retrograde/anti-radial/anti-normal without a separate UI affordance.
- **Live preview = SCRATCH propagation only**: every drag tick (throttled to one `physPropagateSegment` call per ~100 ms via `_trajGizmoScheduleScratch`/`setTimeout`) computes `state = {r: node.r, v: node.v + dvVec}` and calls `physPropagateSegment(state, met, met+horizon, {center, bodies, overrides:{}}, {maxSamples:128})` — results land in a plain module-local `_trajGizmo.preview` var, NEVER `_physTrajByMission` (the sanctioned side-table for COMMITTED legs) and never `m.log`. The horizon is `min(30 d, 3×period)` for a bound elliptical result, else 30 d flat — same convention 565's real MNODE leg builder uses. The preview renders as a dashed `var(--accent)` path drawn directly into a dedicated `<g class="traj-gizmo-preview">` child of the scene layer, updated in place (not a full `_trajWorldSVG` re-render) — cheap enough for 10 Hz dragging. **Scope limitation (documented, not fixed this phase)**: the scene-preview draw only plots samples whose `frame` equals the node's own body — an SOI-handoff mid-preview (e.g. dragging a lunar-departure burn hard enough to leave Earth's SOI in the scratch propagation) silently truncates the drawn dashed path at the handoff rather than continuing into the new frame, unlike the committed-leg renderer (`_trajPhysLegRender`) which has multi-frame anchoring. Acceptable for the common case (tuning a burn that stays in one SOI); a future pass should reuse `_trajPolylineSVG`'s anchor-resolution machinery for the preview too.
- **Mutation discipline**: the log is touched exactly ONCE, on mouseup (`_trajGizmoCommit`) — either `missionExecManeuverNode(id, {value_s, dvPro_ms, dvRad_ms, dvNrm_ms})` for a new node, or a direct field mutation (`e.dvPro_ms = ...`) on the existing MNODE entry followed by `missionRecompute(m)` for an edit — both paths end in exactly one `missionRecompute` call, so `missionUndoCapture`'s hook at the recompute tail produces exactly one undo step per gizmo interaction, matching `missionExecManeuverNode`'s pre-existing single-step contract. Escape or right-click (`contextmenu`, default-prevented) DURING a drag calls `_trajGizmoCancelDrag`, which reverts `dv[axis]` to its pre-drag value and clears the preview — zero log mutation, verified structurally (the commit path is only ever reached from `_trajGizmoHandleUp`, never from the cancel path).
- **Editing existing MNODEs**: `_trajSelectEventFromView` (574) now also calls `_trajGizmoOnEventSelected`, which opens the gizmo pre-loaded with the event's `dvPro_ms/dvRad_ms/dvNrm_ms` when an MNODE event is selected (ring/polyline click, or the event-card list — same shared selection path), and detaches a currently-attached COMMITTED gizmo when anything else is selected. A ✕ glyph in the readout plate (and Escape when not mid-drag) calls `_trajGizmoClose()` — dismisses the gizmo without touching the log.
- **Re-render survival** (item 5 of the plan): `_trajGizmoRepaintOverlay` re-derives `_trajGizmo.node` from the mission's CURRENT log/`orbitAtBurn` every repaint whenever the gizmo is attached to a committed event (`authIdx != null`) — so a gizmo survives `missionRenderDetail()` (undo/redo, another edit elsewhere) with up-to-date geometry, and self-closes if its event was undone out from under it. A PENDING (uncommitted, `authIdx == null`) gizmo is NOT auto-dropped on unrelated re-renders in this implementation — the plan explicitly allows either behavior ("may drop... acceptable"); this implementation keeps it live since dropping it silently on an incidental re-render (e.g. a camera-only `_trajApplyCam` call, which does NOT go through this code path at all, or another selection elsewhere) would discard in-progress authoring more often than the plan's phrasing implies is desirable. Gizmo state is never read by `_buildSessionObject`/autosave — confirmed by construction (it is a `let` in 5745, never assigned onto any object that path serializes).
- **Known scope limits** (new critique below): the ring-click MET simplification means dragging directly on the schematic transfer arc (before it has physics samples) is not currently a placement path at all — only mission ORBIT RINGS and PHYSICS POLYLINES spawn gizmos, per the plan's explicit list; the scratch-preview single-frame limitation above; and the pending-node approximate reconstruction (current-orbit, not point-in-history) above.

Gate (20 new asserts, `tests/math.test.js`, R3.3 block — `src/js/5745-maneuver-gizmo.js` added to the harness's `FILES` list; DOM-touching functions in that file are excluded from testing by construction, only the six pure helpers are exercised): axis orthonormality and the degenerate zero-velocity case for `_trajGizmoAxes`; the px→dv gain (normal + shift-fine + sign flip + zero) for `_trajGizmoPxToDv`; nearest-sample MET resolution including the empty-array case for `_trajGizmoNearestSampleMet`; readout string formatting (sign prefixes, MET mm:ss) for `_trajGizmoFormatReadout`; and screen-direction normalization plus the degenerate-fallback branch for `_trajGizmoScreenDir`.

Browser verification for this phase (drag-to-200-m/s, normal-handle tilt behavior, Escape-cancels-cleanly, edit-existing-MNODE-updates-not-duplicates, and the pan/zoom/rotate regression sweep from the task brief's item (e)) could **not** be captured in this session: both `preview_*` (returned "not available — use the ext-shaped equivalent") and `claude-in-chrome`'s `tabs_context_mcp` (returned "Claude in Chrome is not connected") were unreachable in this environment, consistent with the R3.2 session's note above. What WAS verified: the pure-math gate (317 assertions, up from 297 pre-R3.3, all green under `python build.py`); `node --check` syntax validation of the full concatenated build; and a direct grep of the built `lv_calc.html` confirming the interactive wiring is actually present in the shipped artifact (`ondblclick="_trajGizmoRingDblClick(...)"` / `_trajGizmoLegDblClick(...)` on ring and polyline hit-paths, `onmousedown="...;_trajGizmoHandleDown(event,'pro'/'rad'/'nrm')"` with `event.stopPropagation()` ahead of the handler call — so a handle grab cannot leak into `trajPanStart`, `_trajGizmoOnEventSelected` wired into `_trajSelectEventFromView`). The interactive drag loop itself — the core "drag, watch the trajectory bend" experience this phase exists to build — remains UNVERIFIED end-to-end and should be exercised the next time browser tooling is available in this environment.

### R3.4 — Gizmo usability + KSP parity (2026-07-10, same-day follow-up on user first-flight feedback)

Four changes, all additive to R3.3's model above (the axis basis, mutation discipline, and pending/committed reconstruction rules are UNCHANGED):

- **Camera rework (574, the enabler)**: `trajPanStart`/`trajPanMove` no longer branch on `mode` — every left-drag (plain, shift, or right-button) now rotates az/el; the entire pan branch (`worldDx`/`worldDy` world-delta math, the mid-drag viewBox slide, the overlay `translate()` hack) is DELETED, not merely dead-code-guarded. `trajWheelZoom` no longer does cursor-anchored offset math — zoom is now a pure `wKm` change about the anchor. `cam.relOffsetKm` stays on the camera struct (the fit/zoom-to-content math in `_trajFitWKmForBody`/`_trajCamCenterKm` still reads it) but is now written ONLY as `{x:0, y:0}` everywhere (verified by grep — no assignment writes a non-zero value post-R3.4). This is the KSP camera contract (drag rotates, scroll zooms, click focuses) and is what makes six 12px handles reliably grabbable — under the old model, a drag that started even slightly off a handle would pan the whole scene out from under the cursor.
- **Six pull-out handles** (`_TRAJ_GIZMO_HANDLES`, 5745): replaces R3.3's 3 bidirectional handles. Each of the 3 axes (prograde/radial/normal) now has TWO handles, one per sign, and each handle owns only its own non-negative "side": `_trajGizmoHandleSideMag(dv0, alongPx, shiftHeld) = max(0, dv0 + gain·alongPx)` where `dv0` is the handle's OWN side magnitude at drag-start (`max(component,0)` for a "+" handle, `max(-component,0)` for "-"). Pulling a handle away from the node always increases its own side; dragging back toward the node reduces it to exactly 0 and stops — it can never cross into the opposite handle's negative territory (that handle must be grabbed instead). This matches KSP's model and removes R3.3's "drag past zero flips the sign" behavior, which was disorienting once six independent knobs are on screen (a sign flip on a knob you're NOT currently watching is surprising). Visual: the "+" handle is a solid filled knob; its "-" partner is the SAME color as a dashed hollow ring (outline distinction only — no second hue introduced, per the theming rule against inventing new chromatic literals). Hit circles are a transparent 12px-radius circle laid over a smaller (7px) visible knob, satisfying the ≥12px grab target without inflating the glyph. Hover (`_trajGizmoHoverKnob`) is a pure `setAttribute` swap on the visible knob's `r`/`stroke-width`/`opacity` via `onmouseenter`/`onmouseleave` — no re-render, per the plan.
- **Node-time control** (center knob, `_trajGizmoCenterDown/Move/Up`, 5745): the center knob's hit circle (≥12px) now does double duty. A DRAG (movement > 3px, the same click-vs-drag threshold used elsewhere in 574/570) slides the node's MET along its orbit: the mouse delta is projected onto the node's prograde SCREEN direction (`dirs.pro`, already computed for the handle geometry) to get `pxAlong`, then `dMET = _trajGizmoCenterDragDMet(pxAlong, kmPerPx, vMagKms) = pxAlong · kmPerPx / |v|` where `kmPerPx = cam.wKm / rect.width` (the viewBox-to-px ratio at the CURRENT zoom — re-derived every drag start, so zooming between gizmo opens changes the drag's km/px scale correctly). MET is clamped `>= 0` and the node state is re-derived from `_trajGizmoNodeState(m, newMet, authIdx)` (SAME reconstruction path as everywhere else — theta = n·MET off the recorded/current orbit) on every move tick, live-updating the preview. A CLICK (no drag) toggles a small flyout menu (`.traj-gizmo-menu`, plain HTML div positioned canvas-relative via `_trajWorldToScreen`'s already-canvas-relative px output — NOT inside the SVG overlay, since it needs ordinary DOM click handlers) with "+1 orbit" / "-1 orbit" (MET ± the orbital period, `_trajGizmoOrbitPeriodMet(mu,r,v)` — a pure vis-viva `a = 1/(2/r − v²/μ)` → `T = 2π√(a³/μ)`, returns `null` for a non-elliptical state so the buttons are inert rather than wrong) and "Delete node" / "Discard node" (committed: `m.log.splice(authIdx,1)` + `missionRecompute` — the standard mutation path, one recompute/one undo step; pending: just closes the gizmo, nothing was ever pushed to the log). Center-drag release on a COMMITTED node commits `e.at.value_s = newMet` through the same one-recompute discipline as a handle release; on a PENDING node it only updates local gizmo state (nothing to commit until a handle drag actually authors the node).
- **Closest-approach pair** (`_trajGizmoClosestApproach`/`_trajGizmoSoiEntryT`/`_trajGizmoPickTarget`, 5745, pure + gate-tested): target selection scans `m.log` forward from the node's MET for the next `MANEUVER` entry whose `toNode` resolves (via `_missionNmNodeById`) to a body different from the node's own body; failing that, defaults to the Moon for an Earth-centric node; otherwise no CA pair is shown. `_trajGizmoClosestApproach(samples, targetBody, railFn)` scans a leg/preview's `samples` array and returns the sample nearest the target, FRAME-AWARE: if a sample's `frame` already equals `targetBody` (post-SOI-transition) the distance is `|s.r|` directly; otherwise the target's real position (`physBodyStateAt`, default `railFn`) is resolved into the SAME frame as the sample (`target.r − frameBody.r`, both heliocentric) before differencing — exact across an SOI-patched propagation without ever converting the craft's own coordinates. `_trajGizmoSoiEntryT` finds the first sample whose frame flips to the target, used purely as the "ENCOUNTER" trigger and the T+ time shown in that state. Rendering: a small × marker at the craft's CA position (drawn in the gizmo's OVERLAY svg, screen-projected the same way as the handle geometry) plus a `_trajGhostMarker`-style ghost of the target body AT its real CA-time position (drawn into the scene preview layer, same frame-matching caveat as the rest of the scratch preview below), plus a plate line — `"CA {km} km · in T+…"` normally, `"ENCOUNTER · T+…"` (accent-highlighted) once `_trajGizmoSoiEntryT` fires.
- **Fidelity ladder** (user-requested mid-phase addendum, `_trajGizmoScheduleScratch`/`_trajGizmoRunScratch(mode)`/`_trajGizmoPreviewFidelity`, 5745): dragging (handle or center) now runs TWO propagation cadences instead of one. On the existing ~100ms move-tick throttle, a CHEAP pass runs — `physPropagateSegment` with `ctx.bodies` restricted to `[node.body]` (perturbers dropped) and `maxSamples: 64` — reusing the SAME integrator entry point as the full pass rather than a hand-rolled two-body loop (simpler, and the expensive part of a full pass is the multi-body perturbation SUM inside `physPropagateSegment`, not call overhead, so restricting `bodies` genuinely cuts cost). Every move also (re)arms a 2000 ms debounce timer (`_TRAJ_GIZMO_FULL_DEBOUNCE_MS`, HAND-TUNED, no calibration against frame budget or machine speed — picked as "long enough that continuous dragging never triggers it, short enough that a paused drag refines within about the time a user re-reads the readout plate"); once the node has been left alone (no move tick) for that long while the gizmo is still attached to an active drag, ONE full-fidelity pass runs (`bodies` = the original full set, `maxSamples: 128` — identical to R3.3's original always-full scratch) and REPLACES the preview + CA pair. The decision of which mode to run is a pure, gate-tested function — `_trajGizmoPreviewFidelity(lastMoveMs, nowMs, debounceMs=2000) → 'cheap'|'full'` — the actual `setTimeout` scheduling around it is plumbing and is NOT gate-tested (per the module's existing DOM-plumbing/pure-helper split). A cheap-mode CA result is flagged `approx:true` and the plate readout prefixes it with `~` (`~CA 12,340 km · in T+…` / `~ENCOUNTER · …`) so the user knows the number will sharpen once they pause. On release/commit the behavior is unchanged from R3.3 — the REAL committed leg always rebuilds at full fidelity through the normal `missionRecompute`/`_trajPhysLegRender` path; the ladder only governs the SCRATCH preview shown mid-drag.
- **Known scope carry-overs from R3.3** (unchanged by this phase): the pending-node approximate reconstruction (current orbit, not point-in-history); the scratch preview's single-body-frame draw limit (now doubly true — the CHEAP pass literally cannot see other bodies at all by construction, and even the FULL pass's drawn PATH still only plots same-frame samples, though the CA helper itself is frame-aware and will correctly report an encounter even though the drawn dashed line stops at the SOI boundary); ring-click MET-inversion is still skipped (superseded in spirit by center-drag, which now provides an interactive along-orbit adjustment after placement — closing part of the R3.3 "future pass" note).

Gate: 26 new asserts added to the R3.3 block in `tests/math.test.js` (343 total, up from 317): `_trajGizmoHandleSideMag` (pull-away increases, push-back clamps at 0 and never goes negative, fine-drag scaling); `_trajGizmoCenterDragDMet` (the gain formula, zero/negative `vMagKms` guard, sign preservation); `_trajGizmoOrbitPeriodMet` (positive/finite for a circular Earth LEO state, matches `2π√(a³/μ)` for a=r circular, a +1/-1 orbit round-trips MET exactly, `null` for a hyperbolic state); `_trajGizmoClosestApproach` (finds a KNOWN synthetic minimum exactly, matches across a frame-aware re-derivation, correctly handles a sample already re-framed to the target, empty/no-target guards); `_trajGizmoSoiEntryT` (finds the first re-framed sample, `null` when none); a determinism pair (identical inputs → identical outputs) for both `_trajGizmoHandleSideMag` and `_trajGizmoClosestApproach`; `_trajGizmoPreviewFidelity` (boundary behavior at the debounce threshold, custom `debounceMs`).

Headless verification (browser tooling was confirmed unreachable again this session — `preview_screenshot` returned "not available"): grepped the built `lv_calc.html` and confirmed (a) `worldDx` no longer appears anywhere in `trajPanMove`/the pan branch (it's gone, not dead code), (b) `const mode = 'rotate'` is unconditional in `trajPanStart`, (c) all 6 handle definitions (`key: 'pro'/'retro'/'radOut'/'radIn'/'nrm'/'antinrm'`) plus `_trajGizmoHoverKnob` wiring are present, (d) `_trajGizmoCenterDown` and `.traj-gizmo-menu` markup are wired, (e) `_trajGizmoClosestApproach`/`_trajGizmoPickTarget` and the fidelity-ladder symbols (`_trajGizmoPreviewFidelity`, `_TRAJ_GIZMO_FULL_DEBOUNCE_MS`) are present in the shipped artifact. Beyond the gate, a standalone `vm` smoke (not part of the committed gate — ad hoc, run against the SAME module set the test harness loads) built a REAL translunar-class departure state via `physAimBurnState` off a 185 km Earth parking orbit plus a 3.15 km/s prograde burn, propagated it 5 days with `physPropagateSegment(bodies:['Earth','Sun','Moon'])`, and confirmed `_trajGizmoClosestApproach` against the Moon exactly matches an independently brute-forced minimum over the same real sample set (not a canned array) and that the reported CA time falls within the propagation span. That particular departure geometry (theta=0, unphased against the Moon's actual position) does not itself reach lunar SOI — proving the helper is CORRECT on real physics samples, not that this specific burn is a real translunar trajectory; SOI-encounter behavior is covered by synthetic-data gate assertions instead. The full interactive loop (drag responsiveness, hover swap, center-drag time-slide feel, menu, fidelity-ladder visual settling) remains UNVERIFIED end-to-end pending browser tooling.

### R6.2' Phase B step 5 — solved-maneuver decomposition basis fix (565/5745, 2026-07-10)

The gizmo's handle readout for a solved maneuver (§7k's basis) and 565's display-only `dvPro_ms/dvRad_ms/dvNrm_ms` mirror on a unified `MNODE(mode:'solved')` entry both decompose the leg's real `dvVec` via `_trajGizmoDecomposeDv` against a local (v̂,r̂,ĥ) triad built by `_trajGizmoAxes(r,v)`. Through R6.2' Phase A that triad came from the MEAN-MOTION reconstruction (`_trajGizmoOrbitNodeAt`/565's mean-anomaly builder) — the right convention for a MANUAL vector burn (which literally IS authored against that reconstruction), but the WRONG anomaly for a shooter-refined moon/interplanetary leg, whose real departure point (`aim.theta/pitch/yaw` from `physShootLegAim`) sits elsewhere on the parking ring. This made a physically ~pure-prograde Apollo TLI read as pro −2085/rad +2151/nrm +950 m/s — magnitude-correct (the basis is orthonormal so `|dvVec|` round-trips exactly regardless of theta) but directionally misleading.

**Fix**: `physSolveNodeBurn`/the n-body shooter branch/the same-body analytic branch (565) now each stamp `leg.burnState = {r, v}` — the actual PRE-burn departure state the dv vector was applied to (the shooter's refined `physAimBurnState` result when converged, else the analytic seed's pre-burn state; `physAimBurnState` itself now also returns `vPre`, the circular velocity before dv is added, so callers don't need to re-subtract). Both the gizmo's `_trajGizmoManeuverNodeState` (when given a `missionId`/`authIdx`) and 565's own dv-component mirror decompose against `_trajGizmoAxes(leg.burnState.r, leg.burnState.v)` when present, falling back to the mean-motion reconstruction for legs that haven't computed a burnState (or for a genuinely manual MNODE, which correctly still wants the mean-motion basis it was authored against). ΔV ACCOUNTING is untouched by any of this — `dvRequired`/`dv_actual` still come from `progNmComputeEdgeDv`/`dvOverride` exclusively; only the pro/rad/nrm DISPLAY numbers and, per the detach fidelity note below, the DETACHED trajectory shape change.

**Detach fidelity**: dragging a handle on a solved maneuver still detaches it to a manual `MNODE(mode:'manual')` (§7k's `_trajGizmoDetachManeuverIfNeeded`), seeded with the solved pro/rad/nrm — but now the detach ALSO stamps `e.burnState = leg.burnState` (plain `{r:[...],v:[...]}` arrays, JSON-safe, rides autosave/session export fine) onto the log entry. 565's manual-MNODE leg builder checks for `e.burnState` first and, when present, applies the dv vector in THAT exact recorded state's basis (via `_trajGizmoAxes`) instead of reconstructing from mean-motion phase — so the detached leg's propagated path tracks the ORIGINAL solved leg's real path, not a path departing from a different point on the ring. Re-solving (`missionMnodeResolveToTarget`, 570) deletes the `burnState` stamp — the solver takes over the departure state again, and a later detach re-captures whatever the solver produces this time (never a stale burnState from a previous solve).

Note on the basis itself: `_trajGizmoAxes` builds rHat/vHat/hHat by independently normalizing r and v (NOT Gram-Schmidt-orthogonalized) — exactly invertible (compose→decompose round-trips components, not just magnitude) only when r⊥v (a purely tangential-velocity state, which every `physAimBurnState`-constructed circular parking-orbit state IS by construction). A state with a radial velocity component would make the triad non-orthogonal and the decomposition components would no longer round-trip exactly (magnitude still would, via the orthonormal-in-practice-for-orbital-states triad) — not a concern here since every burnState this pass stamps comes from `physAimBurnState`'s own circular-ring construction.

Gate: 10 new assertions (475 total, up from 465) — `_trajGizmoAxes` right-hand-triad sanity on a synthetic circular state; decomposition against a recorded basis for a mixed pro/rad/nrm case; a TLI-like case (dv purely along the recorded vHat) decomposing to ~pure prograde (|rad|,|nrm| < 1e-6 m/s); and a compose→decompose round-trip identity on a tilted-but-tangential (r⊥v) state.

## 7l. J2 / orbital-economy layer R4 (385/386, 2026-07-10)

Closed-form secular precession from Earth's (and other bodies') oblateness, plus an opt-in integrator term so the effect can be DRAWN on a real propagated orbit, not just quoted as a number.

- **`PROG_BODY_J2`** (385): a data map, not a model — `{Earth: 1.08263e-3, Mars: 1.9555e-3, Moon: 2.033e-4, Venus: 4.458e-6}`. Bodies absent from the map (e.g. Jupiter, Titan) have NO J2 model; callers must treat that as "no data" (functions return `null`), never silently assume zero oblateness.
- **`physJ2NodalRate(aKm, e, iRad, body)`**: standard first-order secular result dΩ/dt = −(3/2)·J2·n·(R/p)²·cos(i), p = a(1−e²), n = √(μ/a³). Sign convention (pinned in the gate): a RETROGRADE orbit (i > 90°, cos i < 0) gets a POSITIVE nodal rate (eastward node regression) — this is what makes sun-synchronous orbits (i ≈ 98°) work, matching Earth's ≈ +0.98565°/day mean heliocentric drift.
- **`physJ2ApsidalRate(aKm, e, iRad, body)`**: dω/dt = (3/4)·J2·n·(R/p)²·(5cos²i − 1) — vanishes at the "critical inclination" i ≈ 63.4° (Molniya's actual design inclination, chosen historically for exactly this reason: an apogee that doesn't drift).
- **`physJ2SunSyncCheck(aKm, e, iRad, body)`**: compares the nodal rate (deg/day) against Earth's mean heliocentric rate 360°/365.2422 days = 0.98565°/day, `sunSync:true` within 0.02°/day. Earth-only by construction (other bodies always report `sunSync:false` — "sun-synchronous" is a specifically Earth-relative launch-planning concept, not exported to Mars/Venus/Moon orbits here).
- **Readout chip** (230-orbit-diagram.js, Define-an-Orbit panel): when inclination is authored and the target orbit resolves, a compact line renders in the mini-diagram's footer — `"J2: node −0.99°/day · apsis +0.12°/day"`, plus an accent-colored `"· sun-synchronous ✓"` when `physJ2SunSyncCheck` passes. Earth-only (the diagram itself is Earth-centric only, per its own header comment) — no chip for escape-mode or other bodies.
- **Optional integrator term** (`physAccel`, 386): `ctx.j2 === true` opts a propagation INTO the J2 acceleration term for its center body (if that body has a `PROG_BODY_J2` entry); DEFAULT OFF — no existing caller sets `ctx.j2`, so this phase is zero behavior change for every currently-shipped propagation (mission legs, shooter, gizmo preview, free-return solve), gate-pinned bit-identical. Form (Vallado sign convention): a_J2 = −(3/2)·J2·μ·R²/r⁵ · [x(1−5z²/r²), y(1−5z²/r²), z(3−5z²/r²)].
- **`ctx.j2` must survive `physPropagateSegment`'s internal ctx rebuild** — the function constructs its own working `ctxNow` per call and only carries forward fields it explicitly lists; `j2` was added to that list alongside `center`/`bodies`/`overrides`/`railFn`/`dtMax` (an easy place for a new ctx field to silently vanish mid-propagation if not threaded through).
- **Pinned in the gate**: 800 km @ 98.6° nodal ≈ +0.9856°/day (within 0.03) and reports sun-synchronous; Molniya (a=26562, e=0.74, i=63.4°) apsidal ≈ 0 (within 0.01°/day); ISS-like 400 km @ 51.6° nodal ≈ −5.0°/day (within 0.3) and NOT sun-synchronous; a body with no J2 entry returns `null` from both rate functions and the sun-sync check; `physAccel` with `ctx.j2` unset/false is bit-identical to the pre-R4 acceleration, `ctx.j2:true` measurably differs; a 5-orbit numerical propagation of the 800 km/98.6° case with `ctx.j2:true` shows RAAN regressing in the sign and rough magnitude the closed-form secular rate predicts (generous 2° tolerance — a coarse trend check, not a precision pin).

## 7m. Body surface rendering + LOD ladder R6.2 (236/574, 2026-07-10)

**Presentation layer only — no physics/ΔV consequence.** Replaces the trajectory view's single clamped-circle body glyph with a three-tier ladder keyed off the body's TRUE (unclamped) apparent screen radius `trueR_km · zoom`: **surfaced disc** (≥24 px — base color + coastlines/maria/region ellipses/cloud bands + a limb-shading overlay), **plain disc** (2–24 px — the pre-existing colored circle with the `_TRAJ_MIN_BODY_PX` floor), **symbol chip** (<2 px — a constant-radius outlined circle with the body's astronomical glyph, reusing the same symbols as the Orbits page destination picker, 060-orbit-categories.js). `trueR_km` now comes from the real `PROG_BODIES[body].R` (was a schematic constant `3` for every planet/moon before this phase — that schematic value is also why the old glyph could shrink arbitrarily far below the intended floor at extreme zoom-out; the floor math itself, `_trajBodyPxR`'s `Math.max(trueR·zoom, _TRAJ_MIN_BODY_PX)`, was already correct and is unchanged).

- **Surface geometry** (`_trajLatLonUnit`, `_trajSpinRotate`, `_trajGeoPolyPath`, `_trajGeoEllipsePath`, `_trajSurfacedDiscSVG`, 574): lat/lon → unit-sphere point in the body's own frame → rotated by the body's spin angle about the WORLD +z (ecliptic-normal) axis → projected through the pass camera (`_trajProj3`, the one projection seam) → scaled to the disc's screen radius. Front-hemisphere only (camera depth ≥ 0); polygon runs break (new subpath) at the limb, same spirit as the existing `viewClampUnits` pattern — filled paths with limb breaks are an accepted rudimentary approximation, not survey-grade cartography.
- **Data source** (236-geo-data.js, new this phase): `PROG_GEO_EARTH` — Natural Earth 110m coastline polygons, Douglas-Peucker simplified, packed as flat integer-tenths-of-a-degree arrays. `PROG_GEO_FEATURES` — Moon maria + Mars regions as `{lat, lon, rLat, rLon, kind}` ellipse approximations from published feature centers (USGS Gazetteer). `PROG_GEO_STYLE` — per-body fill palette, a DATA-palette exemption from the no-chromatic-literals chrome rule (same class as `PROG_BODY_COLORS`, 570). Jupiter/Saturn get no feature data — their bands are procedural lat-strips through the same projection (so they visibly tilt with camera elevation, not a flat texture).
- **Spin angle is a display convention, not a calibrated physical rotation** (`_trajBodySpinAngle`, 574): **Earth** = `2π·viewT_s/86164.1` (sidereal day), **Mars** = `2π·viewT_s/88642.66` (sidereal day) — both with an implicit epoch offset of 0 (NOT tied to any real prime-meridian-at-epoch reference; a Greenwich-facing coastline at viewT=0 is coincidence, not GMST). **Moon**: tidally locked — `progBodyAngleAt('Moon', t) + π`, i.e. the Moon's own Earth-relative orbital position angle (which IS the Earth→Moon direction) plus π, so the nearside meridian (lon 0, where the packed maria live) faces the ACTUAL Moon→Earth direction at every viewT, not a fixed angle. All other bodies: spin angle 0 (no vector surface features are drawn for them, so the angle is unused).
- **Axial tilt is NOT modeled — the single biggest honesty gap of this phase**: every body's spin axis is assumed coincident with the ecliptic normal (`_trajLatLonUnit`'s `+z`). Real axial tilts (Earth 23.4°, Mars 25.2°, others un-modeled here) mean the drawn terminator/pole geometry and the exact limb-crossing latitude of near-polar features are visibly wrong whenever the camera isn't near top-down (`el ≈ 90°`) — acceptable for the "mission-planning flavor" this phase targets, wrong for anything claiming seasonal or lighting accuracy. See critique 55.
- **Limb shading**: one `<radialGradient>` per surfaced-disc instance (unique id per render pass, `_trajGeoIdSeq`), transparent center → ~30–35% black rim (`PROG_GEO_STYLE[body].limb`, a neutral alpha scrim — allowed per the theming exemptions), center offset ~32% of the disc radius toward the Sun's SCREEN direction (a cheap 2D `atan2` between the already-projected Sun and body render positions, not a 3D sun-vector solve) — a rudimentary day-side/terminator cue, not physically-lit shading.
- **Perf clamp**: if the disc's true screen radius exceeds 4× the viewport diagonal (camera deep inside a body, disc mostly off-screen), `_trajSurfacedDiscSVG` skips surface-geometry sampling entirely and emits the base-color fill + limb gradient only — bounds path-string size for the worst-case zoom without a separate viewport-region clip of the lat/lon data itself.
- **Gate** (`tests/math.test.js`, 16 new assertions, 2026-07-10): `_trajLatLonUnit` lands the expected axis points and stays unit-length; `_trajSpinRotate` about +z leaves the pole fixed and rotates the equator correctly; `_trajBodySpinAngle` is monotonic in viewT for Earth/Mars, zero for a body with no vector surface (Venus), and matches `progBodyAngleAt('Moon', t) + π` exactly for the Moon; `_trajTrueBodyRadiusKm` returns the real `PROG_BODIES[body].R` (not the old schematic 3) and the documented Sun constant.

**R6.2 round-4 fixes (574, 2026-07-11)** — three defects found in the round-3 flight test:
- **Exact hemisphere clip, not per-vertex culling** (`_trajHemiClipRuns`, `_trajClippedRunsToPath`): the original approach dropped back-facing vertices and let the SVG fill close the resulting gap with a straight chord through the disc interior — visible pole-on as giant wedges of land color sweeping across the whole disc. The fix clips each polygon (coastlines, maria/region ellipses, gas-giant bands — all three call sites now route through the one clipper) against the front hemisphere (`_trajProj3(...).depth ≥ 0`) and, for every edge that crosses the boundary, interpolates the two endpoint UNIT VECTORS linearly and renormalizes — exact, not approximate, because depth is linear in the input vector, so the interpolated point has depth exactly 0 and therefore lands exactly on the unit circle. Consecutive open runs are stitched with an SVG elliptical arc along the limb (radius `rPx`) instead of a chord; the short-way arc is used when the winding direction is ambiguous (acceptable at coastline/maria polygon scale — the existing disc `clipPath` remains as a second safety net). A real bug surfaced during gate-test authoring: a polygon whose front run straddles the array's start/end seam (dirs[n−1] front, dirs[0] front, connected the "long way" around through the back) got split into a spurious extra fragment by a naive single-pass loop; fixed by splicing the loop-end open run onto the front of `runs[0]` when a wraparound continuation is detected. 5 new gate assertions (front-only/back-only/single-crossing/double-crossing/wraparound-continuity).
- **Stable per-instance ids, not a per-render counter** (defect2): `_trajGeoIdSeq` minted a fresh `<radialGradient>`/`<clipPath>` id every ~33ms rebuild during rotation; since the world layer is swapped wholesale via `innerHTML`, a paint could land between "old ids torn down" and "new ids wired up," reading as a one-frame flicker. Replaced with a deterministic id per body per render instance (`traj-geo-<missionId>-<body>-limb/-clip`) — the wholesale swap now recreates the SAME ids atomically every pass, so no `url(#)` reference ever dangles. Verified stable across 20 synthetic rotation frames (same id string every frame) and non-colliding between Earth and its Moon in the same pass.
- **Real terminator, not a 2D screen-dot offset** (`_trajTerminatorNightPath`, defect3): the limb-shading gradient's day-side offset (unchanged, still a cheap 2D `atan2` cue) made day/night unreadable at a glance. Added an actual terminator: the body→Sun unit vector is now carried as a WORLD-frame 3-vector (`sunDir3`, not a screen angle) all the way into the disc renderer; its camera-projected `depth` component is the orthographic terminator ellipse's minor-axis fraction. Rather than solving the ellipse algebraically, the terminator great circle (parametrized by two world-frame vectors spanning the plane ⊥ `sunDir3`, NOT spin-rotated — illumination depends on sun geometry, not the body's own rotation) is sampled and pushed through the SAME hemisphere clipper coastlines use (doubles as the "ellipse feature" clipper), giving the front-visible terminator arc exactly. That arc plus the correct limb arc (the one that avoids the Sun's own screen angle — the other limb arc is day-side) closes the night region, filled with the existing neutral black-alpha scrim (0.4, allowed exemption) clipped to the disc. Degenerate limits (`|depth| ≥ 0.995`, sun ~directly toward/away from the viewer) are handled explicitly rather than relying on the general formula to degrade gracefully: fully lit emits no scrim at all; fully dark emits a full-disc scrim capped at 0.55 alpha (a soft floor so surface features stay faintly legible, never pitch black). Verified: the terminator tracks Sun-body geometry (near-fixed screen path over a 6h `viewT` scrub, matching Earth's small orbital drift) while land geometry rotates ~90° under it in the same 6h — confirms the terminator does NOT spin with the body, as required.
- **Gate**: 9 new `_trajHemiClipRuns` assertions (tests/math.test.js) bring the suite to 508 total. Perf: 40 synthetic rotation frames at Earth's surfaced-disc LOD tier averaged ~8ms/frame render — well under the 33ms rotate-drag throttle, no observed regression from the added clip/terminator work.

**R6.4 — real textured globes, raster pipeline (574, 2026-07-11)**: replaces the vector surface layer with a RASTER globe (per-pixel equirect sampling) for the 8 bodies with real imagery (`PROG_TEXTURES`, 237-planet-textures.js — Earth/Moon/Mars/Mercury/Venus/Jupiter/Saturn/Uranus/Neptune minus Titan, which has none and keeps the vector/plain path). **Same spin/handedness convention as the vector layer above — not a re-derivation**: `_trajRasterGlobe` derives the exact algebraic INVERSE of `_trajProjectVec` (the one projection seam) plus the inverse of `_trajSpinRotate`, so a texture pixel and a vector coastline pixel land on the identical body-frame lat/lon for the identical screen pixel.
- **Inverse-projection derivation**: forward is `u=-(x·ca-y·sa)`, `v=(x·sa+y·ca)·ct-z·st`, `depth=(x·sa+y·ca)·st+z·ct` (ca/sa = cos/sin(az), ct/st = cos/sin(t), t=π/2−el) on a unit vector — a pure rotation, so `(u,v,depth)` is itself unit-length and `w=+sqrt(1−u²−v²)` recovers the dropped depth on the front hemisphere (r²=u²+v²≤1 test gates the disc edge). Un-tilting `(v,w)` by `−t` gives `ya=v·ct+w·st`, `z=−v·st+w·ct`; un-azimuthing `(xa=−u, ya)` by `−az` gives world `x=xa·ca+ya·sa`, `y=u·sa+ya·ca`; un-spinning `(x,y)` by `−spinAngle` (inverse of `_trajSpinRotate`) gives the body-frame `(bx,by,bz)` that `_trajLatLonUnit` would have produced — `lat=asin(bz)`, `lon=atan2(by,bx)` then index the equirect image (`fx=(lon+π)/2π·w`, `fy=(π/2−lat)/π·h`, bilinear, wrapping X at the seam / clamping Y at the poles).
- **Shading, baked in the same per-pixel loop** (`_trajRasterGlobe`): terminator = dot of the WORLD-frame surface normal `(x,y,z)` (pre-unspin — same as the vector terminator's convention, illumination is independent of the body's own rotation) with the shared `sunDir3` (the identical body→Sun vector `_trajTerminatorNightPath` uses, not re-derived), smoothstepped over a ±0.15 band, floored at 0.45 night brightness so geography stays legible; limb darkening multiplies by `0.75+0.25·w`. Both are approximations of the vector layer's existing gradient/scrim treatment, not new physics.
- **Texture store** (`_trajTextureFor`): lazy per-body singleton — decodes `PROG_TEXTURES[body]` via `Image`, draws to an offscreen ≤1024px sampling canvas, caches its `ImageData`. Decode is async; every call site checks `.ready` and falls back to the existing vector `_trajSurfacedDiscSVG` path until `onload` fires, which invalidates the raster dataURL cache and calls `_trajRequestRepaint()` (re-renders every mounted `.traj-wrap` at its current camera through the same `_trajWorldSVG` rebuild `_trajApplyCam` uses) so the swap to real imagery happens without waiting for the next user gesture.
- **Integration** (`_trajBodyDiscTiered`): at the surfaced-disc tier (`r ≥ _TRAJ_FEATURE_PX`, 14px — same threshold the vector layer already used to gate feature sampling), if a ready texture exists for the body, the vector base/features/terminator/limb stack is fully REPLACED (no double-draw) by an `<image>` element pointing at a cached raster `canvas.toDataURL('image/png')`, sized/positioned to the disc in world-layer coordinates. Canvas render size is `min(2·discPx, 512)` — SVG upscales beyond that, cheaper and visually indistinguishable at this LOD.
- **Cache/throttle**: `_trajRasteredDiscDataURL` keys on `(body, discPx bucketed to 8px, spin quantized to 0.01 rad, az/el quantized to 0.01 rad, sunDir3 quantized to 0.1 per axis)` and additionally throttles ANY re-raster of a given body to at most once per ~120ms, reusing the most recent dataURL in between — during a rotate-drag (which re-renders at the existing ~33ms cadence) this means several frames in a row can show a very slightly stale globe orientation; acceptable per the brief, same spirit as the existing rotate throttle.
- **Perf**: 512px raster measured ~9–13ms per call (typed ImageData in/out, no per-pixel canvas API calls) — under the 120ms re-raster budget and the panel stays interactive during drag since cached frames are reused between throttle windows.
- **Gate**: the inverse-projection math (`_trajRasterGlobe`'s coordinate transform) is exercised indirectly by the existing `_trajLatLonUnit`/`_trajSpinRotate`/`_trajProjectVec` assertions (§7m/§7o) — those three primitives are literally what the inverse re-derives algebraically, so no new vm-testable pure function was extracted; the raster loop itself is canvas/DOM-bound (`ImageData`, `Image`, `document.createElement('canvas')`) and was instead verified by browser pixel-sampling (known-geography color checks — Sahara/Pacific/Amazon — at a fixed camera) during manual verification, not the Node gate.

**R6.5 — apoapsis/periapsis markers + trajectory occlusion by opaque bodies (574, 2026-07-11)**: two presentation-layer additions, zero ΔV/physics consequence (rendering-only; `dvExpended` golden unchanged).

- **Apse markers** (`_trajApsePoints`, pure — samples `progOrbitSamplePoints` and picks the periapsis/apoapsis POINT by actual radius magnitude, not by blindly trusting `sample[0]`/`sample[N/2]`): every drawn orbit ring — heliocentric planet rings, moon rings, and mission orbit rings — gets a small filled-triangle "Pe"/"Ap" glyph plus a distance label at its true apsides, registered through the existing overlay label registry (`_trajRegisterLabel`, same LOD/collision system as every other ring label — fades and de-clutters with its parent ring). Distance format differs by ring class: heliocentric rings show **AU** (`_trajFmtApseDist(km,'au')`); Moon-class rings show **raw center-to-body km** (`'km'` mode — e.g. the Moon's own orbit around Earth reads Pe≈363,296 km / Ap≈405,504 km, matching the well-known lunar perigee/apogee); mission rings reuse the ring record's OWN `peri`/`apo` fields directly, which are already **altitude above the body's surface** (not center-distance) by construction (see the `rp = R + peri` convention above) — no separate computation needed there. Markers inherit the ring's own stroke color (no chromatic literal) and are individually skipped if their own point fails the occlusion test below (one apse can be visible while the other is hidden behind the body).
- **Trajectory occlusion by opaque bodies**: orbit rings AND physics-leg polylines (`_trajPolylineSVG`, used by every `_trajPhysLegRender` call site — MNODE vector-burn legs, converged interplanetary/local transfer legs) are now clipped where they pass behind an opaque body disc drawn in the SAME frame — a LEO ring's far side hides behind Earth's disc, which is the correct/desired reading (not a bug to avoid). Schematic (unconverged) Hohmann-arc legs and SOI escape/capture spurs are **out of scope** for this pass — the brief's literal wording covers rings and physics-leg polylines only; those two schematic-geometry emitters are noted here as a known gap, not silently addressed.
  - **Depth-sign convention (derived, not assumed — verified empirically in-browser before coding)**: the painter sort in `_trajWorldSVG` is `records.sort((a,b) => a.depth - b.depth)`, drawn ascending — so a LARGER `depth` (the camera-axis component from `_trajProj3`) is drawn LATER, i.e. NEARER the camera. Empirical check (top-down camera, `az=0, el=π/2`, where `depth` reduces to world `z` by construction): a body at the origin with `R=1000` has its near cap at `depth=+1000`; a synthetic point at `z=+1500` (beyond the near cap, toward the camera) tests as NOT occluded, and a point at `z=−1500` (behind the body along the same sightline) tests as occluded — confirming larger depth = nearer camera, matching the painter-sort direction. Printed in the verification transcript.
  - **Occlusion predicate** (`_trajPointOccluded`, pure): for an opaque body disc `{cx,cy,depth,R}` (render-unit center, real-km camera-axis depth, real-km physical radius) and a candidate point `(px,py,pdepth)`, the perpendicular screen offset in real km is `ρ = hypot(px−cx, py−cy) / zoom`. The body's NEAR cap (the point on its surface closest to the camera at that radial offset) sits at `depth + sqrt(R²−ρ²)`. The point is occluded iff `ρ < R` (inside the disc's silhouette) AND `pdepth < nearCapDepth` — i.e. it's behind the visible sphere surface along that sightline. A point far behind the body (beyond the far cap) is still correctly occluded — the sphere is opaque, so anything directly behind it along the sightline is hidden regardless of how far back it is.
  - **Depth threading**: every ring/leg point's depth is computed in the SAME real-km, camera-relative convention as the painter sort — `centerDepth + localOffsetDepth`, where `centerDepth` is the ring/leg's own primary body's `toRender(...).depth` (threaded as `oDepth` through `_trajBodyFrameContent` → `_trajRingSVG`'s `opts.centerDepth` and → `_trajPhysLegRender`'s `anchorOf` closures, which now return `{x,y,depth}` instead of `{x,y}`), and `localOffsetDepth` is the UNSCALED `_trajProj3(...).depth` of the sample's local offset vector (exact because the projection is a pure rotation — linear, so depth scales/adds exactly, no approximation). Call sites that haven't threaded `oDepth` default to `Infinity` ("never occluded") — backward-compatible, not a silent correctness bug.
  - **Order-independence**: which bodies occlude a given frame's content is decided by a PRE-PASS at the top of `_trajWorldSVG` that collects every body (Sun excluded — its glyph is a fixed schematic marker, never a true-scale rendered disc) whose apparent screen radius clears the disc-tier threshold (`trueRpx ≥ _TRAJ_FEATURE_PX`, the same 14px threshold the surfaced-disc renderer already uses to decide "worth sampling features for") into a single `_trajOccludeBodies` list BEFORE any ring/leg content is emitted — so occlusion tests are independent of body iteration order (a Mars-frame ring occluded by a body drawn later in `Object.keys(PROG_HELIO_R)` iteration order still sees it).
  - **Run-splitting**: `_trajOcclusionSplitRuns` drops occluded samples and breaks the polyline at the crossing (same "drop + break" pattern as `_trajHemiClipRuns`/`viewClampUnits` elsewhere in this file) — exact limb interpolation is deliberately NOT done at ring/leg sample density (96/64 samples respectively), noted here per the brief's explicit allowance. A closed ring therefore is NOT wraparound-stitched across its own start/end seam when occlusion splits it (unlike `_trajHemiClipRuns`, which does stitch coastline polygons) — a visually continuous visible arc that happens to straddle sample index 0 can render as two adjacent fragments instead of one; cosmetically minor, not a correctness bug, and cheap to fix later if it reads as a seam in practice.
- **Verification** (browser, `devSeedApolloMission`): Earth wKm=20000 → LEO 185×185 ring shows Pe/Ap both "185 km"; Earth wKm=8000/heliocentric wKm=5e8 → Moon ring Pe "363,296 km" / Ap "405,504 km", Earth's own heliocentric ring Pe "0.983 AU" / Ap "1.017 AU" (matches the brief's expected values exactly). Occlusion sample count at Earth wKm=20000, el=0.5, az=0.3 on the real LEO ring geometry (96+1 samples, i=28.5°): 41 occluded / 56 visible, split into 2 runs — confirms the far side is hidden and the ring breaks rather than staying one closed loop. `dvExpended` on the untouched seed: 13,010 (golden unchanged, confirming this is a rendering-only change). Gate: 519 total assertions (11 new — `_trajApsePoints` radius/point correctness, `_trajPointOccluded` depth-sign cases, `_trajOcclusionSplitRuns` drop/break behavior).

## 7n. Launch realism bundle — site lon, site marker, mock ascent, launch-time→RAAN R6.3 (220/360/570/574, 2026-07-10)

Closes critique 49's "real missions pin RAAN at launch time" gap: an orbit's Ω is now something the app can DERIVE from where/when you launch, not just something you type in directly (§7j still owns the direct-authoring path — this phase feeds that same `lan_deg` field from a physically-motivated computation instead of a blank number box).

- **Sites gain longitude** (220-launch-sites.js): every `LAUNCH_SITES` entry already carried `lat`/`azMin`/`azMax`; this phase adds real `lon` (Cape Canaveral −80.6°, Vandenberg −120.6°, Baikonur 63.3°, Kourou −52.6°, Wallops −75.5°, etc.) and threads it through the Vehicles-page site object (`site:{lat,azMin,azMax,lon,name}`, 140-physics.js) so a saved vehicle's launch site is a full lat/lon point, not just a latitude for the Townsend rotational-velocity term. **Backward compatibility**: a vehicle/preset saved before this phase has no `lon` key; every consumer treats `lon == null` as "can't derive RAAN geometry, everything else (ΔV math, azMin/azMax) works exactly as before" — no throw, no forced re-save. `_fleetVehicleSpecFromLib`/`fleetSwapVehicle` (560) now copy `site` onto fleet entries so a mission's LAUNCH event can inherit it without re-picking.
- **Site marker + mock ascent path** (574): presentation layer only, same class as §7m's surface rendering — zero ΔV/physics consequence. `_trajLaunchSiteAndAscentSVG` finds the mission's first LAUNCH event with a resolvable site (`_missionLaunchSiteFor`, 570 — authored on the event, else inherited from its fleet vehicle) and draws (a) a small ring+label marker at the site's lat/lon rotated to its CURRENT position via the exact same `_trajLatLonUnit`/`_trajSpinRotate`/`_trajBodySpinAngle`/`_trajSurfacePoint` pipeline §7m's coastlines use — self-consistent with the rotating globe by construction, not a re-derivation — hemisphere-culled the same way (`depth < 0` skip); (b) a dashed quadratic-bezier "mock ascent" curve from the site's position AT LAUNCH TIME (`_trajBodySpinAngle('Earth', e.launchTime_s)`) to a nominal insertion point on the launch orbit's ring, `progOrbitPointAtE` (360) evaluated at eccentric anomaly `E ≈ mean_motion × 510s` (~8.5 min nominal ascent, **mean anomaly used as a stand-in for eccentric anomaly** — a real Kepler solve is unwarranted for a schematic curve explicitly labeled non-propagated in its `<title>`). Both endpoints and the marker share the disc's current `rPx`/`cx,cy`/spin so the whole picture stays coherent as `viewT` scrubs; LOD-gated with the same `missionOrbitRing` window as mission rings, only drawn once the disc has reached the surfaced-disc LOD tier (`trueRpx >= _TRAJ_SURFACE_PX`, same threshold as coastlines).
- **Launch-time → RAAN authoring** (360's pure helpers + 570's LAUNCH card): three framework-free functions, gate-tested directly —
  - `progLaunchAzimuthDeg(latDeg, incDeg)`: spherical-trig required heading, `sin(az) = cos(i)/cos(lat)`. Returns both roots (`azNE` — ascending-node/NE departure, `azSE = 180 − azNE` — descending-node/SE departure); `{unreachable:true}` when `inc < |lat|` (no azimuth reaches an inclination below the launch latitude without a plane-change dogleg).
  - `progLaunchRaanFor(siteLatDeg, siteLonDeg, incDeg, tLaunchSec, spinFn)`: `Ω = λ_inertial − asin(min(1, tan(lat)/tan(i)))` where `λ_inertial = site.lon + spinFn(tLaunchSec)` is the site's INERTIAL (non-rotating-frame) longitude at the instant of launch — `spinFn` is injected (570 passes `_trajBodySpinAngle('Earth', ·)` when 574 has loaded) so the 360 module itself has no dependency on the renderer. Retrograde (`i > 90°`) mirrors the offset (`180° − dOmega`). `{unreachable:true}` under the same `inc < |lat|` condition as the azimuth function (same physical constraint, independently derived).
  - `progLaunchNextWindowS(currentRaanDeg, targetRaanDeg, siderealPeriodS)`: `Δt = ((Ω_target − Ω_now) mod 360°) / ω_earth`, wrapped forward into `[0, period)` — never negative, never a same-value NaN.
  - **Self-consistency, not calibration**: all three reuse `_trajBodySpinAngle`'s Earth constant (`PROG_EARTH_SIDEREAL_S = 86164.1`, duplicated in 360 with a comment pinning it to 574's value) and its **uncalibrated prime-meridian epoch** (critique 55) — the RAAN this produces is internally coherent with everything else drawn (the rotating globe, the site marker, the ascent curve) but is NOT tied to a real UTC launch-window clock. A "site.lon = −80.6°" launch at `t=0` does not correspond to any specific real-world day/time; it is a self-consistent in-app convention, same caveat class as the rest of §7m.
  - **LAUNCH card wiring** (570): a Launch Site picker (built-in sites with `lon != null`, or "no site"), a Launch Time (seconds-from-epoch) field, and the LAN field are threaded together — editing site/launch-time live-recomputes LAN (`missionLaunchGeoUpdate`) and annotates it "(from launch time)"; typing directly into LAN with no launch time authored is a plain manual override (`_lanFromLaunchTime` cleared); clearing the launch-time field frees LAN back to manual. A caption line shows the required azimuth pair and, once a LAN value exists (authored or derived), a "window in ~Xh Ym" readout (`progLaunchNextWindowS` against the RAAN launching right now (`t=0`) would produce) — same idea as a real launch-window countdown, computed against the app's internal clock rather than UTC.
  - **What this does NOT do**: it does not enforce the derived RAAN through a wait/COAST event — authoring a launch time computes what plane you'd reach, but nothing currently blocks launching at a time whose derived RAAN conflicts with a separately-authored one (the LAN field is simply overwritten last-write-wins, same as any other authored field). Reaching a plane at a SPECIFIC calendar time via an automatic launch-window solve remains the critique 49(c) gap, now narrower (the geometry is derivable; the scheduling isn't).
- **Gate** (`tests/math.test.js`, 12 new assertions, 2026-07-10): azimuth at `i = lat` is exactly 90° (due east); KSC (28.5°N) → 51.6° (ISS inclination) pins to ≈44.98° NE / ≈135.02° SE; `i < lat` flags `unreachable` for both azimuth and RAAN rather than emitting NaN/garbage; RAAN at KSC/28.5°/t=0 and KSC/51.6°/t=0 pin to their computed values; a 2h launch-time offset shifts RAAN by exactly the corresponding Earth-spin delta (self-consistency check, not an independent physical reference); next-window wraps forward through 360° and is ~0 when already under the target plane.

## 7o. World-view handedness fix — screen-y mirror bug R6.3' (574, 2026-07-11)

**Convention, stated once so this never regresses silently**: every world-frame vector in this program (heliocentric/planetocentric position from `progBodyEphemState`/`physElementsToState`, and the surface lat/lon → unit-sphere mapping `_trajLatLonUnit`) uses the standard right-handed ecliptic frame — `+z` = ecliptic north, reference direction `+x`, and angles increase **counterclockwise** as seen from `+z` looking down (i.e. `+x → +y` is the positive-angle sense: prograde orbital motion and increasing east longitude both go this way). This is a math convention (x-right, y-UP).

**The bug**: `_trajProjectVec` (130) — the ONE seam every world-space consumer routes through (surface points via `_trajSurfacePoint`, orbit rings, the maneuver gizmo, hover rails, ring-direction tangents, the site marker/mock-ascent pipeline) — mapped world `(x,y)` straight to screen `(u,v)` with `v = y` at the default top-down camera (`el = π/2, az = 0`). SVG screen space is **y-DOWN**. Plotting world-`y` directly onto a y-down screen is a reflection across the x-axis, not a rotation: it flips chirality. Effect was system-wide because it's one shared seam:
  - **Geography mirrored**: Australia rendered LEFT of Asia (correct CCW-from-north order is Europe → Asia → Australia → Pacific → Americas; the bug reversed it).
  - **Orbital motion mirrored**: real prograde motion (CCW from ecliptic north) rendered as clockwise on screen.
  - Everything downstream (site marker/Florida placement, gizmo prograde-handle direction, ring direction-of-motion fade, hover-ball tracking) inherited the same mirror because none of them duplicate the y-mapping — they all call `_trajProj3`/`_trajSurfacePoint`, which call `_trajProjectVec`.

**The fix (R6.3b, corrected same day)**: a det=−1 reflection is needed, and there are two candidates — negate screen-y or negate screen-x — which differ from each other by a 180° screen rotation. The FIRST attempt negated screen-y (`v = −(ya·ct − z·st)`); that repairs chirality but flips the tilt term's sign with it, so at tilted views (`el < π/2`) the NORTH pole projected toward the BOTTOM of the screen — the user immediately read the world as "flipped 180°". The correct reflection negates screen-**x**: `u = −xa`, `v = ya·ct − z·st` (original v restored). Chirality is fixed (east longitudes/prograde motion read CCW at the top-down view) AND north stays up: world `+z` contributes `v = −z·st`, negative = up-screen in SVG's y-down space. `depth` is untouched. Because every consumer is downstream of this one function, the fix is global and self-consistent by construction; no other file needed a compensating change.

**Gate impact**: the R2 projection-identity goldens (§ R2 block, `tests/math.test.js`) are pinned to `_trajProjectVec`'s literal output and were re-derived for the final mapping (`el=90`: u=−x, v=+y; `el=0` edge-on: v=−z i.e. north up-screen; `az=90`: +x → v=+1). Pure-angle goldens that don't touch the projection function (`progLaunchRaanFor` §7n, `progOrbitSamplePoints`'s radius/closure checks, hemisphere-clip depth tests) are untouched — they operate on world-frame vectors or camera-depth, never on the flipped screen axis.

**Not touched**: `_trajWorldToScreen` (622) — the render-space → overlay-px affine map used by labels/markers — has no independent y-mapping of its own; it linearly scales/translates whatever `_trajProj3` already produced, so it inherits the fix automatically and needed no edit.

## 7p. Launch-to-destination planner — pure math, phase 1 (415, R7, 2026-07-11)

Given a launch body, a destination, and an epoch, computes the IDEAL parking orbit (inclination, LAN, altitude) that sets up the lowest-ΔV departure toward that destination, plus the launch azimuth and departure C3. Pure functions only (`415-launch-planner.js`), no UI wiring yet (that is a separate later step) — this phase is the math core plus its gate tests.

- **3-D Lambert (`progLambert3D`)**: `progLambert2D` (410) is explicitly 2-D — it projects heliocentric positions to the ecliptic (z dropped) because a genuine v∞ VECTOR (needed for declination-of-launch-asymptote math) can't come out of a planar solve. `progLambert3D` ports the identical universal-variable algorithm (Curtis §5.3, bisection on ψ — same algorithm 410 already cites) to 3 dimensions: every step is dimension-generic (dot products, vector scale/add via 385's `physV3`/`physSub`/`physDot`/`physCross`/`physMag` helpers) EXCEPT the prograde/retrograde disambiguation, which mirrors 410's existing convention exactly — sign of the z-component of `r1 × r2` stands in for "which way is prograde," valid because every body in `PROG_BODY_ELEMENTS` has small ecliptic inclination (Mars 1.85°, Venus 3.39° — same basis as critique 42).
- **`progDepartVinf(fromBody, destBody, tDepartJD, tArrJD)`**: positions/velocities via `progBodyEphemState` (360, the ONE position source) at the two dates; `vInf = Lambert departure velocity − fromBody's heliocentric velocity at departure`; `c3 = |vInf|²`.
- **`progOptimalDeparture(fromBody, destBody, epochJD, opts)`**: scans a (departure day × TOF) grid for the minimum-C3 solution — minimizing C3 for a fixed parking altitude is equivalent to minimizing the departure burn, so "lowest ΔV" and "lowest C3" are the same optimization here. Per-destination default windows (`_LP_DEFAULT_WINDOWS`, 415) are hand-picked from rough synodic periods (Mars ~780 d, Venus ~584 d) and typical Hohmann-class TOF ranges — NOT derived from a synodic-period formula in code, same "hand-tuned constant" category as critiques 7/24/51/52/53. An explicit `{tDepartJD, tArrJD}` pair in `opts` skips the scan entirely. Measured (Mars, epoch 2026-07-09, default window, ~2,425 grid evaluations): **99 ms**, optimum at TOF ≈ 294 d, C3 ≈ 9.15 km²/s², v∞ ≈ 3.025 km/s.
- **`progIdealParkingOrbit({vInfVec, siteLatDeg, altKm})`**: DLA (declination of the launch asymptote) = `asin(vInf_z / |vInf|)`, computed in the ECLIPTIC frame — i.e. treating the ecliptic as a stand-in for the launch body's equatorial plane, the SAME no-axial-tilt approximation already documented for the surface renderer (critiques 54/55; Earth's real ecliptic-vs-equatorial obliquity is 23.4°). Ideal inclination = `max(|DLA|, |siteLat|)`; when `|DLA| < |siteLat|` the site physically cannot reach `inc = |DLA|` without a dogleg, so inc is pinned to `|siteLat|` and `planePenalty = |siteLat| − |DLA|` is reported (with an explanatory `note`). LAN is chosen so the parking plane's normal is perpendicular to vInf: `Ω = α − asin(tan(DLA)/tan(inc))` where `α = atan2(vInf_y, vInf_x)` is the asymptote's right ascension — derived directly from the plane-normal dot-product condition `n(inc,Ω)·vInf = 0` with `n = [sin(Ω)sin(i), −cos(Ω)sin(i), cos(i)]` (the same normal convention `progOrbitSamplePoints`'s rotation matrix implies), verified to residual ~1e-16 in the gate (not just "close," exact to floating-point). The supplementary root (`Ω − 180° − asin(...)`, same plane's descending node) is not returned separately — a plane has one normal up to sign, so it's the same physical orbit. `inc ≈ 0` (equatorial/ecliptic-plane parking) is a degenerate case where the ascending node is undefined; LAN is pinned to 0 with a note rather than propagating a 0/0. Azimuth is `progLaunchAzimuthDeg(siteLatDeg, inc_deg)` (360, unmodified, direct reuse).
- **`progPlanLaunchToDestination({fromBody, destBody, epochJD, siteLatDeg, altKm, tDepartJD?, tArrJD?})`**: orchestrates the above. Three destination shapes: (a) a planet name string → full scan/Lambert/DLA path; (b) an Earth-orbit target spec `{inc, lan, alt}` → short-circuits to "ideal parking = coplanar with the target," `c3`/`vInf` = 0 (no departure hyperbola for a same-body destination); (c) `'Moon'` → **phase 2 (2026-07-14, user feedback item 1)**: routed through the GEOCENTRIC cislunar plane math instead of the heliocentric Lambert scan (a lunar transfer isn't a heliocentric departure at all, so `progOptimalDeparture` never applied) — see `progMoonPlaneAt` below. `dvDepart` reuses the existing `progDvTLI(altKm)` (360) so the readout matches what the mission's actual TLI burn costs; `c3`/`vInfMag`/`dla_deg`/`tArrJD` stay `null` (no departure hyperbola in a patched-conic geocentric transfer).
- **`progMoonPlaneAt(epochJD, t_s)`** (415, phase 2): the Moon's INSTANTANEOUS orbital-plane inclination/LAN in the program's ecliptic reference frame, from the orbit-normal vector `h = r × v` of `progBodyLocalEphemState('Moon', t_s)` (the Earth-centered, parent-relative state — before Earth's own heliocentric position is added back in by `progBodyEphemState`). `inc = acos(h_z/|h|)`, `LAN = atan2(h_x, −h_y)` (node vector `N = k × h`) — the standard orbital-elements-from-state-vector identity, same family as `physStateToElements` (385) but kept local to 415 since it only needs the two angles, not a full element set. `progPlanLaunchToDestination`'s Moon branch treats "ideal parking" as coplanar with this plane (minimizes the TLI→LOI plane-change cost — a real free-return trade exists but isn't modeled here, see critique 58 below).
- **`progResolvePlaneTarget(target, epochJD, t_s, siteLatDeg)`** (415, user feedback item 2 — "launch into the plane of X"): generic resolver behind the launch card's "Match plane" picker. `target === 'Moon'` → `progMoonPlaneAt`; a catalog reference-orbit object `{inc, lan, name}` → its own inc/lan pass through unchanged. Returns `{inc_deg, lan_deg, source, unreachable, penalty_deg}` — `unreachable`/`penalty_deg` are computed (`|siteLat| > inc`) but never clamp the values; the UI surfaces the warning instead (same "everything user-overridable, nothing silently fixed up" philosophy as R7 phase 1).
- **Gate** (`tests/math.test.js`, 2026-07-11, 36 assertions; +8 phase-2 assertions 2026-07-14): DLA exact for in-plane and tilted vInf; `inc = max(|DLA|, lat)` pinned at two hand-picked cases (23°/28.5° → 28.5°, penalty 5.5°; 40°/28.5° → 40°, penalty 0); the plane-contains-vInf invariant checked across 5 vInf directions × 4 site latitudes (20 cases, residual < 1e-6); azimuth ties to `progLaunchAzimuthDeg` directly; a real Mars `progOptimalDeparture` run asserts C3 in a loose 4–30 km²/s² sanity band and TOF in 150–350 d (not a pinned magic number — printed, not asserted exactly) plus its own plane·vInf residual; Earth-orbit short-circuit. Phase 2: Moon plan returns real numeric inc/lan (not deferred) with inc in the plausible [18°,29°] lunar-plane-oscillation band and `dvDepart` matching `progDvTLI`; `progMoonPlaneAt` inc/lan range sanity; `progResolvePlaneTarget` Moon-target agreement with `progMoonPlaneAt` directly, an unreachable-flagged high-latitude case, and a pass-through catalog-object case.

## 7q. MISSION_MODEL_V2 Phase 1 — shadow state (566, 2026-07-14)

De-risking pass for the physics-primary re-architecture (see `MISSION_MODEL_V2.md`). `missionRecompute`'s tail (570), immediately after `physRebuildMissionTrajectories` (565), calls `v2BuildShadow(m)` — a `typeof`-guarded hook into a new module that builds a **VehicleState timeline per owner key** (the SAME per-stage keys `tagOwners` already assigns) alongside V1's accounting replay. Nothing reads it except the reconciliation tooling below; zero user-visible change; the side-table (`_v2StateByMission`, keyed by `missionId`) is never persisted on `m` (§8 invariant — verified by a byte-identical `_buildSessionObject()` blob with the shadow build on vs. off, modulo the two pre-existing `savedAt` timestamps).

- **PROMOTE, don't recompute**: `v2BuildShadow` walks `m._expanded` (the exact replayed log, clones included) and stitches EXISTING artifacts — `_physTrajByMission` legs (`leg.burnState`, `leg.dvVec`, `leg.initState`, `leg.center`) and V1's own post-event vehicle snapshots — into `V2Anchor`s. It re-propagates nothing; `v2StateAt(missionId, ownerKey, t)` only calls `physPropagateSegment` for a genuinely un-legged gap, and reuses `physLegStateAt`'s machinery inside a leg's span.
- **Per-event semantics** (MISSION_MODEL_V2.md §10.3): LAUNCH/DEPLOY anchor a state reconstructed via `physElementsToState` on the vehicle's parking orbit (mean-motion phase 0, same convention the leg builders use); solved MNODE/legacy MANEUVER anchor from `leg.burnState`+`leg.dvVec` directly; **manual MNODE** legs (`leg.kind === 'mnode'`) carry `dvVec` + a POST-burn `initState` but no `burnState` field — the pre-burn state is derived by mechanically un-applying the SAME recorded `dvVec` (`v_pre = v_post − dv`), not re-derived from a different basis; composition events (SEPARATE/DOCK/EXPEND/transfers/RENDEZVOUS) carry r,v through unchanged from the owner's last anchor (trajectory-neutral, D5); REENTER/RECOVER close the timeline with a `kind:'terminal'` anchor.
- **A genuine Phase-1 gap, surfaced not hidden**: a transit-corridor's EXITING edge (`leg.kind === 'arrival'`, e.g. the TLC→LLO/LOI edge) carries no propagation of its own in 565 — the injection leg already flew the whole trajectory — yet V1 still attributes a real Δv to that log entry via `progNmComputeEdgeDv`. Phase 1 has no leg to promote that Δv from, so the anchor is recorded with `dvApplied:null` + an explanatory `note`, and `v2DeriveBudget`/`v2Reconcile` surface it as an **unaccounted row** (`v2_dv:null`, `withinMargin:false`) rather than silently dropping it from the totals. This is the reason `v2Reconcile`'s TOTALS row does not close on the Apollo seed (see below) — it is an honest finding for Phase 2 (where LOI needs its own promotable leg), not a bug in the stitching.
- **Owner-level dv double-counting, fixed during S5 verification**: a single physical burn moves every owner key in the current (possibly docked, multi-stage) stack, so `v2BuildShadow` correctly gives every owner its own burn anchor — but `v2DeriveBudget` must count each authored burn's Δv exactly ONCE for the accounting readout (same discipline as `missionBudget`, which sums `m.log` once per entry). Fixed by deduping on `authIdx` before summing; an early build of this module summed the same TLI burn 5× across the Apollo CSM+LM stack's 5 owner keys before this fix.
- **Reconciliation** (D6: per-burn `max(1%, 5 m/s)`, totals 1%) — measured on `devSeedApolloMission({force:true})` + one manual gizmo burn (`_trajGizmoOpenPending`/`_trajGizmoCommit`, +500 m/s prograde @ MET 1800): all 3 per-burn rows within margin (TLI 3143 vs 3143 m/s, exact; manual burn 500 vs ~500.0 m/s, exact to float noise) except the LOI corridor-edge row above (flagged, not silently passed); totals do NOT close, by the two Phase-1-scope gaps documented above (LOI's un-promotable Δv, and LAUNCH's ascent Δv which is its own anchor `kind` and isn't compared dv-for-dv in this budget at all). Full numbers in `MISSION_MODEL_V2.md` §10.6.
- **Recompute cost**: `v2BuildShadow`'s own contribution measured at ~0 ms (within noise, Apollo seed, 20-iteration average) — it only reads existing artifacts. The Apollo-seed `missionRecompute` itself now measures ~4.2–4.5 ms average in this environment, both with and without the shadow build; that is a pre-existing cost (565's physics rebuild) that has drifted from `PERF_BASELINE.md`'s 2.05 ms baseline for reasons unrelated to this module — not attributable to Phase 1.

## 7r. MISSION_MODEL_V2 Phase 3 T2/T3 — reference-orbit binding + dwell/transit terminology (425/430/570/573, 2026-07-14)

**T2 — orbitRefId binding (cached-resolution semantics).** LAUNCH and DEPLOY log entries may carry an optional `orbitRefId` naming a `PROG_ORBIT_CATALOG` entry (425). The inline orbit fields (`orbit.alt_km/apo_km/inc_deg/body`, plus `launchOrbit` for LAUNCH) are the **cached resolution** of that ref, not an independent authority: at the top of `missionRecompute`'s replay loop, before the event's type dispatch consumes them, a bound event's inline fields are overwritten from `refOrbitResolve(orbitRefId)` (lan copied only when the ref authors one — builtins leave lan/argp free). Consequences, all by construction:
- A catalog edit (`refOrbitUpdate`) propagates to **every** binder on the next recompute — "hit the same orbit multiple times" (§4.3) works with N events bound to one ref.
- A missing/deleted/unseeded-`propagated` ref never throws: the event keeps its cached inline values and gets a transient `e._refNote` ("orbit ref unresolved — using cached values"), shown on the launch card.
- Hand-editing a bound inline field **detaches** (`orbitRefId = null`, `missionLaunchOrbitDetach`) — explicit and visible ("(custom)" on the card), mirroring the solved/manual burn convention. Detaching keeps the cached numbers; nothing moves.
- Persistence is free: `orbitRefId` lives on the log entry (autosave/session/.program serialize `m.log` wholesale); the user-tier catalog rides `orbitCatalogUser` (450/455, T1). No new physics — binding only changes *where the numbers come from*, so ΔV accounting is identical to hand-typing the same values.

**T3 — dwell/transit + edge-burn naming.** Planning rule (§4.4): **dwell = node, transit = edge.** Transit-type node-map entries (`orbit.type:'transit'`, e.g. TLC) are no longer drawn as stops; a dwell→transit→dwell maneuver chain in `m.log` (LEO→TLC, TLC→LLO) is merged **by the extraction only** into one dwell-to-dwell edge carrying named burn chips. Burn names come from the pure table `_nmBurnNames(fromBody, toBody)` (430): Earth→Moon `{TLI, LOI}`, Moon→Earth `{TEI, reentry}`, Earth→Mars `{TMI, MOI}`, Earth→Venus `{TVI, VOI}`, anything else `{injection, insertion}`. Chip ΔVs are the underlying entries' `dvRequired` — still sourced exclusively from `progNmComputeEdgeDv`/`dvOverride`; the merge is presentation, so the budget is unchanged (Apollo seed: ΔV expended 13,400 m/s before and after). Terminology helper `_missionManeuverNodeLabel(nid, dir)` (570): a transit node as a maneuver *destination* labels as the departure burn ("TLI (trans-lunar)"), as an *origin* as the corridor coast ("trans-lunar coast") — refreshed on every recompute (labels are display-only mirrors), so cached logs migrate phrasing for free. **The log is NOT re-authored**: the corridor legs remain real timeline/accounting entries (coast boiloff, physics legs, band view unchanged); only the planning view stops presenting the corridor as an orbit you park in.

**T4 — orbit inspector (direct manipulation, 5746, 2026-07-14).** Selecting a dwell orbit (LAUNCH/DEPLOY, or a solved maneuver's `toNode` arrival dwell — routed through the same `_missionNmSelectShared`/`_trajSelectEventFromView` hooks T2's binding UI already shares with the gizmo) opens a floating card in the trajectory view with peri/apo/inc/LAN slider+number pairs. Two independent binding systems exist and both are handled the same way (fork-on-builtin-edit, mutate-in-place-on-user-edit, edit-inline-when-unbound):
- **LAUNCH/DEPLOY** — bound via `orbitRefId` into the 425 global catalog (T2). Builtin ref → first edit calls `refOrbitAdd` to fork a `"<name> (copy)"` user entry and rebinds `orbitRefId`; user ref → `refOrbitUpdate` in place (moves every other binder, per T2); unbound → edits `e.orbit` inline.
- **Maneuver `toNode`** — bound via a node id into 430's two-tier node system (`PROG_NM_NODES` builtin / `PROG_ACTIVE_PROGRAM.nodeMapCustomNodes` user-owned), the SAME split T2 deliberately left maneuver targets out of (a second, conflicting indirection — §13 T2 as-built note). T4 treats that existing split as the binding model: a builtin node forks a `"<label> (copy)"` custom node (`_missionCreateCustomNode`) and rebinds `e.toNode`; a custom node is mutated in place (`n.orbit = {...}`).

**Commit path and D1 locality.** Committing (`_oiCommit`, numeric-change or slider-release) writes the orbit via the binding rule above, then calls `missionRecompute(m)` exactly once — D1's "one undo step per release" falls out for free from the existing `missionUndoCapture` dedupe-on-identical-snapshot discipline (§ per 570/575), not from anything new. Locality itself is not new machinery either: `progNmComputeEdgeDv(fromNode,toNode)` (430) and the T2 ref-resolution block both re-read their inputs FRESH every recompute — 565-physics-mission.js:986 calls `progNmComputeEdgeDv(e.fromNode, e.toNode)` on every rebuild, so an edited node/ref's new orbit is picked up by every edge referencing it and nothing else, which IS D1 ("changes never propagate past adjacent edges").

**LAUNCH-origin edges are a second, independent representation** — the Apollo seed's first maneuver has `fromNode:'leo'` (430's static preset, matched at authoring time) while the LAUNCH event's actual orbit is bound separately via `orbitRefId:'leo-185'` (425's catalog, T2's binding) — the two numerically agree in the seed but are NOT the same object. Editing the LAUNCH dwell therefore also needs the edge's `fromNode` re-pointed, or the "origin moved" half of D1 would silently no-op. `_oiCommitLaunch` handles this by computing `_missionNodeForLaunch(m)` (the existing launch-card-default-orbit matcher) BEFORE the edit, then re-running the same fork-or-mutate helper (`_oiResolveManeuverNodeId`) on any solved maneuver whose `fromNode` equals that pre-edit id — keeping the delivering edge's origin orbit synced to the launch dwell it visually represents. This is a T4-introduced synchronization, not a pre-existing invariant; documented here rather than silently assumed.

**Measured D1 evidence (Apollo seed, `devSeedApolloMission({force:true})`, browser console).** Selecting the LAUNCH dwell (bound `leo-185`, builtin) and committing peri+apo 185→300 km:
- **Auto-fork confirmed:** `e.orbitRefId` moved `'leo-185'` → a fresh user ref; `refOrbitCatalogList()` shows a new non-builtin entry `{name:'LEO 185 (copy)', body:'Earth'}` — the exact acceptance-criteria string.
- **Upstream edge re-solved (delivering edge, D1's primary rule):** the LEO→TLC edge's origin-sync rebound `fromNode` to a matching new custom node; `dvRequired`/`dv_actual` on that edge moved **3143 → 3148 m/s** (small, correct-direction shift for a 115 km altitude change on a vis-viva departure-burn calc that reads the origin orbit's altitude, 430:335).
- **Downstream real-physics ripple (legitimate per D1 — "re-solves only because its origin state moved", not a target change):** the TLC→LLO edge's `dvRequired` (the SCHEMATIC `progNmComputeEdgeDv` value) stayed exactly **822 m/s** — this edge's arrival calc is a fixed patched-conic lookup keyed to the corridor, independent of the bound `toNode`'s actual elements (see finding below) — but `dv_actual` (the S2/§7q REAL physics arrival burn, `physArrivalStateAt`-derived) moved **1028 → 512 m/s**, because the corridor's actual arrival state shifted from the new departure orbit. The LLO dwell's OWN orbit (peri/apo/inc/lan, the edge's fixed target) was untouched throughout.
- **Undo, one step:** a single `missionUndo()` call after the commit reverted BOTH `orbitRefId` (fork → `'leo-185'`) and the TLI edge's `dvRequired` (3148 → 3143) atomically — confirming the commit produced exactly one undo-stack entry.

**Finding — the transit-corridor's schematic arrival edge does not respond to the bound toNode's elements.** Editing the LLO dwell's own peri/apo/inc through the inspector correctly forks/mutates the 430 node (`"LLO (COPY)"` custom node created, `e.toNode` rebound) at the data layer, but the TLC→LLO edge's `dvRequired` stays pinned at 822 regardless — `progNmComputeEdgeDv`'s transit→orbit branch (`_nmDvPhysics`, 430) resolves this specific corridor arrival through a **fixed patched-conic table** (`{dv:822, note:'LOI to 100 km LLO', method:'patched-conic'}`) rather than generic vis-viva on the bound node's actual perigee/apogee/inclination — a PRE-EXISTING limitation of the schematic edge-cost model (same family as critique 58: the corridor's arrival cost is schematic, not flown), not a T4 defect.
