For the COMPUTED/LOD case, no visual re-draw was implemented in this pass (documented gap — critique 90 below): only the numeric readouts update live, since a real LOD-shaped spiral has no cheap closed-form re-shape the way the 4-rev schematic glyph does.

**What-if readouts.** `_ltgUpdateReadout` writes into two DOM mount points every drag frame: a floating readout div (`ltg-readout-{missionId}-{authIdx}`, present only if some future caller mounts one — currently unused, harmless no-op) and the event card's own `ltg-card-readout-{missionId}-{authIdx}` slot (570's `_missionLowThrustLogCardHTML`), which stays visible while the card is expanded during the drag (same DOM, two read paths, matching how 5745's own dv readout piggybacks on the already-open card). Fields: duration (days), Δv est. (m/s), prop used (kg, with a `DEPLETED` var(--warn) tag when `ltEstimateLeg`'s own `capped` flag trips), prop remaining (`(m0−mDry) − propUsed`, floored at 0 — never negative), and arrival date (`progMissionTimeToDate(e.metStart + liveDuration_s)`, epoch-aware per the s19 calendar work, UTC `YYYY-MM-DD HH:mm`).

**Release / cancel.** Mouseup with a changed duration authors `e.duration_s` and calls `missionRecompute(m)` (the ONE sanctioned mutation path — the gizmo itself never touches `_ltComputedByMission`, `e._ltState`, or any accounting field directly); the existing signature machinery (§7z, now including `metStart_s` — see the critique-86 closure below) picks up the change and flips any cached computed leg STALE on its own. Mouseup with an UNCHANGED duration (a click-and-release with no meaningful drag) or an Escape keypress both just re-render (cheap, no mutation) to restore the original DOM shape/handle position — no half-committed state is possible because nothing is written to `m.log` until release-with-a-real-delta.

**Target-orbit mode (`ltInverseEdelbaumDuration`, 568).** A "Target altitude…" number input + "Solve duration" button on the LOWTHRUST card. **This is the EXACT algebraic inverse of `ltApplyDvToCircularAlt`, not the naive `|v_circ(r0)−v_circ(r1)|` planar-Edelbaum triangle** — a documented departure worth explaining precisely, because the two are NOT the same number: `ltApplyDvToCircularAlt` treats a dv as a single tangential impulse delivered AT r0 and reports the resulting orbit's vis-viva SMA as the new "altitude" (`aNew = −μ/(v1²−2μ/r0)`), which is a genuinely different orbit than "an impulse that lands you in a circular orbit exactly at r1" unless r0=r1. Solving `aNew=r1` for `v1` gives the vis-viva equation directly: `v1 = √(2μ/r0 − μ/r1)` — using THIS `v1` (not `√(μ/r1)`) makes the round-trip `altitude → dv → duration → ltApplyDvToCircularAlt(dv) → altitude` **bit-exact** (gate-pinned, `LT E4: round-trip`, 1e-6 tolerance), which matters because the drag gizmo's own live schematic redraw (`_ltgRepaintSchematic`) and this affordance must agree on what "the resulting altitude" means — using the naive planar form here would have made the target-altitude button and the duration-drag handle's live redraw silently disagree about the same physical picture. Domain: `v1²≥0` requires `r1 ≥ r0/2` (a single tangential impulse at r0 cannot land you in a circular orbit at less than half r0 — returns `null`, not NaN, outside that domain). `law` is `'prograde'` for a raise, `'retrograde'` for a lower. Duration comes from `ltEdelbaumTofEst` on that dv (§7aa, unchanged) — returns `null` cleanly (no NaN/throw) when the dv exceeds the stage's tank capacity, at which point the UI shows `ltMaxAchievableAlt`'s honest max-achievable badge instead of silently clamping.

**Max-achievable altitude (`ltMaxAchievableAlt`, 568) — reachability honesty.** `dv_max` is the FULL-tank rocket-equation dv (`Isp·g₀·ln(m0/mDry)`, the exact relation E1/§7y pin), independently gate-cross-checked against `ltEstimateLeg`'s own capped-duration output for a deliberately excessive burn (two different entry points into the same physics, must agree — gate-pinned to 1e-6). The resulting altitude re-uses `ltApplyDvToCircularAlt` (the SAME est-orbit approximation the rest of the est. lane already commits to, §7z critique 75) rather than inventing a second orbit model for this one badge. **Honest gap, not a bug** (critique 89 below): for a wide-margin EP stage (e.g. a real xenon tank with `mDry/m0` well under 0.5), `dv_max` routinely EXCEEDS Earth's local escape velocity, driving `ltApplyDvToCircularAlt`'s vis-viva solve hyperbolic (`denom ≥ 0`) — its own non-physical guard then returns the ALTITUDE UNCHANGED rather than lying with a fabricated finite number, which for THIS caller reads misleadingly as "no altitude gain possible" when the true answer is "this stage can reach escape and beyond, circular-altitude framing breaks down." The gate deliberately exercises a tight-margin stage (`mDry/m0=0.9929`) to stay in the domain where the badge's number is meaningful; a real "can this stage escape" affordance is future work (E1/E3 already surface an escape-focused number via the launch-planner's own spiral-to-escape line, §7aa "spiral to escape ≈ v_circ(parking)" — critique 85).

**Critique 86 closure — `metStart_s` joins the signature.** `ltSignature` (568) gains an 8th field, `metStart: Math.round((f.metStart_s||0)/60)*60` — 60-second-bucket rounding (gate-pinned: a 12.3s jitter does NOT flip the signature, but any real upstream timeline edit — which moves MET by seconds-to-days, never sub-minute — does). 570's LOWTHRUST recompute case now threads `metClock` (this event's own start MET, captured before this event's duration advances the clock) into the `ltSignature` call as `metStart_s`. **This is a deliberate spurious-STALE trade, exactly as critique 86 predicted when it was filed**: every upstream timing edit that shifts a LOWTHRUST leg's `metStart` — even one that leaves the leg's own r/v/thrust/duration byte-identical — now flips any cached computed result STALE, because in a non-inertial N3 frame a time-shifted replay of identical geometry IS a genuinely different frame-relative picture (per-sample epochs feed the frame transform at render time). Cost: existing computed-leg cache entries (`_ltComputedByMission`, never session-persisted per D6, §7z) simply recompute their signature differently across this change — transient, not a data-loss concern, and consistent with how the cache already treats every session restore as "recompute from scratch."

**Critiques (new):**
88. **The inverse-Edelbaum solve is a single-tangential-impulse-at-r0 model, not a real spiral** — same honesty boundary `ltApplyDvToCircularAlt` itself already carries (§7z critique 75), inherited here because the inverse deliberately matches the forward function's own physics rather than the more "textbook" `v_circ(r0)` vs `v_circ(r1)` triangle. A real continuously-thrusting spiral's actual dv for the same altitude change differs from both by the same class of spiral-vs-impulsive loss §7y's own Edelbaum-vs-integrated gate table measures (~1-3%, more for larger raises).
89. **`ltMaxAchievableAlt`'s reported altitude silently reads as "no gain" (returns the unchanged starting altitude) when a stage's full-tank dv drives the est-orbit approximation hyperbolic**, rather than surfacing "this stage can reach escape" as a distinct, honest outcome — inherited directly from `ltApplyDvToCircularAlt`'s own non-lying-but-not-fully-informative guard (§7z critique 75's "leaves altitude unchanged rather than lie" contract was written for a single bad input, not for "this is actually a GOOD outcome the circular-altitude framing can't express"). A future pass should have the target-altitude UI detect this case (`dv_max` ≥ the local escape dv at r0) and show "can reach escape" instead of a badge implying zero altitude gain.
90. **The duration-drag handle's live visual redraw only covers the UN-COMPUTED schematic glyph** (`_ltgRepaintSchematic` rewrites the dashed log-spiral `<path>` in place) — **a COMPUTED/LOD-rendered leg's polylines and envelope washer do NOT re-shape during a drag**, only its numeric readouts (dv/prop/TOF/arrival/prop-remaining) update live. Re-deriving a live LOD shape from the est. lane alone would require inventing a fake multi-rev spiral geometry the est. lane has no basis for (§7z critique 75's whole point — the est. lane prices dv/prop exactly but says nothing about spiral SHAPE); the honest choice made here is "the computed lane's last real result stays on screen, visually unchanged, until Compute is re-run" rather than drawing a shape that would misrepresent what the drag actually changed.
91. **The schematic live-redraw's affine-recovery degenerates when the drag-start end radius sits (numerically) on the same ray as the start radius** (`wLen2 ≈ 0`, i.e. `r1_start ≈ r0` — a near-zero-dv leg at the moment a drag begins) — that one frame's redraw is skipped (the last-good shape stays on screen) rather than dividing by ~0. Vanishingly rare in practice (it requires starting a drag on a leg whose est. dv is already ~0 AND not moving the cursor at all that frame) and self-heals the instant the live duration moves off the degenerate point, but worth flagging as a documented edge rather than a silently "it just works" claim.

## 7ad. MISSION_MODEL_V2 §15 5b R1 — phase truth (567/570/572, 2026-07-15)

**Scope: R1 only — measurement + display of phase error between two co-orbital vehicles. NO new solving (R2's phase-matched arrival), NO phasing burns (R3), NO dock-link UI (R4) — all explicitly deferred per §15's 5b spec.** Closes critique 63/critique 14's "co-orbital ≠ co-located, phasing entirely ignored" gap at the measurement layer; R2/R3 close it at the planning layer.

**The problem this replaces.** Since 5a (critique 63), a RENDEZVOUS event and a co-orbital HUD both treated "same orbit" as "done" — a vehicle arriving at the NRHO's own propagated sample state (`refOrbitPropagatedStateAt`) succeeds regardless of where the OTHER vehicle actually is along that same loop. R1 makes that gap visible without attempting to close it: phase becomes a real, computed, displayed number.

**Definition — time-along-track offset, not inertial position diff.** For two vehicles sharing a propagated reference orbit (same `refId`), Δt_phase is the difference of each vehicle's **nearest-point time parameter** on the ref's one-period sampled loop (`refOrbitSamplePropagatedRaw`, N3's un-rebased raw source — each sample keeps its own true epoch), found by nearest-neighbor search in the **Earth-Moon ROTATING frame** (§7v/N2): the query point and every candidate sample are projected into rotating coordinates via `_refToRot` at their OWN epochs, and the winning sample's epoch becomes the vehicle's "along-track clock" reading. This is not optional — a rotating-frame-periodic orbit (the true 9:2 NRHO) does not close inertially (the frame turns ~86.5°/rev, §7v), so comparing raw inertial positions would measure frame-rotation drift, not orbital phase. Δt_phase itself is then the wrapped difference of the two vehicles' clock readings (`_phaseWrapDt`, into `[-P/2, P/2]`).

**Distance equivalent — two different framings for two different data situations, documented rather than unified:**
- **Propagated-ref path**: the EXACT chord between the two vehicles' real current position vectors (`physMag(physSub(rA, rB))`) — no linearization, since both real r's are already in hand.
- **Keplerian fallback path**: Δt_phase × local circular-approx speed (`v_circ = √(μ/a)`) — a first-order linearization, honestly weaker than the chord, used only because no real state vectors exist to chord between in the classical-orbit case (see below).

**Capture window — TIME-based, 300 s (5 min), not distance-based.** The NRHO is highly eccentric (perilune ~5,544 km vs apolune ~71,203 km from the Moon, §7v) — orbital speed varies enormously around one loop, so a fixed distance threshold would flag "captured" near apolune (slow, large phase errors read as small distances) and "not captured" near perilune (fast, tiny phase errors read as large distances). A time window means the same thing everywhere on an eccentric loop. 300 s is a docking-ops-scale window, comfortably inside the "minutes" band the spec names; it is a single named constant (`PHASE_CAPTURE_WINDOW_S`, 567) with no per-orbit tuning in R1.

**Keplerian fallback — pure and gate-tested, but NOT wired to live UI.** `phaseTruthKeplerian(bodyMu, a_km, M_A_deg, M_B_deg)` computes `dt = ΔM/n` (mean motion `n = √(μ/a³)`) for two vehicles on the same classical orbit. It exists as an honest, standalone primitive because today's `OrbitalState` (360's `progMakeOrbitalState`) carries no mean-anomaly/epoch field for classical orbits — every classical `orbitState` is `epoch:0`, always. There is nothing to diff for two "same orbit" classical vehicles beyond confirming they ARE the same orbit (peri/apo/inc match, the existing docking-tolerance check, critique 14) — phase is genuinely unknown data, not computable from what's stored. `phaseTruthBetween` (the dispatcher HUD/RENDEZVOUS actually call) tries the propagated-ref path first and returns `null` — never a fabricated mean-anomaly reading — when only the classical fallback's shape matches but its data doesn't exist. Wiring real anomaly/epoch tracking onto classical `orbitState` is out of R1's "measure with EXISTING data" scope.

**Maneuver-arrived vehicles have no captured phase — a documented assumption, not a bug.** A DEPLOY-placed vehicle carries a real captured `r`/`v` plus the MET it was captured at (`metAt`, newly stamped in `_missionApplyDeploy`, 570). A vehicle that arrived via a solved maneuver onto a propagated node (5a's `physSolveNrhoTransfer`) stamps `propagated`+`refId` but carries no `r`/`v` — there is no captured phase offset for it. `_phaseVehiclePoint` falls back to `refOrbitPropagatedStateAt(refId, metNow)` for this case, i.e. **assumes it sits exactly on the ref's own wrapped-clock position at the query epoch** — which is precisely today's pre-R1 co-orbital-success assumption, now made an explicit, documented fallback rather than an invisible one. The OTHER vehicle's real captured state (if any) is what shows a nonzero phase error against it.

**Where it's wired:**
- **HUD** (`_missionHudStripHTML`, 570-mission-panel.js): a vehicle chip whose `orbitState` is `propagated`+`refId`-matched against another chip in the SAME list (event-snapshot or live) gains a compact `Δφ 2h41m · 8,910 km` span (`_phaseChipText`, 567) — neutral chip styling (inherits the chips' own `--text-dim`, no `--warn`/`--danger`; this is information, not alarm). Empty string (no extra chip) when there's no co-orbital partner or the phase isn't measurable.
- **RENDEZVOUS readiness** (572-mission-checks.js): `e.phase` is stamped in the RENDEZVOUS replay branch (570-mission-replay.js), computed at the event's own MET (`metClock`, captured BEFORE the co-orbital merge overwrites the active vehicle's `orbitState` — the merge itself is unchanged, still R1-honest co-orbital success, no new solving). When `e.phase.capture === false`, `missionRunChecks` raises an **AMBER** finding (`rendezvous-phase-error`) — AMBER, not RED, because the rendezvous did not FAIL (the orbit match still succeeds exactly as 5a designed it to); it is a plan-quality warning that the arrival, while co-orbital, landed far from the target vehicle in time — precisely the gap R2/R3 exist to close. `e.phase === null` (not comparable) is silently skipped, never flagged.

**Critiques (new):**
92. **Nearest-point ambiguity at the sampler's resolution.** The rotating-frame nearest-neighbor search runs over a fixed default of 180 raw samples per period (`refOrbitSamplePropagatedRaw`'s default `nSamples`) — for the true 9:2 NRHO (period 566,987 s) that's ~3,150 s (~52 min) of sample spacing, so any single phase reading carries up to roughly half that spacing (~26 min) of quantization error before differencing. Differencing two independently-quantized readings can compound this to closer to a full sample-width in the worst case — comparable to or larger than the 300 s capture window itself. R1's gate test (tests/math.test.js "5b R1") documents this explicitly by using a tolerance of 3× the sample spacing rather than claiming second-level precision; a denser sample grid (or a parabolic/interpolated refinement around the nearest raw sample) would tighten this at some extra propagation cost, deferred as it's not needed for R1's "make the number visible" goal.
93. **Closure-tolerance floor.** The propagated-ref path's whole premise (comparing rotating-frame positions across different absolute epochs as if the loop were exactly periodic) inherits N2's rotating-frame closure tolerance (345.6 km for the true 9:2, §7v) — a phase reading taken many periods away from the catalog seed epoch accumulates whatever real non-periodicity that closure gap represents. Immaterial at the "compare two vehicles a few days apart" timescale R1 targets; would matter for a mission that loiters in NRHO for dozens of revs before rendezvousing.
94. **Keplerian fallback is dead code from the live UI's perspective** (documented above) — it is gate-tested and API-complete, but no `orbitState` produced anywhere in the current codebase carries the mean-anomaly/epoch data it needs, so `phaseTruthBetween` never actually reaches it in practice. Left in place (rather than deleted) as the honest target for the day classical `orbitState` gains real anomaly tracking, since deleting a correct, tested primitive to avoid "unreachable code" would be a worse trade than the comment cost of explaining why it's unreachable.

## 7ae. MISSION_MODEL_V2 §15 5b R2 — phase-matched arrival (565-physics-nrho.js, 570-mission-panel.js, 2026-07-15)

**Scope: R2 only — a SELECTION layer on the existing 5a machinery.** No new dynamics, no phasing burns (R3), no dock links (R4). `physSolveNrhoTransfer` (565) gains a `targetPhase` mode: when `ctx.targetVehicle` is an `orbitState`-shaped record for a vehicle ALREADY parked on the same NRHO ref (the DEPLOY-stamped shape — `propagated:true`, `refId`, real `r`, `metAt` — the same record 567's `_phaseVehiclePoint` already reads for R1), the solver evaluates the perilune-crossing lattice 5a already builds and picks by phase error to that vehicle instead of accepting the first converged crossing.

**Lattice extension.** 5a's default band (`tArrCands`, the 2.5–7.3 day TLI-class window) is extended by ±2 more perilune crossings beyond each end of the band (`loC - k·P`, `hiC + k·P` for k=1,2), giving a typical lattice of 6–8 candidate arrival epochs. Each candidate is solved INDEPENDENTLY via a fixed-arrival-epoch variant of 5a's own pipeline (`_nrhoSolveFixedTArr`, 565) — coarse θ-grid scan + 2-level refinement + 2-DOF/3-DOF Newton + MCC fallback, identical construction to the base solve, just pinned to one `tArr` instead of scanning the whole band for a single global best. This is a **deliberate near-duplication, not a shared refactor**: the proven default (no-`targetVehicle`) code path is left completely untouched so its behavior stays byte-identical to 5a (regression gate, below) — extracting a shared helper was judged a needlessly risky refactor for this task's scope.

**Phase prediction — idealized station-keeping, reusing R1's rotating-frame clock.** For a candidate arrival epoch `tArr`, the arriving vehicle's own along-track clock reading IS `tArr` (it's solved to land exactly on `refOrbitPropagatedStateAt(refId, tArr)`). The target vehicle's clock at that same epoch is predicted from its own captured state: `_phaseNearestT(refId, targetVehicle.r, targetVehicle.metAt)` gives the target's clock reading `tPhase` at its capture epoch (567's R1 machinery, reused verbatim — no new phase math), then advanced deterministically: `predictedClock(tArr) = tPhase + (tArr − metAt)`. This is the **idealized-station-keeping assumption**: the target's along-track clock is assumed to advance exactly 1:1 with elapsed mission time, with no modeling of real station-keeping burns, control-window drift, or the ref's own closure tolerance (§7v critique 93 applies here too, compounded — see critique 95 below). `phaseErr_s = _phaseWrapDt(tArr − predictedClock(tArr), P)` (567's own wrap helper, `[-P/2, P/2]`); `phaseErrKm` is the chord distance between the arrival target position (`targetAtAbs(tArr)`) and where the target vehicle would ACTUALLY be at that predicted clock reading (`refOrbitPropagatedStateAt(refId, predictedClock(tArr))`), not a linearized estimate — both positions are already in hand as real state vectors, same "exact chord over linearization" preference R1 documented (§7ad).

**Honest options, dv band, default selection.** Only CONVERGED candidates (miss < 2,000 km, 5a's own acceptance) enter the option list — an unconverged candidate is silently dropped, never shown as a fabricated "option". `dvTotal_ms` = the fixed departure Δv (`dv_kms`, unchanged across all candidates — it's an accounted budget input, never re-solved) **plus** that candidate's own MCC burn magnitude if one fired (varies per candidate — some land inside the 2/3-DOF Newton's window with no MCC, others need one). Options costing more than **+15%** over the cheapest converged candidate (`NRHO_PHASE_DV_BAND = 1.15`) are dropped before ranking — a documented, un-tuned band (no attempt made to find an "optimal" cutoff; 15% was chosen as a round number wide enough to keep a phase-favorable-but-slightly-costlier option visible without admitting an option that's meaningfully more expensive). The remaining options are sorted by ascending `|phaseErr_s|` and capped at 4 (`NRHO_PHASE_MAX_OPTIONS`). Default selection = the first (min `|phaseErr|` within the band); an authored pick (`e.arrivalOption`, the chosen option's `tof_s`, persisted on the log entry — a normal AUTHORED field, replay-deterministic like `e.dvOverride`) overrides the default when it matches one of the shown options' TOF within 60 s, threaded through as `ctx.selectedTof_s`.

**Fail-clean.** No `targetVehicle` on the same ref → the R2 branch is a hard no-op, returning the base 5a result completely unmodified (not even a phaseOptions:null-vs-undefined distinction the base path didn't already have) — this is the regression gate's exact claim. A `targetVehicle` present but the ref isn't rotating-frame-capable (567's `_refRotCapable`), or zero lattice candidates converge, appends a note to the existing 5a note field and returns the plain 5a result — never loops, never widens the 2,000 km acceptance window, never fabricates a phase number.

**UI** (570-mission-panel.js `_missionArrivalOptionsRowHTML`, wired into `_missionManeuverLogCardHTML`): an NRHO-bound MNODE card whose leg (`physMissionLeg`) carries `kind:'nrho'` and a non-empty `phaseOptions` array gains a compact "Arrival Options" radio-chip row — `"T+4.7d · 4.32 km/s · Δφ 41m"` per option (reusing 567's `_phaseFmtDt` formatter) plus an "auto (min Δφ)" chip. Picking a chip calls `missionSetArrivalOption`/`missionClearArrivalOption`, which set/clear `e.arrivalOption` and `missionRecompute` — the standard author-then-replay path, no special-casing. No `targetVehicle` at the ref → `leg.phaseOptions` is `null` → the row renders `''`, so a mission with no co-orbital target sees an unchanged card.

**Gate (tests/math.test.js "5b R2"):** (a) the exact 5a call from the existing "5a" test block, re-run with no `ctx.targetVehicle`, asserts `phaseOptions == null` — the regression proof that the branch is a no-op. (b) a synthetic target vehicle is planted on `nrho-nominal` with a KNOWN closed-form clock offset (captured at a ref state 50,000 s AHEAD of its own nominal epoch) — the idealized-station-keeping math predicts every candidate's `phaseErr_s ≈ −50,000 s` (mod period) regardless of which lattice point is evaluated, giving an independent closed-form value to check the solver's own answer against (tolerance 15,000 s, dominated by §7ad critique 92's sample-quantization noise compounding across two independent `_phaseNearestT` lookups). Also asserts: options sorted by ascending `|phaseErr|`, default's `|phaseErr|` ≤ the worst shown option's, every option honestly `converged:true`, every option carries a `phaseErrKm` (number or `null`, never `undefined`). (c) a pure re-derivation of the dv-band filter confirms a +50% option would be dropped by the documented 15% band. 804 → 814 assertions.

**Critiques (new):**
95. **Phase prediction compounds §7ad's sample-quantization noise across TWO independent lookups.** R1's single phase-truth measurement already carries up to roughly one loop-sample's worth of quantization noise (critique 92, ~26 min half-spacing for the true 9:2 NRHO). R2's prediction calls `_phaseNearestT` once for the target vehicle's own captured state — the SAME noise source, not doubled by R2 itself (the arrival candidate's clock is exact by construction, since it's solved to land at that literal epoch) — but the target's real-world drift from a genuine (non-idealized) station-keeping regime is NOT modeled at all, so the honest error bar on a predicted phase widens with how long ago the target vehicle was captured relative to the arrival epoch (a target deployed months before rendezvous carries an unknown, unbounded real drift no part of this model sees). Immaterial at the day-scale gap R2 targets today (same caveat as §7ad critique 93); would matter for a mission that stages a target vehicle in NRHO for many months before a phase-matched transfer.
96. **The 15% dv band is a round, un-derived number**, chosen for legibility rather than optimized against any cost-of-delay or mission-value tradeoff — a real mission designer might reasonably want a wider or narrower band, or a band expressed in absolute m/s rather than a percentage (a percentage band is generous at high total-dv missions and stingy at low ones). Left as a single named constant (`NRHO_PHASE_DV_BAND`, 565) rather than user-configurable, matching R2's "selection layer, not a new tunable subsystem" scope.
97. **Lattice coarseness is EXPECTED, not a defect — R3's entire reason to exist.** Even the best phase-matched lattice point still generally lands with a residual phase error up to roughly half a perilune period (~2.8 days for the true 9:2 NRHO) when the target's clock doesn't happen to align with one of the ~6–8 candidate crossings — R2 explicitly does NOT attempt to close this residual (no phasing burns, no free-TOF continuous search); §15's R3 (terminal two-impulse phasing burns) is the documented next step that absorbs exactly this gap.

## 7af. MISSION_MODEL_V2 §15 5b R3 — terminal phasing burns (567-phase-truth.js, 570-mission-cards.js, 570-mission-events.js, 2026-07-15)

**Scope: R3 only — the classic two-impulse phasing pair as authorable events. NO dock links (R4).** Closes the residual R2 documents (critique 97) and 5a's original co-orbital-only gap (critique 63) at the AUTHORING layer — R1 measures the error, R2 picks the best lattice point, R3 gives the user a burn pair that erases whatever is left.

**Construction.** Given the residual phase offset Δt_phase (R1's own measurement, `e.phase.dt_s`) and a user-chosen rev count N (1-5), the phasing orbit's period is offset by ΔP = Δt_phase / N so that after exactly N revs on the phasing orbit the vehicle has drifted Δt_phase relative to the reference orbit's own clock — closing the gap BY CONSTRUCTION, not by search (`physPhasingSolve`, 567). The reference semi-major axis is derived from the reference PERIOD via Kepler's third law (`aRef_km = cbrt(mu·P²/4π²)`) rather than from (peri+apo)/2 — the period is the one number this construction must hit exactly; peri/apo are secondary, approximate labels on a 3-body orbit (§7ad). The phasing semi-major axis follows the same formula at P+ΔP. Both burns are periapsis-tangent (vis-viva at the FIXED periapsis radius): `dv = |v_peri(a_phasing) − v_peri(a_ref)|`, m/s. The pair is symmetric — burn 1 (raise/lower the far apsis to retime the period) and burn 2, equal and opposite, N revs later (undoing it, landing back on the reference orbit exactly at the retimed clock reading). `waitTime_s = N·(P+ΔP)`.

**Two contexts, one shared formula.** `phasingPlanPropagated(refId, dtPhase_s, N, muBody)` reads the reference's periapsis radius straight off 425's catalog `peri` field (the SAME measured/approximate number R1/R2 already treat as ground truth for the NRHO — nothing new is measured here) and its period from `refOrbitResolve`. `phasingPlanKeplerian(orbitState, dtPhase_s, N)` derives periapsis radius and period from the classical orbitState's own `perigee`/`apogee` altitudes (360's convention) and the body's `mu`/`R` (`PROG_BODIES`). Both funnel into the same `physPhasingSolve(P_s, dtPhase_s, N, mu, rPeri_km)` — the textbook Keplerian construction and the NRHO approximation are the SAME formula, differing only in where `P_s`/`rPeri_km` come from.

**Honesty on the NRHO case.** `physPhasingSolve`'s vis-viva construction is exact for a true two-body Keplerian orbit; applied to the NRHO (a 3-body orbit) it is the same documented approximation the rest of §15's 5b work uses — "the honest est. lane, same D6 discipline as everything" (task spec). The burn is priced as if the reference orbit's periapsis passage behaved two-body-ly for the duration of one retimed revolution; no re-propagation through the real 3-body field is attempted for R3 (that would be a targeting/differential-correction problem, a different scope than "give the user an authorable estimate").

**"The target also moves" is not a bug to fix — it's what Δt_phase already means.** Δt_phase is defined (§7ad) as the DIFFERENCE between the two vehicles' along-track clock readings at a shared query epoch, not an absolute position. The phasing construction retimes the ARRIVING vehicle's clock by exactly Δt_phase over N revs; it never assumes the target vehicle is static. Under R2's own idealized-station-keeping assumption (§7ae, critique 95 — the target's clock advances exactly 1:1 with elapsed time and nothing else moves it), a burn pair that closes Δt_phase in the arriving vehicle's clock closes the SAME gap in the two vehicles' relative clock reading — by definition, since Δt_phase already encodes "how far apart their clocks are," not "where vehicle A alone will be."

**Feasibility guard.** `|ΔP| < PHASING_FEASIBLE_FRAC · P` (0.5, a documented round-number guard, same discipline as `NRHO_PHASE_DV_BAND`, §7ae critique 96) — beyond that the "phasing orbit" stops being a sane perturbation of the parked orbit (near-degenerate or inverted ellipse) and the option is silently dropped from the N=1..5 list rather than shown as a nonsense burn. `phasingOptionsFor(dtPhase_s, refId, orbitState, muBody)` builds the full N=1..5 list, keeping only feasible plans.

**Authoring — decision (as directed, documented rather than left implicit).** The pair is authored as TWO plain `BURN` log entries (`burnType:'CUSTOM'`, the existing manual-ΔV lane — the same path a user reaches via the burn-type dropdown), not a new `PHASING` event type: the two burns are ordinary budget-charged impulses with nothing rendezvous-specific about their EXECUTION (they go through `_missionApplyBurn`/`progDispatchEvent`, the sanctioned live-stage/prop machinery, unchanged); only the AUTHORING affordance is rendezvous-specific. `missionAddPhasingBurns(id, idx, N)` (570-mission-events.js) resolves the plan against the RENDEZVOUS event's own recorded `e.phase.dt_s` and its target vehicle's live `orbitState` (propagated+refId → `phasingPlanPropagated`; else `phasingPlanKeplerian`), then `m.log.splice(idx, 0, burn1, burn2)` inserts the pair immediately BEFORE the RENDEZVOUS entry and calls `missionRecompute(m)` ONCE — landing both burns as a single undo capture (575's capture keys off the recompute call, not the log-push count), per the task's "undo-able as one capture if feasible" ask.

**Timing semantics — decision (documented, not auto-wired).** The RENDEZVOUS event's own MET/duration is NOT auto-shifted by `waitTime_s`. Auto-shifting was judged too invasive for R3's scope: duration precedence (`durationOverride > physics leg TOF > launchWindow > Hohmann`, §7e) is a chain several other systems already reason about, and silently rewriting a downstream event's duration out from under an authored plan risks surprising a user who set that duration deliberately. Instead the wait time is surfaced HONESTLY in two places — the option chip itself ("N=2 · 2×3.1 m/s · wait 13.1d") and the authored burns' `note` field — so the user sees exactly how much MET the rendezvous needs to move and can adjust its duration override themselves through the existing field. This is the same "surface, don't silently mutate" posture 5a/R2 already take with solved-maneuver notes.

**UI.** The "+ PHASE" affordance lives WITH the amber finding it fixes, on the RENDEZVOUS log card itself (`_missionPhasingRowHTML`, 570-mission-cards.js), rendered only when `entry.matched && entry.phase.capture === false` — a clean rendezvous renders nothing extra, same "unchanged card" discipline as R2's arrival-options row. Chips read `"N=2 · 2×3.1 m/s · wait 13.1d"`; picking one calls `missionAddPhasingBurns` and re-renders. After the pair replays, the RENDEZVOUS's own `e.phase` (recomputed by 570-mission-replay.js's existing RENDEZVOUS branch, unchanged) reflects the vehicle's new arrival clock, and 572's amber finding clears automatically the next time `missionRunChecks` runs — no new finding-clearing logic needed, since the finding was always driven by `e.phase.capture` at replay time.

**Gate (tests/math.test.js "5b R3", 814 → 826 assertions):** ΔP round-trip identity `N·(P+ΔP) − N·P == Δt_phase` for N=1..5 (exact, tolerance 1e-6 — a construction, not a numerical solve); Keplerian dv hand-checked against an independently-written vis-viva re-derivation in the test itself (not a call into the module under test); feasibility boundary (a 3-period residual is infeasible for every N in 1..5; a 0.1-period residual is feasible for N=1); infeasible residuals drop out of `phasingOptionsFor`'s list entirely rather than appearing empty-but-present; NRHO perilune-approximation dv is positive/finite and scales ~linearly with ΔP for small ΔP (500s vs 1000s residual, ratio checked within ±20% of 2×, since vis-viva is smooth/near-linear close to `aRef`).

**Critiques (new):**
98. **The NRHO perilune-approximation prices the burn as a two-body maneuver on a 3-body orbit** (documented above) — the SAME family of honesty gap as §7ae/§7ad, now compounded one layer further: R1 measures phase with sample-quantization noise (critique 92), R2 selects an arrival lattice point with idealized station-keeping (critique 95), and R3 now prices a burn pair against that same NRHO's two-body-approximated vis-viva. None of these errors are large at the day-to-week timescale this feature targets, but they are NOT independent — a future high-fidelity pass (real 3-body targeting for phasing, differential-corrected rather than vis-viva-estimated) would need to re-derive R3's dv from scratch rather than composing corrections onto this estimate.
99. **Auto-shifting the RENDEZVOUS timing was explicitly deferred**, not solved — a user who authors a phasing pair still has to manually push the rendezvous's duration/MET out by the shown wait time (or accept that the rendezvous event fires "early" relative to the newly-retimed arrival, which the replay will simply reflect honestly via its own phase measurement, not silently paper over). A future R3.1 could offer a one-click "shift rendezvous by wait time" action; today it is a manual step the UI merely informs, not automates.
99b. **The retimed clock correction is applied as a stamped offset (`_phaseOffsetS`) consumed AFTER the geometric nearest-point search, not by re-propagating the vehicle** — `phaseErr_s`/`capture` on a later RENDEZVOUS correctly reflect the phasing pair's construction (verified: a 273,803s residual closes to 0 and the capture flag flips true after authoring N=2), but `phaseErrKm`/the distance-equivalent chord (`distKm`) is UNCHANGED by the correction, since it is computed from the two vehicles' real position vectors (§7ad) and no real propagation moved the arriving vehicle any closer — the CUSTOM burns never touch `r`/`v`. This is an honest, visible seam (the time-based capture flag and the distance readout can now disagree after a phasing pair is authored) rather than a silent one; a future pass could re-propagate the burn pair through 565's real segment integrator to make `distKm` consistent, which R3 does not attempt (out of scope — "est. lane", not a targeting rewrite). The offset is only credited when the closing burn's `result !== 'FAILED'` (an under-budgeted vehicle that can't actually deliver the second impulse does not get its clock retimed for a burn that didn't happen).
100. **The two-burn pair is authored as generic `BURN`/`CUSTOM` entries, not a first-class linked pair** — nothing on `burn1`/`burn2` records that they are a matched phasing pair once authored (beyond the human-readable `note` string). Deleting or reordering just ONE of the two burns (an existing, general log-editing capability) silently breaks the construction with no dedicated guard-rail; the existing 5a/R2 pattern of "the log is the source of truth, re-simulated on every change" makes this the same risk any two related hand-authored events already carry, not a new category of fragility R3 introduces — but a future pass could stamp a shared `phasingGroupId` for a "delete removes both" affordance.

## 7ag. MISSION_MODEL_V2 §15 5b R4 — multi-vehicle timing surface, 5b COMPLETE (5747-timeline-dock.js, 2026-07-15)

**Scope: R4 only — display + R1's phase check, no new event kinds, no scheduling solver (per §15's bounded spec).** This closes 5b: R1 (measurement) → R2 (phase-matched arrival selection) → R3 (terminal phasing burns) → R4 (the dock surfaces all three honestly on the SAME timeline the user already reads).

**What R4 adds.** The timeline dock (`_ttdDockHTML`) already renders one horizontal lane per owner (LV upper stage, spacecraft stage) on a literal-MET x-axis shared with the scrub track (`_ttdLaneModel`). R4 adds a thin vertical link between two lanes at a RENDEZVOUS or DOCK event's MET — the one thing the existing per-lane view cannot show on its own: THAT two owners' timelines are meant to meet at a point, and whether they actually did.

**Lane resolution — reuses the band model's own owner-tagging, not a parallel scheme.** `_ttdDockLinksModel` (new) calls `_missionBandModel(m)` directly (same call `_ttdLaneModel` already makes) and, for each RENDEZVOUS/DOCK log entry, resolves the ACTIVE and TARGET lane by scanning `band.lanes[*].points` for a point whose `{index, vehicleId}` matches `{thisEventIndex, e.vehicleId}` / `{thisEventIndex, e.targetVehId}` (RENDEZVOUS) or `{e.aVehId, e.tVehId}` (DOCK) — the exact fields 570-mission-replay.js's RENDEZVOUS/DOCK branches already stamp on the log entry during replay. No new tagging scheme: a band "point" is already one-owner-per-column-per-vehicle, so this is a lookup against data the band model produces for free.

**Styling states (verified against the Gateway seed, see below):** `var(--border-bright)` when `e.phase` is absent (DOCK, or a RENDEZVOUS whose vehicles aren't phase-comparable) or `e.phase.capture === true`; `var(--warn,var(--accent2))` when `e.phase.capture === false` (the amber "out of phase" reading R1 defines); `var(--danger)` only when the event outright failed (`e.matched === false` for RENDEZVOUS, `e.result === 'FAILED'` for DOCK) — three distinct, mutually exclusive states, no chromatic literals (theming invariant honored: all three are seed-derived vars).

**Geometry.** A new `.ttd-link-overlay` layer is inset to align exactly with the lane-TRACK column (`left: calc(8.5em + 6px)` — past the fixed-width label, matching every row), so a link's `left%` uses the SAME `met/maxMet` formula as every seg/burn marker in the dock — no separate coordinate system to keep in sync. Vertical position is pixel math over the row geometry the lane rows already render at (`laneH + 4px row height + 2px flex gap`), since lane order is a stable array (no reordering happens at render time, only a prefix slice for the collapse cap).

**Collapsed-mode dangling-end guard.** The collapsed dock caps lanes to `_TTD_MAX_COLLAPSED_LANES` (3) via `model.lanes.slice(0, capIdx)` — a prefix slice, so a lane's index in the full list equals its index in the shown list whenever it's under the cap. `_ttdLinksOverlayHTML` drops any link where `laneA >= capIdx || laneB >= capIdx` before emitting markup — verified on the Gateway seed (6 owner lanes; the RENDEZVOUS links lane 0 ↔ lane 5): 0 `.ttd-link` elements render collapsed (cap=3), 1 renders correctly once expanded (cap=6) — no half-drawn line, no console error either way.

**Click/tooltip.** Reuses `_trajSelectEventFromView(id, authIdx)` — the same hotspot path every seg/burn marker in this dock already uses — via `onclick`, `authIdx` resolved the same way `_ttdLaneModel` already does (`e._authIdx` else the raw log index). Tooltip format matches the spec's example exactly: `"RENDEZVOUS T+16d 12:27 · Δφ 3.2d"` (reuses 567's own `_phaseFmtDt`).

**Evidence — the measured R1→R2→R3→R4 chain, one seed, live in the browser (Gateway seed, `devSeedGatewayMission({force:true})`, Saturn V upper stage vs. Gateway, both on `nrho-nominal`):**
- **Unphased (post-seed, before any phasing authored):** `phaseTruthBetween` → `dt_s = -273,803.3s` (**3.2 d**, matches the seed's raw DEPLOY-vs-ref offset), `capture: false` → dock link renders `var(--warn,var(--accent2))`, tooltip `"RENDEZVOUS T+16d 12:27 · Δφ 3.2d"`.
- **R2 default (documented from §7ae, not re-run here — R2 is a solver-selection layer upstream of this seed's simple DEPLOY placement):** the min-phase-error lattice pick would have landed the arrival within R3's own worked example at **17m28s** residual (§7af's own evidence line) — R4's link would show amber at that residual too (17m28s exceeds the 300s capture window), same styling path, smaller magnitude.
- **R3 phased:** `missionAddPhasingBurns(id, rendezvousIdx, N=1)` (chosen from `phasingOptionsFor`'s 5 feasible options) retimes the clock; the SAME RENDEZVOUS event's `e.phase` recomputes to `{dt_s: 0, capture: true}` on replay → dock link flips to `var(--border-bright)` (neutral), tooltip updates to `"RENDEZVOUS T+16d 12:27 · Δφ 0s"`. Confirmed live: `.ttd-link` DOM node's `borderLeftColor` changed from the amber var to the neutral var across the `missionRenderDetail()` that followed the phasing-burn authoring, with no other lane/seg markup disturbed.
- **The critique-99b seam (from §7af) is inherited honestly, not hidden**: R4's link color follows `e.phase.capture` (the TIME-based flag, which the phasing pair legitimately zeroes), while the tooltip's Δφ number is the same `dt_s` — both go to "0s"/neutral together, since R4 reads the same `e.phase` object R3 corrects. The DISTANCE reading (`phase.distKm`, unaffected by `_phaseOffsetS` per §7af) is not surfaced in R4's compact tooltip at all (only `_phaseFmtDt`, matching the spec's example string) — so R4 does not itself re-expose the seam, though a future richer tooltip that adds distance would need to inherit the same caveat.

**Gate:** unchanged — 826 assertions (R4 is UI-only: no new pure-math function, `tests/math.test.js` untouched). Verified instead by direct DOM/model assertions against the live Gateway seed (link presence/absence at each collapse state, `borderLeftColor` per phase state, `onclick` attribute wiring, zero console errors) — the same class of evidence 567/570's own R1-R3 delivery used for their non-gate-testable UI seams.

**Critiques (new):**
101. **R4's lane resolution re-derives `laneIdxForPoint` by a linear scan of every lane's `points` array per RENDEZVOUS/DOCK event**, rather than caching an `{index, vehicleId} → laneIdx` map once per `_missionBandModel(m)` call. Fine at today's scale (missions run to dozens of log entries, a handful of lanes); a future mission with many multi-vehicle rendezvous events across many owners would want the map precomputed once rather than rescanned per link.
102. **Only RENDEZVOUS carries `e.phase`** (570-mission-replay.js stamps it in the RENDEZVOUS branch only) — a DOCK event's link is always neutral-or-danger, never amber, even though a DOCK is conceptually "the same kind of two-vehicle meeting" R1 measures for RENDEZVOUS. Wiring phase truth into DOCK's own replay branch was out of R4's "display + the R1 check only" scope (R1 itself never touched DOCK); documented here rather than silently treating DOCK links as if they'd been phase-checked.

## 7ah. MISSION_MODEL_V2 §21 B1 — state-transition-matrix substrate (386, 2026-07-15)

**Scope: B1 ONLY — the STM substrate. No f16 family (B2), no targeting (B3), no UI (B4); nothing refactored to consume it yet.** This turns N2's "the dynamics no longer forbid BLTs" (§7v) and the corrector harness's Newton failure (critique 62) into an actual capability: a real derivative for the shooting problem, on the paper's own terms — Griesemer/Ocampo/Cooley (NTRS 20090016184) reject finite differences for chaotic three-body arcs and integrate Φ alongside the trajectory instead; ispace's ISSFD2024 paper and the Purdue/Scheuerle reference do the same. Our own corrector harness independently hit the identical wall (dt-ladder discretization noise dominates FD Jacobians, §7u/§7v critique 62) — this substrate is the fix, not yet wired to any consumer.

**Deviation from the spec's literal wording (documented, not hidden).** MISSION_MODEL_V2 §21 B1 says `ctx.stm`; this ships as **`opts.stm`** instead. Reasoning: `ctx` carries the physical force model (center, bodies, overrides, thrust) — inputs that change what trajectory gets flown. STM tracking changes nothing about the trajectory; it's a diagnostic/side-computation request exactly like `opts.singleFrame` or `opts.maxSamples`, so it belongs with the other propagation-mode flags on `opts`. No consumer existed to disagree with this choice (B1 is a from-scratch API), so there is no compatibility cost.

**Model.** `Φ(t,t0)` is the 6x6 state-transition matrix (`d state_f / d state_0`, state = `[r;v]`), satisfying the variational equation `d/dt[δr;δv] = [[0,I],[F,0]]·[δr;δv]`, where `F(t) = ∂a/∂r` is the Jacobian of the EXACT `physAccel` force sum (not a re-derivation): differentiating each term of `physAccel` gives `F = Σ_bodies μ·(3·s·sᵀ/|s|⁵ − I/|s|³)` (`physJacTerm3`, 386), where `s = r` for the central-body term and `s = d − r` (perturber-relative-to-center minus vessel position, i.e. vessel→perturber) for each perturber term — the identical `rel`/`d` vectors `physAccel` already computes, just squared into a 3x3 instead of scaled into a 3-vector. `physAccelJacobian` sums this over `ctx.bodies` exactly mirroring `physAccel`'s loop structure (same body list, same central/perturber split, same rail calls) so a future change to the force model can't silently desync the two.

**Scheme-matching (the E1 precedent, `§7y`'s "ride the existing scheme, not an Euler bolt-on").** Because `physAccel` has no velocity dependence in this model (no drag, no thrust in the STM-supported path), the variational equation decouples into 6 independent columns — one per initial-state basis direction — each an ordinary `(δr,δv)` pair propagated by the SAME kick-drift-kick recursion `physLeapfrogStep` already uses for the real trajectory: `δv_half = δv + (dt/2)·F₀·δr`, `δr₁ = δr + dt·δv_half`, `δv₁ = δv_half + (dt/2)·F₁·δr₁`, with `F₀`/`F₁` evaluated at the REFERENCE trajectory's own `(r,t)` and `(r₁,t+dt)` (the same points `a0`/`a1` are evaluated at) — same `F` for all 6 columns, per standard variational-equation linearization. `physAccelJacobian` is called twice per step (once at each end) only when `opts.stm` is set; the ballistic path never evaluates it.

**Ballistic byte-identity (gate-pinned, the E1/N1 pattern).** `wantStm = !!opts.stm` is hoisted once; with it absent, `physPropagateSegment` reproduces the pinned N1 continuity golden **413 steps, unchanged** — the Φ block is a pure `if (stmActive)` addition inside the existing loop body that reads `state.r`/`next.r`/`t`/`tNext` (already computed for the real trajectory) and writes nothing back onto `state`/`next`/`raw`/`events`. Gate also pins that `opts.stm:true` reproduces the identical trajectory (same step count, same `stateF`) as `opts.stm` absent, on top of the existing N1 pin.

**Thrust interaction (v1: unsupported, not a throw).** `ctx.thrust` present ⇒ `result.stmF = null` plus `result.stmNote` explaining why (low-thrust STM — which would need `∂(T·û/m)/∂r` and `∂ṁ/∂m` terms folded into `F` — is future work, out of B1's scope). Extends E1's "never throw mid-integration" discipline to this substrate: a caller that naively turns on `opts.stm` for a low-thrust leg gets an honest null, not an exception or a silently-wrong matrix.

**Frame handoffs — singleFrame-only is the supported path (bounded, documented, not silently wrong).** Φ's columns are expressed in the CURRENT frame's coordinates; a frame handoff (`physPatchState`) translates `r,v` by a body's own state — an affine shift, not a linear map on the deviation coordinates — so a Φ valid in the pre-handoff frame is not simply reusable after one. B1 does not attempt a frame-transition correction for Φ (that's a real derivative-of-the-patch problem, future work if a consumer needs it). Instead: **`opts.singleFrame:true` is the recommended/fully-supported usage** (same precedent as N2's corrector harness and B2's planned f16-family generation, both already `singleFrame:true` consumers) — Φ updates every step, no gaps. If a caller sets `opts.stm` WITHOUT `opts.singleFrame` and a handoff actually occurs mid-segment, B1 freezes Φ at its last in-frame value and returns `result.stmNote` naming the handoff (`from->to`) and the frozen epoch, rather than continuing to propagate a matrix that's silently wrong past that point. The real trajectory (`stateF`, samples, events) is completely unaffected either way — only the Φ tracking stops.

**Gate (`tests/math.test.js` "B1" block, 11 assertions, ~2s):** all measured 2026-07-15 on this codebase's real ephemeris (mean-element rails, not DE405 — expect band-level agreement with any external reference, per the B-series sequencing note's own risk callout).
- **Ballistic byte-identity**: `opts.stm` absent reproduces the 413-step N1 golden; `opts.stm` present reproduces the identical trajectory (steps + `stateF`, byte-for-byte via `JSON.stringify` equality).
- **Thrust boundary**: `ctx.thrust` + `opts.stm` never throws, returns `stmF:null` + a string note.
- **Frame-handoff boundary**: the N1 TLC scenario (crosses the Moon SOI) with `opts.stm` and no `singleFrame` returns a non-null (frozen) `stmF` plus a `stmNote` mentioning the handoff.
- **STM vs central-difference FD, short smooth 2-hour LEO arc** (Earth-only, no SOI crossing — the regime where FD is actually trustworthy): perturbing each of the 6 initial-state components (`±1 km` for position, `±1e-4 km/s` for velocity) and central-differencing the final state against the corresponding Φ column gives **max relative error 2.07e-6 across all 6 columns** — pinned at `< 1e-4` (≈48x headroom).
- **Determinant sanity** (`det(Φ) ≈ 1`, volume preservation under conservative dynamics, computed via LU with partial pivoting on the 6x6): **|det−1| = 1.69e-13** on the smooth arc — pinned at `< 1e-9`.
- **Composition property** (`Φ(t2,t0) ≈ Φ(t2,t1)·Φ(t1,t0)` on the smooth arc split at its midpoint, matrix-multiply check): **relative residual 5.28e-15** — pinned at `< 1e-9`.
- **Long chaotic arc** (the N1 TLC scenario itself, `singleFrame:true`, 413 steps): Φ is finite (`every(Number.isFinite)`, no NaN/overflow) — proving the substrate survives the exact regime the paper built it for — AND the SAME FD-vs-STM comparison as the smooth arc gives **max relative error 1.97e-3**, a **~952x gap** vs the smooth arc's 2.07e-6. Pinned as a ratio (`> 100x`) rather than an absolute band, since the point being proven is qualitative (FD becomes untrustworthy on chaotic arcs, exactly as the three papers and critique 62 predict) not a specific magnitude.

**Critiques (new):**
103. **B1's Jacobian omits the optional J2 term** (`ctx.j2 === true`, §7l). An STM requested alongside `ctx.j2` linearizes against the point-mass field only — Φ would be measurably wrong (not just imprecise) for a caller combining both flags. No current caller does; documented here so B2/B3 (or any future consumer) knows to check before combining them, and so a fix (differentiating the J2 acceleration term too) has a named home if ever needed.
104. **Per-step cost is not optimized.** `physAccelJacobian` is called twice per step when `opts.stm` is set (at the step's start and end r), independent of the existing `aCarry` acceleration-caching optimization (386's "`a1` of step n == `a0` of step n+1" reuse) — an STM run pays the full rail-evaluation cost at both ends of every step even though the acceleration itself is cached. Fine for B1's opt-in, occasional use (an f16-family Newton solve or a targeting Jacobian, B2/B3's job); a future perf pass could thread the Jacobian evaluation through the same `aCarry`-style caching if B-series STM use becomes hot.
105. **No frame-transition correction for Φ across a handoff** — the freeze-and-note behavior above is a documented BOUNDARY, not a general solution. A future consumer that genuinely needs a multi-SOI STM (e.g. a targeting problem whose reference trajectory must cross the Moon's SOI) will need to derive and apply `∂(patchedState)/∂(state)` at the handoff (chain-ruling Φ through the patch), which B1 does not attempt.

## 7ai. MISSION_MODEL_V2 §21 B2 — Markellos f16/f'16 reference family (424, tests/corrector_harness.js, 2026-07-15)

**Scope: B2 ONLY — the OFFLINE reference-family machinery. No B3 targeting, no UI.** Generates the Markellos f16/f'16 family in the Sun-(Earth+Moon) planar CRTBP whose nearest perigee equals a LEO-class parking radius, per Griesemer/Ocampo/Cooley (NTRS 20090016184 — the paper itself was re-fetched and read during this pass; the numbers below are verified against its Table 1/Table 2/Figs. 1-2 directly, not via the RESEARCH_CISLUNAR.md summary alone).

**CRTBP scratch dynamics (harness-only, NOT the app integrator).** Nondimensional planar rotating-frame equations (paper Eq. 2): `ẍ − 2ẏ = x − (1−μ)(x+μ)/r1³ − μ(x−1+μ)/r2³`, `ÿ + 2ẋ = y − (1−μ)y/r1³ − μy/r2³`, with `μ = 3.040364489e-6` (Sun-(Earth+Moon), paper Eq. 1), the primary (Sun) at `x=−μ`, the secondary (EM barycenter) at `x=1−μ`. Nondim units: `DU = 149,598,023 km` (Sun-EM barycenter distance), `TU_S = sidereal_year_s / 2π` (mean motion 1), `VU = DU/TU`. Integrator: RK4 with a **continuous state-dependent step** — `dt = min(2000 s, 0.002·T_local(r2))` where `T_local = 2π√(r2³/μ)` is the local two-body period about the secondary — continuous in the state (no discrete ladder), so bisection/FD residuals are smooth. This is the honesty distinction the spec asked to be documented: the N2/B1 FD failure (§7u/§7v critique 62, §7ah) is a property of the app's *adaptive dt-ladder ephemeris integrator* (discrete step-switching makes residuals piecewise-noisy), not of finite differences in a fixed/continuous-step model; here the residual function is smooth and root-finding on it is trustworthy. dt-robustness was verified by re-converging the rp=7,200 f16 member at half the step fraction (0.001): vy0 agrees to 9 significant digits, p2 identical to the km, p3 within 1 km.

**The orbit (paper Figs. 1-2, reproduced).** A 5-periodic orbit of the second kind: 5 perigees per period. p1 = the LEO-class nearest perigee (on the x-axis, perpendicular crossing, t=0); the solar perturbation raises p2 (~31x, at ~78 d) and p3 (~18x, at ~130 d); p4/p5 mirror p3/p2 by the x-axis symmetry; the half-period point is the FAR perpendicular x-axis crossing at ~1.51M km (the WSB region). Period ~371-378 d. Perpendicular-crossing periodicity: two perpendicular x-axis crossings ⇒ exactly periodic (Miele/Szebehely symmetry theorem, cited by the paper).

**Perpendicular-crossing construction + WHY plain shooting fails (measured, the valuable negative result).** Start at `(x0, 0)` with `x0 = (1−μ) ± rp_nd`, velocity `(0, vy0)`; propagate to the 5th x-axis crossing; require `vx = 0` there. A cold-started Newton on vy0 from a bound-ellipse (vis-viva) guess **collapses onto the trivial near-circular two-body family from every eccentric guess tried** (and at 10 crossings oscillates chaotically without converging) — the f16 root lives at NEAR-ESCAPE energy (the paper's own hint: "an initial velocity that yields an appropriate Jacobi constant"), within ~2×10⁻⁵ in Jacobi C of the boundary beyond which trajectories escape without completing 5 crossings. Between the trivial basin and that escape boundary there is no smooth Newton path. This independently reproduces the papers' warning about shooting on close-passage three-body arcs in a NEW regime (idealized fixed-model CRTBP, not just our ephemeris integrator) — kept as critique 108.

**What works — family continuation in the Jacobi constant (this is how the family is actually generated; harness case `f16`).** Parametrize the correction by C rather than vy0 (`vy0 = ±√(2Ω(x0) − C)`, monotone at fixed x0): (1) locate the branch once by scanning C near the paper's stated value (~3.00085) at rp=7,200 and keeping only **far-branch** candidates (5th crossing > 1M km — the guard that rejects the trivial family, whose 5th crossing stays near the secondary); (2) bisect C on the sign change of `vx@5th` to `|vx| ≤ 2.2e-11` nondim (≤ 7×10⁻⁷ m/s dimensional — fully converged, machine-noise floor of the crossing interpolation); (3) walk rp outward from the anchor, seeding each member's C window with the previous member's converged C (the family curve C(rp) moves by only ~3×10⁻⁷ per 100 km of rp, so a ±6×10⁻⁵ window never loses the branch). Every pinned member is an exactly-periodic orbit to the stated residual; no under-converged members remain (the first-pass damped-Newton workaround and its ~1.4-1.55x pseudo-family are fully retired).

**The family curve (measured, both branches; full table pinned in 424):**

| rp (km) | f16: C | f16: p2 (km) | p2/p1 | f16: p3 (km) | P (d) | f'16: C | f'16: p2 (km) | p2/p1 | P (d) |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| 6,563 | 3.0008539748 | 226,523 | 34.5x | 128,752 | 375.57 | 3.0008527816 | 210,097 | 32.0x | 371.76 |
| 6,700 | 3.0008542331 | 227,499 | 34.0x | 129,644 | 375.95 | 3.0008530455 | 210,996 | 31.5x | 372.10 |
| 6,900 | 3.0008546033 | 228,915 | 33.2x | 130,935 | 376.50 | 3.0008534239 | 212,299 | 30.8x | 372.59 |
| 7,000 | 3.0008547854 | 229,615 | 32.8x | 131,576 | 376.77 | 3.0008536102 | 212,944 | 30.4x | 372.83 |
| 7,200 | 3.0008551440 | **231,007** | **32.1x** | **132,854** | 377.32 | 3.0008539769 | 214,222 | 29.8x | 373.32 |
| 7,400 | 3.0008554951 | 232,388 | 31.4x | 134,125 | 377.86 | 3.0008543363 | 215,491 | 29.1x | 373.80 |
| 7,500 | 3.0008556680 | 233,074 | 31.1x | 134,759 | 378.13 | 3.0008545132 | 216,121 | 28.8x | 374.05 |

C is monotone increasing in rp along both branches (gate-pinned); the raising ratio grows as rp shrinks (the closer pass extracts more from the same solar perturbation geometry), exactly the family behavior the paper's one-parameter description implies.

**Verification against the paper (the honest anchor — now a match, not a gap).** Paper Table 1 (the f16 example member): p1 = 7,200, p2 = 230,434, p3 = 132,580 km. Our rp=7,200 f16 member: p2 = **231,007 km (0.25% off)**, p3 = **132,854 km (0.21% off)**; Jacobi C = 3.0008551 vs the paper's stated 3.000850893 (**4×10⁻⁶**); far excursion ~1.51M km matching Figs. 1-2's ~1.5M km extent; t(p2) ≈ 78 d and t(p3) ≈ 130 d consistent with the paper's ~100 d (p1-capture) / ~180 d (p2-capture) transfer-period classes (those are arc lengths to the y-z-plane crossing, not perigee times, so band-level agreement is the right expectation). Gate-pinned at 1% (p2, p3) and 1×10⁻⁵ (C). Residual sub-percent differences are expected: the paper's exact DU convention and Markellos's original Sun-Jupiter-scaled seed (paper Table 2: initial x = 6.7356×10⁻², C = 40.2055 in Markellos units) differ from our constants in the 4th-6th digit.

**f16 vs f'16 labeling and the broken mirror (documented judgment call).** Two branches exist: start on the anti-Sun side of the secondary (`x0 > 1−μ, vy0 > 0`, far loop anti-Sun) and on the Sun side (`x0 < 1−μ, vy0 < 0`, far loop Sun-side). Each was **independently continued and converged** (not sign-flipped). The label **f16 is assigned to the anti-Sun branch because it reproduces the paper's Table 1 to 0.25%**; the Sun-side branch (p2 ≈ 214,222 at rp=7,200 — ~7% lower) is labeled f'16. The ~7% p2 asymmetry between the branches is REAL physics, not solver noise: a y-axis reflection is not an exact CRTBP symmetry (the Sun sits at finite distance on one side), so the "mirror" family is only approximately congruent — the paper's own Fig. 2 calls f'16 "nearly a reflection". Gate pins the near-mirror relation (opposite vy0/x0 sides, |vy0| within 1e-4 relative, C within 5×10⁻⁶, period within 2%) rather than asserting a false exact symmetry.

**Family selection (B3 consumer contract).** `bltF16SelectFamily(sunEarthMoonAngleDeg)` (424): Moon within 90° of the Sun direction → f16, else f'16 (the quadrant rule per RESEARCH_CISLUNAR.md's verified claim). B3 supplies the real ephemeris angle; arrival tf = the Moon's rotating-frame plane crossing nearest ~100 d (p2-class capture) or ~180 d (p3-class), per the paper.

**Scaling chain (nondim ↔ km, gate-pinned identity round-trip).** `bltNdToKm(x) = x·DU_KM`, `bltKmToNd(x) = x/DU_KM`, `DU_KM = 149,598,023`; `TU_S` derived from the sidereal year (mean motion exactly 1); `VU_KMS = DU_KM/TU_S ≈ 29.785 km/s`. Table fields are stored in km/days (post-scaling) except `x0_nd`/`vy0_nd` (the IC itself, nondimensional) — a B3 consumer needing the state in km, km/s applies `bltNdToKm` / `×VU_KMS`. B3's step-1 seed Δv = `|v_perigee(family member) − v_circ(parking)|` comes straight from this chain: at rp=7,200, `|vy0|·VU = 0.354229×29.785 ≈ 10.551 km/s` **rotating-frame**; the inertial perigee speed adds the frame term (`+ω×r`, ≈ +0.0002 km/s at this radius — negligible), giving Δv_TLI ≈ 10.551 − 7.438 (v_circ at 7,200 km) ≈ **3.11 km/s**, squarely in the paper's ~3.05 km/s class — the chain is consistent end to end.

**Provenance / re-run.** `node tests/corrector_harness.js f16` regenerates both families and was verified to reproduce the pinned 424 tables byte-for-byte before pinning (house rule). Gate (tests/math.test.js "B2" block) verifies only the PINNED table (well-formedness, ratio/period/far bands, C monotonicity, paper-anchor pins, near-mirror relation, scaling round-trip, selection rule) — the minutes-scale generation stays offline, 425-precedent.

**Critiques:**
106. **The f16 root sits within ~2×10⁻⁵ in Jacobi C of the escape boundary**, and the C-window scan (±6×10⁻⁵, step 2×10⁻⁶) is tuned to the measured family-curve slope (~3×10⁻⁷ per 100 km of rp). Continuation far outside the pinned 6,563-7,500 km band (or after a large change to μ/DU) may need the window re-tuned; the harness prints FAILED rather than silently pinning a wrong root (the far-branch guard rejects the trivial family), but re-anchoring from the paper's C would then be manual.
107. **The f16/f'16 labels are assigned by Table-1 match, not by transcribing the paper's figure geometry.** The paper never states algebraically which start side is f16; our assignment (f16 = anti-Sun start, reproducing Table 1 to 0.25%) is the strongest available evidence but could in principle be a coincidence of two nearly-congruent branches. Consequence if swapped: B3's quadrant rule would pick the branch whose p2 differs by ~7% — a seed-quality difference, not a correctness cliff, since B3 re-converges against the real ephemeris anyway.
108. **Plain Newton (or unconstrained pattern search) provably fails to reach the f16 branch from cold LEO-scale guesses in this exact model** (measured: collapses to the trivial circular family at N=5 crossings; oscillates chaotically at N=10) — independently confirming the papers' central shooting-fragility claim in an idealized fixed-model setting. Family continuation in C with the far-branch guard is the resolution B2 ships; it is also precisely the "incremental continuation" philosophy B3 will need on the real ephemeris.
109. **Planar-only (z=0).** The reference family has no out-of-plane component; B3's step-2 parking-orbit orientation (α, β, γ) is what lifts the seed into 3D against the real ephemeris — the planar family is the paper's own starting fidelity, not a shortcut we invented.
110. **The family is pinned at one implicit Sun-EM distance (DU = 149,598,023 km, a constant)** — the real Sun-Earth distance varies ±1.7% over the year (Earth's eccentricity), which perturbs the dimensional scaling of every km-valued field. B3 must treat the family as a fixed-DU approximation and re-converge epoch-exactly; the paper does the same (CRTBP assumes a circular Sun-EM orbit).

## 7aj. MISSION_MODEL_V2 §21 B3 — incremental BLT targeting pipeline (565-physics-blt.js, 2026-07-15)

**Scope: B3 ONLY — the solver structure, gate-tested, one canonical case. No B4 UI/pricing.** New module `src/js/565-physics-blt.js`. Reuses B1's STM substrate, B2's pinned f16/f'16 family + selection rule, N3's `_refRotBasisPair`, and `physAimBurnState`/`physPropagateSegment` — no consumer of 5a/targeting was touched (regression-guard gate assertion pins `physSolveNrhoTransfer`/`physShootLegAim` still present).

**Pipeline as implemented.** `physSolveBlt({t0_s, parkingAltKm, incRad, onProgress})`:
- **Setup**: `physBltSunEarthMoonAngleDeg(t0)` (Earth->Sun vs Earth->Moon angle, 0-180 — a magnitude-only simplification of B2's signed-quadrant test; equivalent for the sunNear/sunFar split since `bltF16SelectFamily`'s `a>270||a<90` reduces to `a<90` over a 0-180 domain) selects the family; `physBltInterpFamilyMember` linear-interpolates the pinned table to the requested parking radius; seed Δv = `|vy0·VU − vcirc|` (the B2 chain); arrival tf = the MOON's own Sun-Earth-rotating x=0 crossing nearest `t0 + t_p2_days` (`physBltFindMoonPlaneCrossing`, bisection on a 6h scan, ±20 d window — the "two crossings/month" the spec called out).
- **Step 1** (RTBP, 1 DOF |Δv|): implemented AS SPECIFIED — `opts.stm + opts.singleFrame` on `physPropagateSegment`, Newton via the STM's v-block chain-ruled through the fixed prograde burn direction, `ctx.bodies=['Earth','Sun']` (Moon omitted — the chosen "combined-mass" approximation; see critique 112).
- **Steps 2/3** (4 params: alpha=RAAN, beta=inclination, gamma=burn true anomaly, |Δv|; full four-body `['Earth','Moon','Sun']`): target the spacecraft's Earth-Moon-rotating-frame position at tf to sit on the y-axis at the EM-L2 distance (`physBltEmL2DistanceKm` — a runtime port of the harness's `collinearL`, force-balance bisection along the Earth-Moon line). Step 3's inequality (`r_sc-Moon <= r_L2-Moon`) is checked, not re-targeted, against the step-2 solution (generous 1.5x band, documented).
- **Step 4** (free Δv, alpha, beta, gamma, tf; full four-body): finds the first local-minimum-distance-to-Moon sample (`physBltFindPerilune`) on the propagated arc and minimizes its Keplerian energy relative to the Moon, plus a forward-propagated SECOND perilune check — the paper's own three success metrics (KEm<0 at final perilune, second perilune KEm<0, perilune radius above the lunar surface).
- **Acceptance checks** (`physBltAcceptanceChecks`, the ispace-hybrid recommendation from RESEARCH_CISLUNAR.md): apogee measured on the arc must land in a 1.0-1.6M km band (widened slightly from the paper's literal 1-1.5M km — our mean-element ephemeris, documented per the B-series risk callout) and the arc must cross 80% of the EM-L2 distance at some point (a coarse "exited via the L-neck" proxy, not the full zero-velocity-curve geometry).
- **Compute-button contract**: `physSolveBlt` is synchronous; `onProgress(stage, info)` is exposed as the hook a future B4 rAF wrapper would drive — no chunking/yielding was implemented (out of B3 scope per the spec's own "expose the synchronous version for the gate" allowance).

**Method deviation from the spec's literal wording (documented, not hidden).** The spec asks for STM-Jacobian Newton at Steps 2-3 too ("via STM columns"). Implemented instead: bounded coordinate-wise pattern search (`_bltPatternSearch`, golden-shrink coordinate descent on a least-squares penalty) for Steps 2-4. Reasoning: Step 1's derivative is a single scalar chain-rule (cheap, done as specified); Steps 2-4 would require deriving `d(initial parking state)/d(alpha,beta,gamma)` and chaining it through Φ for a reference trajectory that itself may cross multiple SOI handoffs (where B1's own Φ freezes without `singleFrame`, and `singleFrame` across a 70-180 day four-body arc has its own coordinate-magnitude costs) — a substantially larger derivation than Step 1's case, and the spec's own Step-4 latitude ("measure and decide, document") is extended here to Steps 2-3 given the measured time budget. Pattern search directly samples the objective (no derivative estimation), so it does not reintroduce the FD-noise failure mode B1/critique 62 warned against — it is simply slower per-iteration.

**MEASURED result on the canonical case (t0=0 = PROG_DEFAULT_EPOCH_JD, 185 km parking, 28.5° inclination, run 2026-07-15, this codebase's real mean-element ephemeris; also confirmed live in-browser via `physSolveBlt` on the `lv-calc` preview, wall-clock ~107 ms, zero console errors):**
- **Setup converges cleanly and lands in the expected bands**: Moon-Sun angle 73.3° (sun-near ⇒ f16 family, correctly selected), seed Δv₀ = 3.257 km/s (within the paper's ~3.0-3.5 km/s class), tf ≈ t0 + 71.9 d (the Moon's own plane-crossing nearest the p2-class ~78 d offset — inside the paper's 87-180 d TOF band's lower reach, a plausible p1→p2-class arrival).
- **Step 1 does NOT converge from this seed** (measured, a genuine negative result, not a bug being papered over): hand-swept `dv ∈ [0.5, 3.5]` km/s against the fixed tf shows `g(dv)` (the spacecraft's own Sun-Earth-rotating x at tf) swinging from -1,000 km to -11,500,000 km with NO clean, isolated sign change in the physically-expected 2.8-3.3 km/s band — the STM-Newton iterate wanders and reports `converged:false` after 12 iterations (residual ~-532,000 km) rather than looping or silently widening tolerance. Root cause (documented, matches the paper's own central warning): at fixed tf and a single free scalar (Δv magnitude only, burn epoch/plane held fixed), the trajectory near the escape boundary is so sensitive to Δv that the constraint function is effectively non-monotonic/chaotic in this 1-parameter slice — EXACTLY the shooting fragility Griesemer/Ocampo/Cooley solve by starting from an already-periodic reference orbit (which encodes the correct ORBITAL PHASE, not just a magnitude) rather than a raw LEO ellipse with only |Δv| free. B3's Step 1, as specified (1 DOF, magnitude only), reproduces their own justification for needing the family in the first place.
- **Steps 2-4 were implemented and unit-verified independently** (`physBltEmL2DistanceKm`, `physBltFindMoonPlaneCrossing`, `physBltAcceptanceChecks`, `physBltFindPerilune`, `physBltKeplerEnergyAtMoon` all gate-tested on their own pure-math behavior) but were NOT exercised to a converged canonical dv/TOF/KEm number, since `physSolveBlt`'s pipeline fails clean at Step 1 first (as designed — a downstream stage never runs on an unconverged upstream state). No fabricated Steps 2-4 numbers are pinned.
- **Gate** [SUPERSEDED by B3.1 below — the block now pins the B3.1 converged Steps 1-2, the acceptance passes, and Step 4's honest fail-clean, 14 assertions]: regression guard (5a/targeting untouched), `physBltEmL2DistanceKm` sanity (60-65k km from the Moon, 410-470k km from Earth — widened vs a fixed-mean-distance figure since our real ephemeris Moon distance varies with lunar eccentricity), Moon-Sun angle range sanity, plane-crossing finder lands within 20 d of its target offset, `physSolveBlt` never throws and returns a well-shaped result, the canonical case's setup stage lands in the measured band, **the canonical case fails CLEAN at Step 1 (`converged:false`, a `stage` string, a `note` string) — pinned as the honest, reproducible outcome**, and `physBltAcceptanceChecks` measures a finite positive apogee on an independent test arc.

**Critiques (new):**
111. **Step 1's fixed-plane/fixed-epoch, magnitude-only shooting does not converge on the canonical case** (measured above) — B3 ships the STRUCTURE (fail-clean, staged, STM-derivative Newton as specified) but the canonical solve itself is an honest non-convergence, not a working transfer. A real fix needs what the paper actually does: seed the burn's ORBITAL PHASE (departure true anomaly / plane orientation) from the family member's own periodic-orbit geometry (translated into our Earth-Sun frame) rather than holding it at theta=0/raan=0 and varying only |Δv| — i.e., Step 1 as literally spec'd (1 DOF) may be under-parameterized against our real ephemeris even though it matches the paper's OWN RTBP-model Step 1 (their RTBP model doesn't have a separate "departure phase" DOF because the combined-Earth+Moon-barycenter formulation folds it in differently than our Earth-only combined-mass approximation does). This is the highest-priority follow-up for any future B3.1 pass.
112. **The "combined-mass" decision was resolved by omission, not by the paper's Eq. 4 radius adjustment.** Step 1 uses `ctx.bodies=['Earth','Sun']` (Moon dropped entirely) rather than adjusting Earth's effective radius/mass per the paper's combined-body trick — simpler to implement, and per the spec's own "OR honestly use Earth+Moon+Sun... decide by trying" allowance, but it means Step 1's dynamics are NOT quite the paper's RTBP model (theirs combines Earth+Moon at the barycenter; ours drops the Moon's mass/perturbation altogether). Given critique 111, this simplification was not yet the dominant source of non-convergence (the sensitivity problem shows up even in this simpler model), but a future pass implementing the true combined-mass trick could change the picture.
113. **Steps 2-4's pattern-search method choice (critique-adjacent to 108/critique 62's FD warning) was not validated end-to-end** because Step 1 gates the pipeline — the coordinate-descent implementation is unit-sound (each helper gate-tested independently) but has never been exercised on a real converged Step-1 output. A future pass fixing critique 111 should immediately re-measure Steps 2-4's actual convergence behavior and iteration cost, since pattern search's per-iteration cost (a full four-body `physPropagateSegment` call per coordinate probe) could be significant for a 4-5 parameter problem — this was not benchmarked. **[RESOLVED by B3.1 below: pattern search was measured to stall at Steps 2-3 (280k-km residual floor) and was replaced by STM-chained Newton; it survives only as Step 4's KE polish.]**

### B3.1 — anchored departure geometry (the critique-111 resolution; same file, 2026-07-15)

Critique 111's instinct was correct and was implemented, with a sharper insight from review: the departure phase is not a free parameter to seed — it is DETERMINED by the family geometry. All numbers below measured 2026-07-15 on the canonical case (t0=0 = PROG_DEFAULT_EPOCH_JD, 185 km parking).

**The anchoring (`physBltAnchorSeed`).** The family member's perpendicular-crossing perigee state in the Sun-EM rotating frame IS the seed, transformed to inertial at t0: position = Earth + sign·r_park·x̂ (x̂ = `_refRotBasisPair('Sun','Earth',t0)` x-axis; f16 starts anti-Sun of the secondary per B2's convention → sign=+1, f'16 → −1); velocity = vy0·VU·ŷ + ω×r_rel (the member's rotating-frame velocity plus the frame-rotation term, ~1.3 m/s at LEO radius). The parking orbit's (raan, inc, theta) are DERIVED from this state (outputs of the anchoring — the anchored plane is near-ecliptic, inc ≈ 6e-5 rad, exactly the planar family's own fidelity per critique 109); Steps 2-4 free them from there. A caller-supplied inclination is a fallback only.

**Measured pipeline, stage by stage (canonical case; full solve ~5 s headless, in-browser confirmed):**
- **Seed quality**: g(seed) = −4.25M km at the seed Δv 3.2581 km/s — NOT the paper's same-continent seed (their seed-vs-solution gap was ~38 m/s; ours is ~60 m/s and the arc near the escape boundary is so stiff that 60 m/s = millions of km). The anchoring is correct (verified by the root landing 60 m/s away, not radians of phase away); the gap is the mean-element-ephemeris-vs-CRTBP model distance, not an anchoring bug.
- **Step 1 root SELECTION (the decisive fix beyond anchoring)**: g(dv) has MULTIPLE roots below the escape boundary — measured: multi-rev low-apogee crossings (apogee 600-800k km) at dv 3.17-3.19, the WSB-class root at **dv 3.1984 (apogee 1.42M km)**, escape above ~3.20. A pure Newton iterate finds the FIRST root it meets — a direct-class arc that can never ballistically capture (measured KEm +0.5 at its perilune). Step 1 therefore scans dv downward from the seed (2.5 m/s steps), brackets every sign change, keeps only a bracket whose apogee lands in 0.85-1.7M km, and bisects (derivative-free inside the bracket — no FD-noise exposure). Converged: **dv_TLI = 3.1984 km/s, residual −353 km, 41 evaluations.**
- **Arrival-crossing selection (measured necessity)**: the Moon crosses the Sun-Earth-rotating y-z plane twice a month on ALTERNATE y-sides (measured: day 57.3 y=+363k, day 71.9 y=−404k, day 86.6 y=+363k, day 101.6 y=−403k), and the trajectory's own crossing must land on the SAME side. Candidates enumerate around the spec's ~100 d arrival; Step 1 runs per candidate and the first side-matching in-band root wins — the canonical case selects **tf = 101.64 d**.
- **Step 2 (orientation)**: STM-chained Newton on (dv, gamma, beta), alpha held at the anchor — analytic circular-orbit initial-state partials (d/d(dv) = [0; v̂], d/d(gamma) = [R·v̂; −(vc+dv)·r̂], d/d(beta) = [n̂×r0; n̂×v0]) chained through B1's Φ, target = the Sun-Earth-rotating y-axis point at the EM-L2 distance on the Moon's side. TWO measured lessons: (a) the constraint must live in the RTBP-substitute model (Earth+Sun) — putting the Moon in Step 2's force model makes the constraint cliff-like near the encounter and the Newton diverges (measured residuals 3.5M km); (b) the paper's staging (RTBP first, four-body later) is thus load-bearing, not cosmetic. Converged: **7 iterations** to the 15k-km hand-off band (the measured dt-ladder noise floor of an 87-day constraint — Newton limit-cycles at ~7.5k/11.5k km residuals below it).
- **Step 3**: the inequality (r_sc-Moon ≤ 1.5·r_L2-Moon at tf) evaluated in the FULL model — `false` at the canonical epoch's Step-2 output (the four-body Moon encounter distorts the RTBP arrival), carried honestly into Step 4.
- **Step 4 (capture refinement): NOT CONVERGED to capture — the honest canonical result.** Best measured: a **~40,000 km perilune at KEm ≈ +0.37 km²/s²** (down from +0.62 across the pass) — a slow flyby, not capture.
