Two real discoveries en route: (a) the **Moon's out-of-plane offset is the distance floor** — the Moon sits at z = +32k km (ecliptic) at the arrival epoch, exactly the ~25-40k km floor a near-planar arc hits; chasing it needs the full ~5° lunar inclination in the arc's plane, and at the anchored near-zero inclination alpha-and-gamma are DEGENERATE rotations (an alpha-including gate Jacobian went near-singular and wandered 0.65 rad); (b) an L2-gate-targeting Newton (position constraint at Moon+62k km along the Earth-Moon line, params beta/gamma/dv) stalls at ~220k km with a persistently wrong Newton direction — the 110-day-arc linearization through the lunar encounter region is unreliable, precisely the single-shooting fragility both reference papers escalate past with MULTIPLE SHOOTING.
- **Acceptance checks (ispace-hybrid, RESEARCH_CISLUNAR.md): both PASS** on the canonical arc — apogee 1,423,889 km (in the 1-1.5M band), L-neck exit true.
- **Final canonical numbers**: dv_TLI 3.198 km/s (paper class 3.0-3.2 ✓), TOF ~110 d (band 87-180 ✓), apogee 1.42M km (✓), KEm +0.37 at a 40k-km perilune (capture ✗) — `{converged:false, stage:'step4', note}` fail-clean, no false capture claim. Gate pins Steps 1-2 convergence + the acceptance passes + the fail-clean shape + Step 4's real perilune (868 total assertions).

**Critiques (new):**
114. **Step 4 does not reach ballistic capture on the canonical epoch.** The capture set at this energy is a measure-thin filament (the WSB structure itself); single-shooting STM-Newton through the lunar-encounter region has a measured-unreliable linearization at 110-day range, and pattern search stalls on the spiky perilune landscape (the perilune IDENTITY jumps between distant local minima under mm/s parameter changes — measured 24.9k vs 184.6k km perilunes from near-identical params). The escalation both papers prescribe — multiple shooting with patch points (+ the ispace backwards-from-arrival seeding RESEARCH_CISLUNAR.md recommends) — is the named fix; it is a solver-architecture investment beyond B3's single-session scope.
115. **The Step-2 hand-off tolerance (15k km) is the measured dt-ladder noise floor, not a solution tolerance** — below it the Newton limit-cycles. Any future tightening needs the integrator's step ladder made continuous in the state (the corrector harness's §7ai approach) or the constraint evaluated on a fixed-step re-integration of the terminal arc.
116. **Perilune identification (`physBltPeriluneFrom`) is scan-resolution-bound.** It prefers exact Moon-frame periapsis events (only fired inside the SOI) and falls back to a 512-sample argmin over the continuation window; distant local minima (L2-region loiters at 200-900k km, KEm ≈ 0-0.3) are structurally easy to confuse with true perilune candidates, and Step 4's objective inherits that ambiguity. An SOI-entry-gated definition ("first periapsis event after SOI entry, else no perilune") would be stricter but was measured to make the objective mostly-undefined at canonical-epoch parameters (the arc rarely enters the SOI) — the looser definition keeps the search alive at the cost of critique 114's landscape spikiness.

### B3.2 — multiple-shooting capture closure attempt (offline, `tests/mshoot_harness.js`, 2026-07-15 — NOT wired into 565-physics-blt.js)

Critique 114's named escalation (multiple shooting + ispace-hybrid backwards-seeding) was implemented, audited, and run headlessly against the canonical case. **Result: honest non-convergence — but with the failure mode now precisely localized and bookkeeping-clean, so the conclusion is credible physics rather than solver noise.** Not wired into `physSolveBlt` — Step 4 (single-shooting STM-Newton to an L2 gate + KE pattern polish) is UNCHANGED and remains the shipped path, per the spec's own "if MS doesn't clearly win, keep single-shooting, document" latitude.

**The first-pass bug — kept as narrative because the diagnosis is the lesson.** The first run reported position continuity closing to 0.14 km while velocity continuity "stalled" at **70.8 km/s** and called it a stationary point. Coordinator review rejected that number on physical grounds (no two ballistic arcs meeting at one cislunar point disagree by 70 km/s — that magnitude is frame/units territory, ~2.4× Earth's heliocentric speed), and the audit proved it: `physPropagateSegment`'s main loop is `while (t < tMax)`, so the backward-seeding call (tMax < t0) **never iterated and silently returned the initial state as the final state**. The "backward-propagated" seed nodes were the raw terminal capture state stamped at epochs 9-18 days earlier — a systematic bookkeeping offset fed straight into the corrector, which then least-squares-smeared it. Three permanent guards were added: (1) backward propagation done properly THROUGH `physPropagateSegment` via time reversal (gravity incl. the non-inertial center-frame terms is velocity-independent, so integrating `(r, −v)` forward in τ = −t with a `railFn` that evaluates bodies at −τ, then negating the final velocity, is exact — no ad-hoc integrator), verified by a forward-then-backward round-trip self-check (recovers the start to 0.07 km / 0.0001 m/s); (2) the **initial residual profile** is printed before any correction — the forward-trunk internal boundaries come from one continuous chained propagation and are now ASSERTED to chain at ~zero (measured: exactly 0.000 km / 0.000 m/s at all of them; only the deliberate backward-seed seam shows mismatch, ~718k km / 1.11 km/s — physical scale); (3) a physicality guard: any velocity residual > 10 km/s anywhere aborts with the boundary index as a bookkeeping bug, never fed to the corrector.

**Final method** (after three measured formulation improvements on top of the fix): trunk segmented ~9 days (t0→tf), encounter leg segmented ~3 days (tf→tf+15d; uniform 9-day segments Moon-centered through the encounter had chaotically-amplifying STMs and stalled the corrector — the non-uniform grid was measured to unstick it); nSeg=16. All shooting state heliocentric regardless of a segment's integration center (the `singleFrame` geo STM equals the helio Jacobian since a segment's center state doesn't depend on the unknowns — mixed centers need no patch correction). Terminal condition made **SQUARE**: instead of pinning the backward-seed's full 6D state (which over-determined the system by 3 and parked a ~2.4 km/s least-squares leftover at the terminal boundary), the last segment targets 3 scalars — Moon-relative radius = 4,000 km, radial velocity = 0, KEm = −0.049 km²/s² (the perilune of a genuinely mildly-captured 4,000×50,000 km ellipse; the first pass's 0.9·vcirc construction was actually the APOLUNE of a deeply-bound a=3,360 km ellipse, KEm −0.73, whose backward propagation winds ~20 tight revs instead of escaping like a real WSB arrival). tf freed as an explicit unknown (75 equations, 76 unknowns — minimum-norm LM step; pinning perilune to an exact epoch was measured to creep-stall). Velocity rows weighted ×1000 and the KEm row ×10⁴ so all residual classes are commensurate in the LM objective (legitimate now that bookkeeping is proven clean — the pre-fix weighting experiment ran against broken seeds and was inconclusive). Uniform trust-region step scaling (per-component clipping was measured to mangle the Newton direction). Wall-clock ~15 s for 70 iterations.

**Measured outcome (canonical case)**: the corrector descends cleanly 7.2×10⁵ → 1.45×10³ (weighted norm, ~500×) in ~30 iterations, closing every position residual to ≤ 2.3 km and the perilune condition to −53 km / 3.0 m/s / 0.0016 km²/s² — but stalls with **velocity discontinuities smeared across the encounter boundaries: max 0.81 km/s at one boundary, aggregate ≈ 2.8 km/s across boundaries 8-14** (trunk boundaries 0-7 stay at mm/s). Flipping the capture sense (retrograde perilune velocity, `node tests/mshoot_harness.js flip`) is worse (2.0 km/s at the seam). Reading: with position continuity essentially free, the corrector has found the minimum-ΔV set of impulses connecting this trunk to this capture state — and it is **LOI-class (~2.8 km/s), not a small residual**. That is a physical finding: the B3.1 trunk, although it passes both paper acceptance checks (L-neck exit, apogee band), does NOT arrive at the encounter with capture-compatible velocity geometry at the canonical epoch — the WSB corridor is not where this trunk goes. The honest ispace-style framing: an explicit-DSM itinerary exists (the corrector's solution IS one) but at ~2.8 km/s it is no better than a conventional direct-transfer LOI, so it is reported as a measurement, not shipped as an option.

**Ballistic-capture status**: NOT achieved; `physSolveBlt` keeps Step 4's single-shooting fail-clean result (40,000 km perilune, KEm +0.37, `converged:false`) as the canonical honest outcome. Acceptance checks on the corrector's best departure state still pass (apogee 1.102M km, in band; max |z| 16,473 km along that arc). The harness stays in the repo, offline and ungated (same status as `tests/corrector_harness.js`), as re-runnable provenance for all of the above.

**B3.2 WSB-seam variant (offline, `tests/mshoot_wsb_harness.js`, 2026-07-15).** Critique 117's own named open lever — "seam at the weak stability boundary, not inside the encounter; free alpha" — was implemented and run. The physics argument: the first-pass seam sat 3-18 d from perilune INSIDE the stiff lunar-encounter region, where joining two arcs costs LOI-class Δv by nature; move the seam to the WSB (~1M km apogee), the maximally-connective region where capture/escape flips under mm/s. Measured results (canonical case, all from real runs of `mshoot_wsb_harness.js`):
- **ESCAPE ASSERTION (the precondition) PASSES for the retrograde sense**: the mild 4,000×50,000 km capture ellipse (KEm −0.091 km²/s²), placed in the RETROGRADE Moon-frame sense a few days after the trunk's plane crossing (tPeri ≈ 104.6–107.6 d), backward-propagates OUT through the L2 neck to Earth-apogee **1.15–1.41M km** (`escaped=true`) — i.e. it is genuinely WSB-compatible and meets the trunk's own ~1.1M-km apogee region. The PROGRADE sense stays bound (≤0.7M km, `escaped=false`) and is not WSB-compatible. Backward propagation is done through `physPropagateSegment` by time reversal (the §7aj first-pass bug's fix), round-trip self-checked (7.2 km / 0.004 m/s).
- **Seam placement is decisive**: the corrector's stalled aggregate velocity discontinuity is 2.6 km/s (seam at 50 d) / 3.4 (58 d) / **0.56 (65 d, best)** — placing the seam at the late-apogee approach (~65 d, out of the stiff region) cuts the implied DSM ~5× versus the encounter seam.
- **Freed alpha is DEGENERATE at the anchored near-zero departure inclination** (α stays ~π, unused — the same α/γ degeneracy B3.1 critique 114 documents). The plane DOF that matters is departure INCLINATION (β): seeding β = 0.05 rad makes the arc reach the Moon's out-of-plane offset (max |z| **31,734 km** ≈ the Moon's +32k km) and drops the ZERO-DSM forward perilune to ~**12,800 km** — but the arc is still HYPERBOLIC there (KEm **+0.72**, not captured) and the aggregate DSM is UNCHANGED at ~0.56 km/s.
- **The ~0.5 km/s floor is real, not slow convergence**: 400 iterations only creep the weighted norm 194 → 170 and the aggregate asymptotes to ~0.55 km/s, never toward zero; the zero-DSM forward-propagated trajectory never captures (KEm stays positive as the weighted objective trades residual classes).

**VERDICT (B3.2, both harnesses): ballistic capture NOT achieved at the canonical epoch — the stalled-clean outcome.** But the WSB seam reduces the measured MINIMUM-IMPULSE connection from ~2.8 km/s (encounter seam, `mshoot_harness.js`) to ~**0.5 km/s** (WSB seam + plane tilt, `mshoot_wsb_harness.js`) — no longer LOI-class, but a SMALL deterministic mid-course DSM, i.e. exactly the ispace-style small-DSM BLT regime that real weak-stability-boundary missions actually fly. `physSolveBlt` Step 4 (single-shooting STM-Newton to an L2 gate + KE polish, 40,000 km perilune / KEm +0.37 / `converged:false`) is UNCHANGED and remains the shipped path. An explicit ~0.5 km/s mid-course-DSM capture option is the honest B4-adjacent follow-up feature.

**Critiques (new):**
117. **B3.2's multiple-shooting corrector, with bookkeeping proven clean (trunk boundaries chain at exactly zero; physicality guard; backward round-trip verified), still cannot close the trunk-to-capture seam ballistically at the canonical epoch — but the WSB-seam lever this critique named reduces the minimum implied impulse from ~2.8 km/s (LOI-class) to ~0.5 km/s (small-DSM class).** The first-pass "70.8 km/s stationary point" was a backward-propagation bookkeeping bug (`physPropagateSegment` was forward-only and silently no-oped on tMax < t0), not physics. **RESOLVED (N1c, 2026-07-15):** `physPropagateSegment` now hard-guards tMax < t0 — the default returns an explicit `{error:'backward propagation unsupported'}` (stateF null) instead of a silent no-op, and `opts.backward:true` does real backward integration by time reversal (integrate (r,-v) forward in tau=-t with rails at the true epoch, negate final v — the mshoot_harness.js `propHelioBack` pattern lifted into 386, still all-through-physPropagateSegment). Forward calls are byte-identical (strict `<` guard; N1 golden 80.67 km / 0.2192 m/s / 413 steps unchanged). Gate: `tests/math.test.js` "N1c" block pins both the error shape and a backward round-trip (100k-km 2-day cislunar arc, 0.038 km / 0.0004 m/s, singleFrame and default paths). **The two open levers this critique flagged were then executed (`tests/mshoot_wsb_harness.js`, 2026-07-15) — see the B3.2 WSB-seam subsection above:** (a) a genuinely WSB-compatible capture seed WAS constructed — a retrograde-sense mild ellipse backward-propagates OUT through the L2 neck to 1.15–1.41M km (escape assertion passes), placing the seam in the soft ~1M-km apogee region where the trunk actually flies; (b) alpha WAS freed, and measured DEGENERATE at the anchored near-zero inclination (the α/γ degeneracy of critique 114) — the effective plane DOF is departure inclination β, which when seeded lets the arc reach the Moon's +32k-km z-offset (dropping the zero-DSM perilune to ~12.8k km) but does not remove a ~0.5 km/s in-plane energy/timing floor. What this SETTLES: seam placement matters (encounter seam 2.8 km/s → WSB seam 0.5 km/s), and the residual is a genuine small deterministic DSM, not a solver artifact (a 400-iteration run asymptotes to ~0.55 km/s, not zero; the zero-DSM forward arc never captures, KEm stays +0.7). What remains open: the ~0.5 km/s was demonstrated as an UPPER BOUND on the min-DSM (the corrector creeps rather than proving a KKT minimum), for ONE trunk against ONE capture-ellipse family; whether a fully ballistic (zero-DSM) neighbor exists at a DIFFERENT epoch/quadrant is untested, and the honest possibility stands that the canonical epoch's trunk has no ballistic-capture neighbor (critique 114's measure-thin filament). The B4-adjacent product implication: an explicit ~0.5 km/s mid-course-DSM capture is a legitimate feature (real BLT missions fly small deterministic DSMs), not a failure. (Resolved above: `physPropagateSegment` got BOTH — an explicit error on tMax < t0 by default and opt-in `opts.backward` time-reversal support, N1c.)

## 7ak. MISSION_MODEL_V2 §22 — transfer chains, per-MET budget charging (570-mission-panel.js, 570-mission-lifecycle.js, 572-mission-checks.js, 2026-07-16)

**The complaint (user, 2026-07-15/16):** departure and injection are two burns but were folded into one node — "what we are not focused on enough is being able to have a destination set up without having it all in one node." **Decision (user):** no legacy support, replace outright; chain ownership is LOOSE-WITH-RESOLVE (solver writes the chain once at authoring, then the events are plain user-owned burns; a group-header ↻ re-solve control rewrites them; staleness surfaces through the existing readiness checks, never auto-overwrites).

**What changed.** `missionExecManeuver` (570-mission-panel.js) — the node-map edge-insert / Add-Event-maneuver authoring path, unchanged entry point — now authors a GROUPED CHAIN (`m.groups[gid] = {kind:'transfer', name, route, hadInject, solved}`) instead of a single MNODE, for the two-hop corridor pattern both dev seeds already exercise (LEO→TLC then TLC→destination):

- The corridor-ENTRY edge (e.g. LEO→TLC) stays EXACTLY the unified solved MNODE it always was (`mode:'solved'`, dv auto-derived from `progNmComputeEdgeDv`) — just tagged `chainRole:'depart'` + `groupId`. This is deliberate: 565's `lastTransit` single-pass state machine (the mechanism that lets a corridor-exit edge see the entry edge's own `physSolveNrhoTransfer` solve) is keyed off `_evIsSolvedManeuver(e)` — converting the entry edge to a plain BURN would have silently broken the corridor solve for every edge downstream of it. No new exec path was invented to avoid this; the existing MNODE path is reused verbatim.
- The corridor-EXIT edge (e.g. TLC→destination) — the edge users actually complained about, since its ONE schematic dv silently absorbed a physics-solved mid-course-correction burn and the injection burn with no visibility into either — is split by `_missionChainSplitInject` (pure, 570-mission-panel.js) into up to two real, independently-owned log entries, read STRAIGHT off the leg record `physRebuildMissionTrajectories` (565) already produced (one-source rule; nothing here re-solves physics):
  - an **MCC** member, ONLY when the entry leg carried one (`leg.mccBurn`, `kind:'nrho'` — 5a Stage-2): a plain `BURN`/`burnType:'CUSTOM'` entry (the SAME sanctioned manual-Δv lane §7af's phasing-burn pair already uses — no parallel exec path), `chainRole:'mcc'`, `burnParam` = `|leg.mccBurn.dvVec| × 1000` (m/s), `durationOverride` = `mccBurn.t − departMet` (its own MET spacing, read off the solve, not re-derived).
  - the **INJECTION** member: the ORIGINAL solved MNODE (unchanged accounting path — `_missionApplyManeuver`'s "arrive at destination" orbitState logic fires exactly as it always did), `chainRole:'inject'`, with its `dvOverride` STAMPED from `leg.arrivalBurn`'s own magnitude (replacing the schematic patched-conic estimate with the solver's real insertion cost) and `durationOverride` = the remaining coast after the MCC. Safe to override post-solve: an `'arrival'`-kind leg's physics build never reads `dv_ms` at all (it rides the entry leg's already-flown trajectory — see 565's `fromO.type==='transit'` branch) — this dv value cannot feed back into the physics that computed it.
- A **direct** solved edge (no transit hop — the common case, e.g. LEO→LEO altitude change) produces a single-member chain (`chainRole:'inject'` only) with byte-identical accounting to before — nothing to separate when departure and arrival are the same physical impulse.

**Budget honesty (the actual accounting change).** Before this change, `leg.mccBurn`/`leg.arrivalBurn`'s physics-solved magnitudes were DISPLAY-ONLY (rendered into the stitched trajectory, never charged) — the mission budget only ever charged the corridor-exit edge's schematic `progNmComputeEdgeDv` total, at ONE MET (arrival). After this change: the MCC burn is a real, separately-charged log entry at its own (correctly mid-coast) MET, and the injection burn charges the solver's own insertion magnitude (usually LARGER than the old schematic estimate, since it must absorb the co-orbital, not phase-matched, arrival mismatch — critique 65) instead of the schematic value. **This means total mission ΔV for a corridor transfer goes UP** relative to the pre-chain model, by (MCC + the injection delta) — this is the honesty gain the feature exists to deliver, not a bug. Measured on `devSeedGatewayMission` (canonical epoch, true 9:2 `nrho-nominal`): depart 3,143 m/s (unchanged, fixed solver input) / MCC 519 m/s / injection 2,762 m/s (was ~607 m/s schematic pre-chain) — chain total ≈6,424 m/s vs. the old single-event charge of 3,143 (depart) + 607 (schematic exit) ≈3,750 m/s. **Any golden pinning the old combined total re-pins to the new, larger, honest total, with this section as the documented cause (D6).**

**Flyby (falls out free, no new code).** Deleting the injection member leaves the entry MNODE's own "arrive at toNode" logic as the LAST word on vehicle state — for a corridor entry this is the transit-corridor marker (`orbitState.transit:true`, `destination:<body>`), not the destination orbit. The vehicle is thus honestly left "in transit, never arrived" — exactly the flyby semantics C1 asked for, achieved by NOT executing an event rather than by any new physics. 572-mission-checks.js surfaces this as an INFO finding ("No insertion — flyby"), anchored to the group's first member, gated on `m.groups[gid].hadInject` (stamped true the first time an inject member was authored, so a chain that never had one — e.g. an entry edge alone, awaiting its second hop — is not mistakenly flagged).

**Loose-with-resolve (C2).** `missionChainResolve(id, gid)` (570-mission-panel.js) strips the transient MCC member + stamped overrides, re-recomputes (current authored inputs — departure MET, destination, vehicle state), re-runs `_missionChainSplitInject`, and re-stamps the group's staleness signature — ONE undo capture (the intermediate re-derive recompute is wrapped in the existing `_missionUndoRestoring` guard 575 already respects, the same suppress-then-final-capture pattern `missionExecManeuver`'s own two-pass authoring uses). **Verified** (Gateway seed, shifting the DEPLOY's timing 5 days so the depart MET moves 1,047s→433,048s): staleness fires (`chain-stale` amber) before resolving; after `missionChainResolve`, departure dv holds at 3,143 m/s (fixed solver input, as expected) while MCC (519→485 m/s) and injection (2,762→1,800 m/s) both move to match the new arrival phase geometry — exactly the "departure stable, injection re-solves" behavior C2 asked for.

**Staleness (572-mission-checks.js).** A group-level pass (independent of the per-event suspend-on-red scan, since chain inputs describe authored state, not a replay failure) compares `progNmComputeEdgeDv(g.route.fromNode, g.route.toNode).dv` and the depart member's current `metStart` against the signature stamped at last (re-)solve; either moving by more than 1 (m/s / s) fires `chain-stale` amber. **Documented gap:** upstream VEHICLE MASS changes (e.g. removing a payload before the transfer) are NOT detected — neither signal above depends on mass, only on the two orbit nodes and the departure clock. A future pass wanting full staleness coverage would need to fingerprint the acting vehicle's wet mass at the depart MET too.

**Gate (`tests/math.test.js`, "§22" block, +16 assertions):** pure tests of `_missionChainVecMs` (km/s-vector → m/s-magnitude, matching the 5a gate's own convention) and `_missionChainSplitInject` against synthetic leg fixtures shaped like 565's real `kind:'nrho'`/`kind:'arrival'` records — component sourcing (mcc/inject dv come only from the leg, never re-derived), MET-spacing sums to the entry leg's own `tof_s` exactly (no dropped/duplicated coast), the no-mcc fallback path, and that both components are strictly positive when present. End-to-end chain authoring/flyby/re-solve/staleness are verified in-browser (both dev seeds) rather than in the pure-math gate, since `missionExecManeuver`/`missionChainResolve` need `PROG_ACTIVE_PROGRAM` + DOM, which this harness stubs out.

**Scope, honestly bounded:** the split above only fires for the two-hop corridor pattern (`entryEntry` found + exit leg `kind==='arrival'`) — a hypothetical DIRECT one-hop NRHO edge (skipping the TLC waypoint entirely) is not exercised by either dev seed and stays a single `'inject'` member with unchanged (schematic) accounting; the physics-solved mcc/insertion split for that shape is unimplemented, not merely untested.

## 8. Trade studies (165)

All sweeps evaluate the SAME pure pipeline per point: assemble vehicle (worksheet DOM or `_tsVehicleToBase` adapter) → S1.5 expand → `destOnOrbitDV` for the destination (or the swept parking/inclination/C3 values via the minimal replicas `_tsOnOrbitDVCircular`/`_tsOnOrbitDVEscapeC3`) → `lvMaxPayload` / `lvPerformance`. Metrics: max payload; ΔV margin (at the vehicle's payload input); **payload fraction** = maxPay / tMas(at maxPay) × 100 (tMas from lvPerformance = exact liftoff mass incl. fairing/boosters — not an approximation); total ΔV; total burn time; liftoff T:W = A0/g0. Capability curves are clipped at their interpolated zero-crossing; margin curves keep their negative region. **Tornado**: one-at-a-time ±p% on each authored parameter (per-stage Isp/dry/prop, booster prop/thrust/Isp), re-deriving the S1.5 split per evaluation, bars sorted by |swing|; no parameter interactions.

**C3 sweep**: x-axis is escape characteristic energy C3 (km²/s²) over a user-set range/step (default 0→120), y-axis is the same metric selector as every other sweep, held at the vehicle's OWN parking altitude (the first compared vehicle's parking altitude, shared across the series set, same convention as the inclination sweep's `sharedParkingAlt`). Per point: `_tsOnOrbitDVEscapeC3(c3, parkingAlt)` computes `Vh = sqrt(2·MU/(RE+parkingAlt) + c3)`, `onOrbitDV = (Vh − sqrt(MU/(RE+parkingAlt)))·1000` m/s — a term-for-term replica of `destOnOrbitDV`'s `dest.mode==='escape'` branch (145) with `decl` implicitly equal to `siteLat` (the same default `destOnOrbitDV` applies when `dest.decl` is omitted), which makes the plane-change term `ds = |siteLat|−|decl|−0.5` always negative and drop out identically in both places. `tests/math.test.js` pins the replica equal to `destOnOrbitDV` (float precision) at every escape-mode C3 in `ORBIT_CATEGORIES` (all of which use decl=28.5=siteLat, perigee=185 — exactly the condition the replica assumes), plus a monotonicity check and a Saturn V capability-curve sanity check (payload non-increasing in C3, with at least one strict drop). C3 below the parking orbit's minimum reachable energy (`c3 < −2·MU/(RE+parkingAlt)`) reports `error` and renders as a zero/blank point, same convention as the destination sweep. Caveat: like the destination sweep, this assumes a FIXED parking orbit per vehicle across the whole C3 range — it does not re-optimize parking altitude/inclination per C3 the way a real mission designer might trade departure orbit against injection ΔV.

## 7al. MISSION_MODEL_V2 §20 — OBLIQUITY, real spin axes (360/385/415/565/566/5744, 2026-07-16)

**The reported bug (user-diagnosed 2026-07-15):** plane-matching a KSC (28.5°N) launch to the Moon read UNREACHABLE — the tool compared the site's EQUATOR-referenced 28.5° latitude against the Moon's plane inclination computed in the ECLIPTIC frame (~5.15°), because no body in the model had axial tilt (Earth's equator coincided with the ecliptic). Real KSC launches reach the Moon routinely because the real comparison is against the Moon's inclination TO EARTH'S EQUATOR, which (from the ~5.15° ecliptic figure combined with Earth's 23.44° obliquity) oscillates ~18.3–28.6° with the nodal cycle phase — reachable from 28.5°N at least part of every cycle, with a modest dogleg the rest of the time. Decision (user, weighed against "just inflate the Moon's ephemeris inclination"): tilt the bodies for real, don't corrupt the ephemeris.

**Audit (performed BEFORE editing, per the spec's own risk warning that the double-application bug is this section's primary failure mode).** Every call site where an AUTHORED (user-facing, equator-referenced) `{inclination, lan_deg}` pair becomes a world-frame physics state via `physElementsToState`, or a world-frame orbit normal becomes a displayed inc/lan:

| # | Site | Authored source | Seam applied? |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | `physAimBurnState` (565-physics-targeting.js) — generic ring-state builder, called by damn near every solver in 565/5744/5745 | `incRad`/`raanRad` args, always populated from `.inclination`/`.lan_deg` upstream | **NOT edited directly** — see below, seam applied one level up instead |
| 2 | `physShootLegAim` (565-physics-targeting.js) — the Apollo-style Earth→Moon/interplanetary leg shooter (`physAimBurnState` calls at the θ-grid scan + sol1/sol2/sol3), plus its RAAN-solve cone-axis equation (`mHat`/a,b,c) | `fromOrbit.inclination`/`.lan_deg` | **YES** — this section's actual fix locus |
| 3 | `physShootLegAim`'s `toAuthoredPlane` (arrival-plane target) | `toOrbit.inclination`/`.lan_deg` | **YES** |
| 4 | `v2BuildShadow`'s LAUNCH/DEPLOY anchor reconstruction (566-mission-state-v2.js ~line 100) | `os.inclination`/`os.lan` | **YES** |
| 5 | `progIdealParkingOrbit` (415) — interplanetary launch-planner DLA/LAN | `vInfVec` (world, from `progDepartVinf`) rotated into `fromBody`'s equator frame BEFORE the existing DLA algebra runs unchanged | **YES** |
| 6 | `progMoonPlaneAt` (415) — Moon's instantaneous plane for "match plane" | Moon's geocentric `r×v` (world/ecliptic) rotated to Earth-equator via `progWorldToEqElements` on the WAY OUT | **YES** |
| 7 | `565-physics-nrho.js` (Gateway/NRHO shooter — ~15 `physAimBurnState` call sites, its own parallel RAAN-solve cone equation) | `.inclination`/`.lan_deg` | **NOT DONE — scope cut, see below** |
| 8 | `565-physics-blt.js` (BLT incremental targeting, §21 B3) | `setup.incRad`/`.raan0` | **NOT DONE — scope cut** |
| 9 | `565-physics-mission.js` (3 sites: ~line 233 solved-node burn, ~458 `_nmMatchOrbitToNode`, ~781 arrival-burn construction) | `.inclination`/`.lan_deg` | **NOT DONE — scope cut** |
| 10 | `5744-trajectory-eventnodes.js` / `5745-maneuver-gizmo-hover.js` / `5745-maneuver-gizmo-drag.js` (ring-position rendering for event nodes / gizmo hover-ball / drag) | `.inclination`/`.lan`/`.lan_deg` | **NOT DONE — scope cut (rendering only)** |

**Why sites 7–10 are an explicit, honest scope cut, not an oversight.** The original plan was to push the eq→world rotation into `physAimBurnState` itself (site 1) so every downstream caller (7–10) would be fixed for free, one choke point. That was abandoned after discovering `565-physics-nrho.js` runs ITS OWN parallel RAAN-solve cone-axis equation (mirroring the exact `a·sinΩ+b·cosΩ=c` pattern this section fixes in `physShootLegAim`) — if that equation's own `mHat`-style target vector is ALSO left in world coordinates (not yet audited in the time available), rotating `physAimBurnState`'s inputs centrally would silently double-transform the NRHO path's already-converged geometry (the exact failure mode the spec warns about), corrupting the Gateway seed with no test coverage to catch it. Given the budget for this pass, the seam was instead applied surgically at sites 2–6 (the actual reported-bug locus: Apollo-style Moon/interplanetary leg targeting + the launch-planner's advisory numbers), leaving the NRHO/BLT/gizmo-rendering paths on their PRE-EXISTING (world≈equator) approximation — unchanged behavior, not a new bug. **Follow-on work**, tracked here rather than silently dropped: audit `565-physics-nrho.js`'s cone-axis equation the same way §7al did for `physShootLegAim`, then either (a) fix it the same way, or (b) once BOTH are confirmed frame-consistent, migrate the seam down into `physAimBurnState` itself and delete the per-caller duplication.

**The transform itself** (`physEqBasis`, `progEqToWorldElements`, `progWorldToEqElements` — 385-physics-core.js) is a pure orthonormal change of basis: `zEq` = the body's pole (`physBodyPoleAt`, 360, PROG_BODY_POLES table — obliquity+node per body, convention `pole = [sin(node)sin(obliquity), −cos(node)sin(obliquity), cos(obliquity)]`, the SAME formula family as the pre-existing orbit-normal convention used all over 565); `xEq` = `ẑ_world × zEq` normalized (the equator's ascending node on the ecliptic — for Earth with node=0 this is exactly `ẑ_world`'s cross with the tilted pole, which reduces to the standard "rotate about x by obliquity" astronomy convention, and `xEq` = world +x exactly, matching the pre-§20 zero-tilt behavior at the reference epoch by construction); `yEq` completes the right-handed set. Only `(inc, raan)` are rotated — `argp`/`nu` are measured from the orbit's own ascending node (a direction intrinsic to the orbit plane), so they are frame-invariant under a pure re-basing of the plane's orientation and are deliberately left untouched. Round-trips to machine precision by construction (pure basis change) — gate-pinned across 7 (inc, lan) pairs plus the untilted-body (Jupiter, absent from the table) identity case.

**`physShootLegAim`'s specific fix** (site 2): `incRad` stays EQUATORIAL throughout the function (unchanged meaning/value — it's the ring's inclination MAGNITUDE, invariant under re-basing). The RAAN-solve cone-axis equation (targeting the Moon's instantaneous direction `mHat`) previously treated `mHat`'s WORLD components directly against a cone whose axis was implicitly world-z (the pre-§20 bug: assumes the ring's inclination cone is centered on the ecliptic pole) — now `mHat` is rotated into the equatorial basis (`physEqBasis(fromBody)`) FIRST, so the cone is correctly centered on Earth's real pole. The solved (or authored) `(incRad, raan)` pair, still equatorial, is rotated to world via `progEqToWorldElements` at the ONE place a ring actually becomes a state (`aimBurnEq`, wrapping `physAimBurnState`) — applied exactly once, verified by inspection (no other call in this function reaches `physElementsToState`).

**Measured impact — Apollo seed (`devSeedApolloMission({force:true})`, LEO→TLC leg, canonical epoch, fixed TLI |Δv|=3,143 m/s):** pre-fix miss (world-frame cone bug) = 129,221 km; post-fix miss (equator-frame cone, physically correct) = 216,917 km — WORSE, and both non-convergent (`converged:false`). This is an honest, measured >20% move, reported per the spec's own instruction rather than silently re-pinned, WITH cause: this leg was already non-convergent before §20 (a pre-existing "capture stage honestly open" gap per the B3/B3.1 commit, not something this section broke) — the fixed-|Δv| TLI's true required geometry, now correctly referenced to Earth's REAL tilted pole instead of the ecliptic, needs a larger plane change than the schematic 3,143 m/s burn can deliver at this specific departure epoch/theta-grid seed; the solver reports that honestly (`converged:false`) both before and after. **Not a regression of a working feature** — verified by reverting this file alone (`git stash`) and re-measuring: the pre-fix number was ALSO non-convergent, just by a smaller (still wrong-cone-axis) margin. **Gateway seed unaffected** (confirmed in-browser): `devSeedGatewayMission({force:true})` legs `{kind:'nrho', converged:true}` + `{kind:'arrival', converged:true}` — bit-for-bit the same as before this section, because `565-physics-nrho.js` (site 7) was deliberately left untouched (see the scope-cut note above).

**Measured impact — the actual user acceptance test** (`progResolvePlaneTarget('Moon', epoch, 0, 28.5)`): at the program's default epoch (JD 2461230.5), Moon inc-to-equator = 19.02°, penalty from a 28.5° site = 9.48° (a real, single-digit-degree launch-window dogleg). Pre-fix this same call would have compared 28.5° against the ~5.15° ECLIPTIC figure — a ~23° gap reading as essentially unreachable on every date. A 19-year (saros-scale) sweep (76 samples, tests/math.test.js "S20" block) confirms: inc-to-equator stays within [18.30°, 28.60°] on every sample (0 out-of-band); the plane-match is genuinely reachable (`unreachable:false`) on 5/76 samples and, on the rest, the dogleg penalty stays under 12° (max measured 10.20°, avg 5.23°) — a world away from the pre-fix ~23° gap on literally every date. **Honest note on "majority reachable":** this is NOT a majority of dates, and that is physically correct, not a shortcoming — exact coplanar match with the Moon's instantaneous plane is a genuine once-per-nodal-cycle constraint (this is WHY real Apollo/Artemis missions have monthly launch windows, not daily ones); the bug this section fixes is "reads unreachable with a huge, physically-wrong gap on EVERY date" → "reads reachable with zero penalty on the dates real missions actually launch, and a small, physically real dogleg on the rest."

**O2 — rendering (deliberately narrow scope this pass).** Saturn's ring-plane tilt (`_trajRingPlaneBasis`, 5744-trajectory-globe.js) now SOURCES its obliquity/node from `PROG_BODY_POLES` (360) instead of its own local `_TRAJ_RING_OBLIQUITY_DEG` constant — mechanical unification, zero behavior change for Saturn (node=0 in the table reduces to the exact pre-existing formula; gate-pinned: the ring basis's cross product equals `physBodyPoleAt('Saturn')` exactly). **Full globe-tilt rendering (Earth's spin axis rendering visibly tilted, launch-site markers/surface features riding the real pole, `body-fixed` frame basis using the real pole instead of world-z) is EXPLICITLY DESCOPED this pass** — a deliberate, budget-driven cut, not an oversight: `_trajProjectVec`/the surface-rendering pipeline was the site of a genuine, hard-to-diagnose chirality regression as recently as 2026-07-11 (§7o), and reworking the globe-spin/body-fixed-frame math to rotate about a tilted pole (rather than the assumed world-z spin axis every consumer of `_trajBodySpinAngle`/`_trajLatLonUnit`/`_trajFrameBasisAt('body-fixed',...)` currently shares) touches that exact same high-risk shared seam, with no spare budget in this pass to re-derive and re-verify its screen-space goldens the way §7o did. Tracked as follow-on work, not silently dropped.

**Critique 72 (new).** The pole table (`PROG_BODY_POLES`, 360) is STATIC v1 — no precession, for any body. Real planetary poles precess (Earth's ~26,000-year axial precession; the Moon's pole precesses on an 18.6-year cycle tied to its orbital nodal regression, the Cassini-state coupling). The Moon's 6.68° obliquity-to-ecliptic figure in the table is this project's chosen v1 constant for the lunar SPIN axis (distinct from, and not to be confused with, the Moon's ORBIT inclination to the ecliptic, ~5.145°, which `progMoonPlaneAt` derives independently from the live ephemeris `r×v` — the table entry is currently unused by any Moon-as-`fromBody`/`dest` consumer in this pass's scope-cut list, since sites 2–6 only exercise Earth's entry; it exists for forward-compatibility with a future lunar-surface/globe-rendering consumer). Mars/Saturn `node_deg=0` is an approximation (real values not sourced) in the same "hand-tuned constant, undocumented provenance" family as critiques 7/24/51/52/53/57.

## 9. Regression protection

`tests/math.test.js` (run by every `python build.py`): Tsiolkovsky hand values, lvPerformance invariants + golden snapshot, booster single-vs-array equivalence, lvMaxPayload convergence, `destOnOrbitDV` pinned to browser-captured `calculate()` outputs (GTO / plane change / escape), `_tsOnOrbitDVEscapeC3` (the trade-study C3-sweep replica) pinned equal to `destOnOrbitDV`'s escape branch at every escape-mode C3 in `ORBIT_CATEGORIES` plus monotonicity and a Saturn V capability-curve sanity, S1.5 split conservation + expanded-golden + divergence proof, preset name-resolution integrity, and (since 2026-07-14, §7q) MISSION_MODEL_V2 Phase 1 shadow-state shape/synthetic-mission tests (launch-anchor state reconstruction, solved+manual MNODE dv reconciliation, D6 margin math, separation owner-forking), plus (§7r) Phase 3 T1/T2/T3 catalog/binding/terminology tests and a T4 pure D1 re-solve-locality test (synthetic 4-dwell chain A-B-C-D: editing node B's altitude re-solves the delivering edge A→B and the departing edge B→C, edge C→D two hops away is untouched). **Changing physics intentionally = re-capture goldens in the same commit, with a dated comment.**

---

## Critiques (known weaknesses, roughly by severity)

**Ascent model**
1. K3/K4/T3s/Tmix are fitted empirical constants of unstated provenance and envelope. They validate well against 1960s–present orbital LVs (Saturn V, F9 land within a few % of published capability), but nothing warns the user when a design leaves the fit's envelope (TWR ≪ 1.2 at liftoff, very lofted or depressed trajectories, air-launch).
2. Single static Isp per stage/engine — no altitude dependence. First-stage numbers are whatever the data author blended by hand; two vehicles with identical physics but different Isp-averaging conventions aren't comparable at the few-percent level.
3. The booster integrator blends Isp correctly per interval, but the Townsend penalty is computed from aggregate `A0`/`Isp_avg`/`Ta` — parallel-staged vehicles get a serial-equivalent loss estimate.
4. `Ta`'s fractional-stage interpolation uses an exponential prop-fraction heuristic, not the actual time-to-Vcirc within the stage.

**Destination ΔV**
5. Plane-change logic assumes initial inclination = |site latitude| and charges nothing for inclinations above latitude (real azimuth constraints and range-safety corridors can make those expensive). Retrograde/SSO targets are only "charged" via lost rotation credit.
6. The combined-plane-change-at-apo optimization triggers on an arbitrary `apogee > 10,000 km` threshold; elliptical targets never get the combined treatment (plane change added scalar, not vectorially).
7. Tolerance constants (5 km "circular", 50 km "same perigee", 0.5° plane-change deadband) are unlabeled magic numbers.
8. Escape declination penalty is a crude great-circle plane change at parking speed; no DLA/azimuth interaction.

**S1.5**
9. BECO located on stage-only mass (payload ignored) — the "< 5 %" error claim is asserted, not tested, outside Atlas-class proportions.
10. Phase-1 blend (when used) is static; booster-pack jettison is instantaneous with no thrust decay, and `s15_beco_twr` defaults to 1.2 regardless of vehicle.
10a. The blend's sustainer term uses `s15_sust_isp`, which the library stores as the VACUUM value (it primarily serves Phase 2) — so the Phase-1 blend is optimistic at low altitude, where the sustainer's real SL Isp is far worse (LR-105: ~215 s SL vs 309 s vac). With Atlas data the blend RAISED Ph.1 Isp (282 → 285.5 s) rather than lowering it. To make the blend represent liftoff conditions you'd need a separate sea-level sustainer Isp field, or the user must fold the pessimism into `s15_boost_isp`. Known and accepted for now.

**Mission / node map**
11. Two ascent models coexist: Townsend (when LV performance data exists) vs `Vcirc + fixed-loss table`. A mission can silently switch fidelity depending on how the vehicle was created. The loss table itself (Earth 1550 etc.) is a rough constant independent of TWR or target altitude.
12. LOI/TEI patched-conic underestimates (~10 %, documented); TEI = LOI symmetry is a simplification; no free-return or finite-burn losses anywhere.
13. Interplanetary transfers: circular coplanar planets, no plane change, no arrival-timing coupling between the node map ΔVs and the porkchop's Lambert solutions — the two subsystems can disagree about the same trip.
14. Docking tolerance windows (1 km, 0.1°, 1° LAN) are arbitrary, and `epoch`/phasing is entirely ignored — co-orbital ≠ co-located.
15. Lunar/Mars ascent = scaled constants; no TWR sensitivity, no plane targeting from launch site latitude on those bodies.
16. Boiloff: constant fractional rate, insulation as a linear multiplier in the exponent; no tank thermal state, no ullage/pressurization losses. Rate is constant over the whole coast regardless of duration — a 261-day cislunar loiter and a 3-day one lose mass on the exact same exponential curve, with no allowance for a tank's fill fraction changing its effective surface-to-volume ratio over time.
16f. Propellant-type provenance is asymmetric and easy to get wrong silently: launch-vehicle stages always get a valid cryo/storable type (Isp-inferred by `progVehicleDefToLiveStages`), so they DO boil off; spacecraft stage definitions default to `propType: 'MMH/NTO'`, which is not a registered key in `PROG_PROPELLANT_TYPES` — an unedited spacecraft stage therefore boils off NOTHING, not because MMH/NTO is storable (it correctly would be, if modeled) but because the lookup silently misses. The net effect (storable-by-default) happens to be physically reasonable for a real MMH/NTO service module, but it's an accident of naming, not a deliberate default — a future propellant type added to the registry under a different key would need to also update this default, and nothing enforces the two stay in sync.
16g. Boiloff during LAUNCH ascent is modeled (applied, not skipped) but negligible by construction — ascent burn times are minutes, and the exponential over minutes is indistinguishable from zero at any realistic cryo rate. Documented rather than special-cased to avoid a silent gap in the "every clock advance boils off" invariant.

**Mission time (T1)**
16a. Hohmann-model TOF ≠ real trajectories: the translunar half-ellipse estimate lands ~5 days vs. Apollo's actual ~3-day free-return trajectory — the model doesn't know about faster non-Hohmann transfers, and overrides are the intended escape hatch for any mission that needs an accurate date.
16b. All burns (BURN and MANEUVER) are treated as impulsive/instantaneous in the clock — only the COAST/transfer between them accumulates time. A long low-thrust burn would in reality itself take meaningful time; not modeled.
16c. Interplanetary TOF ignores launch-window phasing (planets aren't actually where a Hohmann departure needs them) unless the mission has a specific porkchop-selected Lambert window (`PROG_ACTIVE_PROGRAM.launchWindow`) — absent that, the number is a generic Hohmann estimate, not a real date.
16d. `durationOverride`/`dvOverride` intentionally bypass the physics by design — they exist so the user can substitute a known real value (e.g. actual Apollo TOF/ΔV) for the model's estimate. The UI badges overridden events "(custom)" so this is never silently confused with the computed value, but nothing prevents an override that's physically inconsistent with the vehicle's actual propellant/ΔV budget beyond the normal margin checks.
16e. Transit-corridor convention: a coast is charged once, on the leg EXITING the corridor node (TLC→LLO carries the ~5 d translunar TOF; LEO→TLC is the impulsive injection, 0 s). Found the hard way: the first implementation charged both legs and doubled every corridor transit.

**Boiloff on clock advancement (T2)**
16h. Boiloff is applied to EVERY live vehicle whenever ANY vehicle's clock-advancing event fires — a docked depot idling in LEO boils off in step with the vehicle actually coasting to the Moon, which is physically correct (time passes for everyone), but it means a mission with many idle vehicles pays the boiloff cost once per clock-advancing event rather than once per vehicle-day, which is the same total mass loss but a different (equivalent) computational path worth noting for anyone auditing `applyMissionBoiloff` in `missionRecompute` (570).
16i. The readiness check's cumulative-boiloff percentage (572, `boiloff-losses`) divides by "initial propellant capacity," which is captured at LAUNCH/DEPLOY time (`fv._initialPropCap`). A vehicle born mid-mission from a SEPARATE or DOCK (no LAUNCH/DEPLOY of its own) has no cached initial value; the check falls back to that vehicle's CURRENT capacity at the moment boiloff is first detected, which understates the percentage for any vehicle whose stages were combined after already losing propellant to burns — a known, documented approximation, not a hard guarantee.

**Body kinematics (C1a) — RETIRED BY R1 (2026-07-09)**
16j. ~~No ephemeris~~ — RESOLVED: R1 rails are the JPL approximate mean elements evaluated at real absolute time (`epochJD`); `t=0` is a calendar date.
16k. ~~Perfectly circular orbits~~ — RESOLVED: real eccentricities (Mercury e=0.2056, Moon e=0.0549) with elliptical speed variation (analytic Ė-form velocity).
16l. ~~Everything coplanar~~ — RESOLVED: bodies have real inclinations and 3D states (R1), the renderer is a 3D camera over true geometry (R2), and vessel states carry their authored inclination (R3, §7h). Remaining plane fictions are the Ω=0 convention (critique 49) and the schematic plane-change accounting (critique 50).

**Trade studies**
17. Inclination/altitude sweeps use the minimal circular-orbit ΔV replica (clearly labeled) — fine for trends, not for elliptical or escape comparisons.
18. Tornado is one-at-a-time and symmetric: no parameter interactions, no asymmetric sensitivities (e.g. Isp loss usually hurts more than gain helps near margin limits).
18a. The C3 sweep holds parking altitude FIXED across the whole C3 range (the first compared vehicle's own parking altitude) — a real mission would often trade a higher parking orbit for a cheaper injection at high C3, or a lower one for cheap low-C3 escapes; the sweep shows neither optimization, only "this vehicle's chosen parking orbit, escaping at varying energy." It also silently assumes decl=siteLat (no plane-change penalty), matching `destOnOrbitDV`'s own default when a destination doesn't specify a departure declination — real interplanetary departures generally do care about declination-of-launch-asymptote (DLA) vs. site latitude, which this sweep (like the destination table's escape entries) does not charge for.

**Trajectory view LOD + mission-state rendering (C2)**
21. Vehicle-on-arc position (`_trajArcPointAt` driven by `_trajLegPathFraction`) is uniform PATH-LENGTH-ANGLE progress along the drawn half-ellipse, not true orbital motion — real vehicles move faster near periapsis (non-uniform true-anomaly rate under Kepler's second law), so the dot's on-screen speed is constant while a real vehicle's isn't. Schematic "roughly here" indicator only. — **Retired for physics-backed legs (P3, 2026-07-09)**: legs with a propagated side-table record place the dot by interpolating the propagated samples at viewTime (`_trajPolylinePointAt`, §7f), which IS Kepler-true to sample resolution; the linear-fraction schematic remains only for legs without physics (physics off, degenerate/unpropagated legs).
22. The Moon-frame patched-conic seam fallback (`_trajLocalRadius`'s `max(2×bodyR, 0.5×mo.r)` departure radius for a transit arriving at its own destination frame) is an arbitrary rendering placeholder, not a Hill-sphere/SOI calculation — it exists purely so the arc has SOME finite departure point to draw from in the destination's own frame; the radius value carries no physical meaning (it's tuned to clear the transferArc LOD window's px floor across camera anchors, not to model the real hyperbolic approach geometry).
23. Orbit-ring "history" dimming only tracks vehicle-LEVEL EXPEND events (`expendMetByOwner`, keyed by originKey) — a stage-level EXPEND (partial jettison) doesn't retire the ring's owner, so a ring whose vehicle survives via a remaining stage never dims even if the specific stage that occupied that orbit is long gone.
24. The zone-of-influence fade-in threshold (~30px neighborhood extent) and the LOD ramp's "20% of `lo`" cap are both hand-tuned constants with no derivation beyond "looked right in testing" — same category as the pre-existing magic-number critiques elsewhere in this document (see critique 7).
25. ~~Planet-phase calibration is per-mission fiction~~ — RETIRED BY R1 (2026-07-09): the calibration and its second-leg ghost rendering were deleted with the schematic rails; all missions and the porkchop plotter now share the ONE real ephemeris. The trade: a schematic Hohmann arc at an out-of-window MET now honestly fails to reach its planet (converged:false, ghost marker at the true arrival position) instead of being bent to connect.
26. **Zone-of-influence gate blocks mission content at moonless-planet camera anchors** (found while implementing planet-phase calibration, not introduced by it — pre-existing): `_trajWorldSVG`'s per-planet `zoiAlpha` computation is `Math.max(neighborhoodExtentPx, _trajBodyNeighborhoodPx(body, zoom))` against the `zoneOfInfluence` window's 30px floor, but `neighborhoodExtentPx` is a fixed 6px clamp by construction of `_trajBodyPxR` (not derived from actual mission-content extent) and `_trajBodyNeighborhoodPx` only considers a body's MOONS. A moonless planet (Mars, Venus, Mercury, etc.) therefore NEVER opens its own zone-of-influence at any zoom, so its transfer arcs and orbit rings never render — verified in-browser (a Mars-orbit mission leg's arc stayed invisible with the camera anchored directly on Mars, at every tested `cam.wKm`; content only became visible after monkeypatching `_trajBodyNeighborhoodPx` in a debug session, confirming the extraction/geometry itself is correct and only the LOD gate is at fault). This is a real user-facing gap (the planet focus-bar button flies exactly to this camera state) — flagged as a follow-up task, not fixed here (out of this change's scope: `574-trajectory-view.js`'s general LOD architecture, not the calibration feature itself). — FIXED (2026-07-09): the ZOI gate and the fly-to fit now share one neighborhood definition (_trajBodyNeighborhoodKm: moon rings, mission-content extent, 4R floor), so any camera that frames content opens the gate that draws it.

27. **Physics core rails — R1 update**: ~~circular and coplanar~~ — rails are now real mean-element ephemerides (§7a); the remaining rail-fidelity gaps are the mean-element approximations themselves (critiques 41–43). `physKeplerPropagate` still returns `null` on Newton non-convergence (near-parabolic + extreme dt corners) — every consumer MUST handle the null rather than assume a state.
28. **Integrator (P1, 386) accuracy is schematic-grade, not ephemeris-grade**: 2nd-order leapfrog at 360 steps/orbit accumulates ~0.3 km/orbit of phase error, and Jacobi fidelity is ~1e-3 on high-e lunar encounters — fine for illustrating free returns and flybys, NOT for real trajectory design. Close-approach accuracy also depends on the step ladder catching the encounter: a vessel screaming through the Moon's SOI at a coarse cruise step takes its first fine step only AFTER the SOI event is detected (transition location error up to one coarse step). The rails' small dynamical inconsistency is still absorbed as a permanent perturbation — R1 flavor: the Moon's mean-element rate L̇ (solar-perturbed, 27.32 d) differs ~0.5% from the two-body rate √(μE/a³) the integrator's gravity field implies, so a body's rail motion and the field it generates don't exactly agree (same class as the old ω²d³ vs μ mismatch, now smaller and better justified).
29. **SOI transitions are detected, not bisected, in P1**: the handoff happens at the first step-end INSIDE the new sphere rather than at the exact boundary crossing (physFindEventTime exists but isn't wired into the transition path yet) — a deliberate simplification; revisit if P4 shooter convergence turns out to be sensitive to the patch point.
30. **P2 analytic phasing is aim, not targeting**: `physSolveNodeBurn` places the burn point assuming a pure Hohmann half-ellipse in a two-body field, then propagates in the FULL perturbed field — lunar gravity bends the real trajectory, so the propagated leg generally arrives near, not at, the Moon (recorded as SOI-entry `converged` metadata; a clean miss is legal and expected until P4's differential corrector). Interplanetary phasing is cruder still: the LEO burn point is placed at a schematic heliocentric angle with no ejection-asymptote geometry (that arrives in P3's `physEscapeGeometry`).
31. **MNODE honesty gap (P2)**: an MNODE burns real propellant through the rocket equation but advances NO orbit state — the node-map orbit and the physics-side trajectory diverge silently after any MNODE. The band/orbit views keep showing the pre-burn orbit. P3 (render from side-table) and P4 (authoring UI + shooter) close this; until then MNODE is a console-only accounting event.
32. **P2 physics TOF is stale-by-one**: the duration a replay uses comes from the PREVIOUS rebuild's legs, reconciled by at most one extra recompute pass per edit. If an edit changes an upstream duration, downstream legs fall back to schematic TOF for that single pass (their MET signature no longer matches) — momentarily inconsistent durations inside one recompute cycle are possible by design, never persisted.
33. **P2 delivered-ΔV coupling through boiloff**: because physics TOF changes coast durations and boiloff is time-dependent, enabling physics shifts *delivered* ΔV/propellant on cryo missions slightly (Apollo seed: +1 m/s / +8 kg on LOI) even though *required* ΔV is byte-identical. Anyone diffing mission totals across the P2 boundary must compare required, not delivered, figures.
34. **P3 spur orientation is schematic, not solved (574/385)**: the departure spur's asymptote is aligned with the departure body's heliocentric velocity direction (prograde/retrograde by destination), NOT with the ejection asymptote a Lambert/porkchop solution would demand — and the capture spur is an explicit mirror (incoming asymptote anti-parallel to a schematic arrival v∞ ≈ ∓v_planet) with no hyperbolic timing at all. The spur's burn point on the parking ring therefore generally DISAGREES with the physics leg's analytically-phased burn point (§7e) and with the heliocentric arc's departure end — they are separate schematic layers that agree in spirit, not in angle. P4's shooter is the fix.
35. **P3 polyline is decimated and clip-approximate**: drawn physics legs use ≤256 integrator samples (frame changes preserved), linearly interpolated for the vehicle dot — fine visually, but tight flyby curvature between samples is chorded, and the clip at the arrival leg's `metArrive` trusts the mission's replayed duration rather than re-locating the periapsis event, so up to one sample interval of post-arrival trajectory can render (or be cut) at the seam. (The second half of this critique — polylines rendered against calibrated positions while propagation used uncalibrated rails — is RESOLVED by R1: there is one position source and no calibration.)

36. **P4 shooter targets closest approach, not arrival state**: the corrector drives the trajectory's closest approach to the destination to the destination-orbit radius — it does NOT control the arrival v∞, approach direction, or capture geometry, so the arrival burn's ΔV (patched-conic, §6) and the propagated approach can be mutually inconsistent (a trajectory arriving retrograde is charged the same LOI as a prograde one). The 2-DOF timing component (0.5 km/s scale factor) is a hand-tuned conditioning constant, same category as critique 7/24.
37. **P4 MNODE honesty gap, narrowed but not closed**: the MNODE trajectory is propagated and drawn (closing critique 31's "advances NO orbit state" render half), but the NODE-MAP orbit still doesn't change — the band view and downstream maneuvers keep planning from the pre-burn orbit while the trajectory view shows the post-burn ballistic path. Also the burn is placed on the MEAN-altitude circular reconstruction at θ = n·MET — an elliptic parking orbit's true position at that MET is not honored (same circularization simplification as §7e's solved burns).
38. ~~P4 free-return solve leans on rotational symmetry~~ — **REWORKED (R3, 2026-07-10)**: the rotated-golden seed is replaced by the deterministic seed scan of §7h (the rotation shortcut could not seed an inclined departure at all — measured: the R1 golden never re-enters the return band from a 28.5° ring). The [30, 500] km band check on the FINAL propagation still guards against a bad seed shipping a non-returning "free return"; window-limited coverage and the scan's tuned constants are critique 51.
39. **P4 shooter cache is keyed on authored identity only**: `fromNode|toNode|met|dv` — a change to the gravitating-body set, integrator constants, rails, or EPOCH invalidates nothing (module-lifetime cache, cleared on reload). Deliberate for code-level constants; note the new epoch wrinkle: an epochJD edit mid-session (no UI yet, console only) would serve stale aims until reload.

**Real ephemeris rails (R1, 2026-07-09)**
40. **Rings are still schematic circles; glyphs ride real orbits**: the top-down view draws heliocentric and moon rings at the old mean radii (`PROG_HELIO_R`/`PROG_MOON_ORBITS.r`) while body glyphs sit at real element-evaluated positions — an eccentric planet visibly sits off its ring (Mercury: up to ~9.1e6 km ≈ 24 px at Mercury-fit zoom; measured 2026-07-09). Stated in the view footer; R2 replaces rings with true sampled orbits. — **RESOLVED (2026-07-09, R2)**: rings sample the real elements (§7f-R2); Mercury's glyph measured 1.9 px from its 120-point orbit polyline (vertex quantization, on-curve ≈ 0).
46. ~~R2 mission-orbit rings assume Ω = ω = 0~~ — **RESOLVED for FLOWN rings (R3.1, 2026-07-10, §7i) and AUTHORED rings (R3.2, 2026-07-10, §7j)**: a ring whose (body, peri, apo) matches a converged physics leg's departure or arrival now draws with that leg's ACTUAL plane (`rec.elements`, tooltip "plane from flight") instead of the Ω=ω=0 convention — the drawn ring and the physics that created it are one object for the departure case (literally, by construction) and agree to finite-difference accuracy for the arrival case. R3.2 adds a tier ABOVE that: an orbit spec that explicitly authors `lan_deg`/`argp_deg` wins outright over both flight-derived and default, tooltip "Ω,ω authored" — factored through the pure `_trajRingOrientationFor(rec)` resolver so the precedence is one function, gate-tested directly. The Ω=ω=0 convention survives only for rings with NEITHER physics parentage NOR an authored orientation (schematic-only legs, unauthored define-an-orbit previews, orphan snapshots) — those still draw Ω=ω=0, annotated as before. Two same-inclination orbits launched at different times still can't be distinguished if neither is physics-backed NOR authored.
47. **R2 painter sorting is per-body, not per-primitive**: a body's glyph and ALL its embedded content share one depth (the body center), and orbit rings always draw behind glyphs. Exact occlusion for ring-vs-glyph and content-vs-content across bodies is not modeled — visible only in contrived tilted alignments; a full z-sorted primitive pipeline is not worth the complexity at SVG's layering granularity.
48. **R2 rotate/tilt interaction constants are hand-tuned**: 0.008 rad/px drag rate, 33 ms rotate-render throttle, 5° minimum elevation, grid alpha ∝ sin(tilt) capped 0.35 — same magic-number category as critiques 7/24.
41. **Moon is mean-elements-only**: no evection (±1.27°), variation (±0.66°), annual equation, or parallactic inequality — real lunar position error up to ~2° in longitude (≈14,000 km). Fine for mission schematics; not an ephemeris for real ops. Same class: planet elements are the Standish 1800–2050 approximation (arcminute-level), not DE-series.
42. **Porkchop Lambert is still 2D**: real ephemeris positions are PROJECTED to the ecliptic (z dropped) because `progLambert2D` is planar — C3 error is small for low-inclination targets (Mars 1.85°, Venus 3.39°; worst-case few % near nodes) but grows for high-i targets if ever added.
43. **Titan's plane is wrong on purpose**: modeled circular in SATURN'S ORBITAL plane; the real Titan orbits near Saturn's equatorial plane (~26.7° tilt from orbital). Positions are schematic for any Titan mission; fix when a Saturn-system mission actually matters.
44. ~~Vessels are still coplanar (z = 0) against inclined targets~~ — **RESOLVED (2026-07-10, R3)**: parking orbits, solved burns, MNODE burns and the free-return solve all live in the orbit's authored plane (§7h), the shooter gained the out-of-plane yaw DOF, and moon-leg acceptance tightened to min(SOI/3, 25,000 km) with a 5,000 km solver tolerance. Residual gaps live on as critiques 49/50: the plane is authored-inclination-with-Ω=0 (a convention, not a launch-time RAAN), and the schematic ΔV engine's plane-change accounting is unchanged.
45. **Epoch has no UI and no window-coast coupling**: epochJD defaults to 2026-07-09 and is persisted, but only editable via console; and selecting a porkchop window changes a leg's TOF, not its departure MET — reaching the real window still requires the user to author a COAST to the window's dep_day (verified: Mars leg closest approach improves 61e6 → 3e6 km when a 118 d coast is authored to the real window).

**3D physics coherence (R3, 2026-07-10)**
49. **Ω = 0 is a convention on every vessel plane — EXCEPT moon-leg departures/arrivals (solved/derived, §7h/§7i), orbits that AUTHOR their own plane (R3.2, §7j), and now orbits whose plane is AUTHORED FROM A LAUNCH SITE + TIME (R6.3, §7n) — RESOLVED for the "real missions pin RAAN at launch time" gap this critique originally raised**: an orbit spec can now carry explicit `lan_deg`/`argp_deg` (§7j direct authoring, unchanged), and when it does, `physShootLegAim`'s departure-plane solve (§7h) is SKIPPED — the authored plane is fixed geometry, not a target, and a fixed-|Δv| leg that can't reach it from that exact plane honestly fails rather than being silently re-aimed onto a different, reachable plane. R6.3 adds a physically-motivated SOURCE for that same `lan_deg` field: `progLaunchRaanFor` (360) derives Ω from a launch site's lat/lon and an authored launch time via standard spherical trig, so a user no longer has to type a bare RAAN number to get a launch-time-consistent plane — the LAUNCH card computes and writes it, annotated "(from launch time)" so it's visibly derived rather than hand-picked. Arrival targets that author a plane get a dedicated plane-alignment residual (§7j) instead of the R3 yaw DOF's generic out-of-plane miss. **The drawn-ring-vs-physics-plane split is CLOSED for flown legs (R3.1, §7i) and for authored orbits (R3.2/R6.3, §7j/§7n)**: the departure/arrival plane the corrector actually flew is recorded on the leg (`departElements`/`arrivalElements`) and consumed by the ring renderer, and an authored plane (typed or launch-time-derived) wins over both. What remains: (a) UNFLOWN, UNAUTHORED rings (no converged leg, no `lan_deg`, or physics off) still draw Ω=ω=0 by convention; (b) free-return windows are STILL solved against the conventional Ω=0 departure plane even when the active vehicle's current orbit has an authored plane — R3.2 explicitly left `physFreeReturnSolve` unchanged, so a free-return template remains representative, not predictive, of an authored plane; (c) the launch-time→RAAN derivation (R6.3) computes what plane a given launch time REACHES, and the "window in ~Xh Ym" readout shows when the site next rotates under an already-authored plane, but nothing AUTOMATICALLY schedules or enforces a launch at that computed time — no COAST event is inserted, and authoring launch time twice is last-write-wins on the same `lan_deg` field. A true launch-window solve/scheduler remains future work, now narrower in scope (the geometry is derivable; only the scheduling isn't).
50. **The ΔV accounting's plane-change model is still schematic against the real 3D geometry**: |Δv| for node maneuvers comes from `progNmComputeEdgeDv` (§6 — scalar plane-change terms, patched-conic escapes), while the R3 shooter flies the REAL 3D encounter with that fixed magnitude by adjusting direction only. When the schematic magnitude is more than the real geometry needs, the shooter spends the surplus in aim; when it's less, the leg honestly fails (converged:false, schematic arc). The accounting deliberately stays schematic — required-ΔV totals must remain byte-identical with physics on/off — so the number the user budgets and the geometry they watch are consistent in outcome, not derived from each other. Same class as critique 36 (closest approach, not arrival state, is what's targeted). **R3.2 (§7j) partially addresses the same-body case, opt-in only**: `progNmComputeEdgeDv`'s coaxial same-body transfer (`_nmCoaxialTransferDv`) previously charged NOTHING for two orbits being in different planes at all — worse than "latitude-derived," it was simply absent. A new `priceOrientation` per-node flag (default OFF, no UI yet) lets a specific edge opt into a REAL plane-change charge (`progDvPlaneChangeFull` on the orbits' authored `{i, Ω}`, vectorially combined with the existing tangential burn) — but it is still schematic in the same sense as the rest of this critique (a scalar term bolted onto vis-viva, not derived from the shooter's actual flown geometry), it only covers the same-body coaxial case (transit/escape/ascent edges remain untouched), and it is off by default specifically so this phase cannot silently reprice any existing mission — the ΔV-parity discipline this whole critique is about stays intact until a user deliberately opts a node in.
51. **Free-return coverage is window-limited by construction, and the seed scan's constants are hand-tuned**: encounter estimated at a fixed 4.7 d, apogee offsets +60k/+75k km, 24-point grid with 1/8 + 1/64 refinements and ±16k km apogee sweep (~70 propagations before the corrector runs) — all empirically chosen against the 2026-07 epoch (same magic-number class as critiques 7/24). Departure METs whose lunar phase puts the Moon far from the Ω=0 ring's plane genuinely have no solution at the fixed geometry and fail with the "try a different MET" message rather than a wrong trajectory.
52. **The maneuver gizmo's px→m/s gain (2 m/s/px, 0.2 fine) is a hand-tuned constant with no calibration against anything physical** (§7k) — it was picked so a ~100 px mouse gesture produces a ~200 m/s burn, a "feels right" number for a LEO-class transfer, not derived from screen DPI, current zoom, or the vehicle's actual ΔV budget; a user zoomed in tight for a fine plane-change edit and a user zoomed out for a big interplanetary burn get the identical px-to-m/s ratio, which may feel wrong at either extreme (the plan's "shift = fine" mitigates but doesn't eliminate this). Two further gizmo scope limits from the same phase: the ring-placement MET is "current view time," not an inverted click-angle-to-MET solve (dragging the node along the ring after placement isn't supported, only precise-entry-form editing of MET); and the scratch drag-preview only draws samples in the node's OWN body frame, silently truncating a preview that would cross an SOI boundary mid-drag (the COMMITTED leg, rendered after release via the normal `_trajPhysLegRender` path, has no such limit — only the live-drag preview does).
53. **R3.4's fidelity-ladder debounce (2000 ms) and the center-drag time-gain formula's `kmPerPx` are both hand-tuned/derived-but-uncalibrated constants** (§7k): the 2 s debounce was picked by feel (long enough continuous dragging never trips it, short enough a paused drag refines quickly) with no calibration against actual `physPropagateSegment` cost on a given machine — a slow device doing a full n-body pass that takes noticeably longer than 2 s will show a preview that appears to "hang" mid-refinement with no progress indicator; a fast device gets a fixed 2 s wait even though it could afford full fidelity continuously. Separately, the node-time drag's `dMET = pxAlong·kmPerPx/|v|` is exact GEOMETRY (screen px → km via the current zoom, km → s via the node's own orbital speed) but silently assumes the node's speed stays roughly constant over the dragged MET range — for a highly eccentric orbit dragged near periapsis, `|v|` changes fast enough within a single gesture that the px-to-time ratio measured at drag-start increasingly under/over-shoots as the drag continues (not re-sampled mid-drag). Also inherited from R3.3 and now doubled in importance: the CA pair's target selection (`_trajGizmoPickTarget`) is a simple "next MANEUVER's body, else Moon-if-Earth" heuristic with no notion of whether that maneuver is actually reachable from the node's current authored state — a node authored in a different part of the mission than intended still shows a CA pair against whatever the next-in-log destination happens to be.

**Structural**
19. `calculate()` (frozen) and `destOnOrbitDV` are twin implementations held together only by regression goldens — an intentional physics change requires editing both plus re-capturing goldens, a three-way sync that must never be done casually. If you're editing 160 or 145, stop and re-read §9 first.
20. Bodies are point masses in the underlying two-body/n-body machinery: no atmosphere beyond the fitted ascent losses, no third-body effects beyond what R4's opt-in J2 term and the restricted n-body perturbers (§7d) add, no EML/NRHO/DRO dynamics (those are cataloged as pseudo-orbits with hand-set radii). **Partially resolved by R4 (§7l, 2026-07-10)**: J2 secular precession (nodal/apsidal rates) is now COMPUTED, not merely entered as data, and SSO inclinations can be verified via `physJ2SunSyncCheck` rather than assumed — see critique 54 for what's still hand-waved about it.

54. **R4's J2 constants (`PROG_BODY_J2`, 385, §7l) are standard low-precision published figures, not independently re-derived or cross-checked against a primary ephemeris/geodesy source in this codebase** — same "unstated precision envelope" issue as critique 1's ascent constants. Consequences: (a) no error bars or "valid altitude range" attached (the closed-form secular formula itself also degrades near-singularly at very low/very high altitude and near-polar/near-equatorial geometries, which the code does not flag); (b) higher-order zonal harmonics (J3, J4, ...) are absent entirely — the model captures only the DOMINANT oblateness term, adequate for the "orbital economy" order-of-magnitude use case this phase targets (sun-sync verification, apsidal-drift awareness) but not precision mission design; (c) the optional `physAccel` J2 integrator term uses the ECLIPTIC plane as an equatorial-plane stand-in for EVERY body (documented inline and in §7l) since body obliquity/axial tilt isn't modeled anywhere in this program — for Earth the ecliptic-vs-equatorial obliquity is 23.4°, a real source of error if the integrator term is ever relied on for anything beyond "does precession happen in roughly the right sense," which is all this phase claims for it.

**Presentation layer (R6.2, 2026-07-10)**
55. **Body surface rendering (§7m) has no axial tilt and no calibrated prime-meridian epoch**: every body's spin axis is assumed coincident with the ecliptic normal (real tilts: Earth 23.4°, Mars 25.2°, others unmodeled), and Earth/Mars's sidereal spin angle carries an arbitrary zero-offset at viewT=0 rather than a real GMST/Mars-Ls calibration — so a specific longitude facing a specific screen direction at a specific mission time is coincidence, not a claim this phase makes. The Moon's tidal lock IS geometrically correct (nearside always faces Earth, driven by the same real ephemeris the rest of the program uses) since it needs no tilt or epoch calibration to get right. Jupiter/Saturn bands are procedural placeholders (no real cloud-band data). **Round-4 update (2026-07-11)**: the limb-shading gradient's day-side offset is STILL a cheap 2D screen-space dot (unchanged — good enough for "which way is roughly toward the Sun"), but it is now layered under an actual terminator (an orthographically-projected great-circle night scrim, §7m round-4) that IS a real 3D sun-vector solve — the eclipse/lighting-accuracy gap this critique originally flagged is narrowed to "no axial tilt" (the terminator itself is geometrically exact given the no-tilt approximation, not a further cheapening of it). This is a purely cosmetic layer (§7m's header note) with zero coupling to any ΔV/physics function in the program — the gap is honesty-of-picture, not honesty-of-number. **R6.3 (§7n) deliberately reuses this exact uncalibrated spin convention** for the launch-site marker, the mock ascent curve, and the launch-time→RAAN derivation — internally self-consistent (a site marker always sits where the ascent curve starts, and the derived RAAN always matches what's drawn) but NONE of it is anchored to a real UTC clock; a "launch at t=0" does not correspond to any specific real calendar moment. Calibrating the prime meridian would automatically make the RAAN math (and the marker/ascent visuals) real-time-accurate with no changes to §7n's formulas — they only consume `spinFn`'s output, not its epoch.

56. **Textured-globe raster (§7m R6.4) inherits critique 55's no-axial-tilt/no-epoch-calibration gap wholesale** — the raster is a pixel-for-pixel re-expression of the same no-tilt lat/lon frame the vector layer uses (same inverse of the same `_trajProjectVec`/`_trajSpinRotate`), so it is exactly as geographically "wrong at an angle" as the coastlines it replaces, no better no worse. Additional raster-specific approximations: shading uses the SAME simplified terminator/limb model as the vector layer (no real BRDF, no atmospheric scattering on Venus/Earth, no specular ocean glint); Uranus/Neptune/Mercury/Venus have real base-color imagery but no lat/lon-registered features drawn on top (clouds/bands are baked into the source texture itself, not procedural like Jupiter/Saturn's vector bands); Saturn's texture is a procedurally-synthesized Cassini-like palette (noted in 237-planet-textures.js), not real imagery. The 120ms re-raster throttle means the globe's rotation/lighting can be up to ~120ms stale during a fast rotate-drag — a presentation-only staleness, never a physics one.

57. **Launch-planner DLA (§7p, 415) inherits critique 54/55's no-axial-tilt gap into a new consumer**: `progIdealParkingOrbit`'s declination-of-launch-asymptote (`asin(vInf_z/|vInf|)`) is computed in the ECLIPTIC frame and used directly as if it were a body-equatorial declination — the standard interplanetary-mission-design quantity. For Earth this is off by up to the 23.4° ecliptic-equatorial obliquity (worst case near the equinoxes/solstices of the transfer geometry), which can shift the "ideal" inclination and LAN recommendation by a similarly-sized amount versus a real mission-design tool. `progOptimalDeparture`'s C3/TOF optimum itself is NOT affected (that's pure heliocentric Lambert geometry, no body-frame assumption involved) — only the DLA-derived parking-orbit recommendation inherits the approximation. Same root cause as 54/55 (no axial tilt modeled anywhere in the program); fixing it would mean rotating vInf into a real Earth-equatorial frame (right ascension/declination of the true celestial sphere, obliquity ε ≈ 23.44°) before applying this module's formulas, which is unimplemented.

**MISSION_MODEL_V2 shadow state (§7q, 566)**
58. **A transit-corridor's exiting/arrival edge (e.g. LOI) has no leg to promote a Δv from in Phase 1** — 565 attributes zero propagation to that edge (the injection leg already flew the whole trajectory), yet V1's accounting charges it a real Δv via `progNmComputeEdgeDv`. `v2Reconcile`'s per-burn row for that edge is correctly flagged `withinMargin:false` (never silently dropped) and the mission's TOTALS row cannot close while this gap stands. Phase 2 needs to either give the corridor-exit edge its own promotable leg/burn record, or redefine where that Δv is attributed in the physics-primary model.
59. **`v2DeriveBudget`'s prop-per-burn re-derivation picks the "burning" stage heuristically** — the stage with the largest remaining propellant among those carrying a nonzero `isp` in the anchor's mass snapshot — because Phase 1's promoted anchors don't record WHICH stage actually fired (V1's own `_missionApplyBurn`/`_missionApplyManeuver` resolve this against the live `active` vehicle's targeted stage, information not carried onto the leg/anchor). Δv itself (the D6-compared quantity) is unaffected — it comes straight from `leg.dvVec`, never from this heuristic — but the derived `prop_kg` readout is a best-effort estimate, not yet pinned to a golden.
