60. **LAUNCH's ascent Δv is not compared dv-for-dv against V1 in `v2Reconcile`'s per-burn rows** — a `kind:'launch'` anchor carries no `dvApplied` (ascent isn't a discrete burn in the V2 anchor model), so `v2DeriveBudget` never emits a row for it, and `v2Reconcile`'s TOTALS therefore compare V2's burns-only Δv against V1's `missionBudget().dvExpended`, which DOES include the ascent term. This asymmetry (documented, not hidden) is the other half of why totals don't close on the Apollo seed alongside critique 58's corridor-edge gap — Phase 2 needs to decide how ascent Δv folds into the unified accounting readout. **CLOSED by MISSION_MODEL_V2 Phase 2 S3 (2026-07-14, see entry below)** — `v2DeriveBudget` now sums a dedicated `ascent` field (from `e.stagingResult.dvDelivered`, same field V1 stamps) and `dvTotal = ascent + Σ|burns|`, closing the asymmetry.

**MISSION_MODEL_V2 Phase 2 S1–S3 (2026-07-14, 566/565/570)** — critiques 58 and 60 above, resolved (S4's "stamp these into the user-visible read surfaces" is the remaining step; not done this pass):
- **S1 (F3, deterministic replay identity, 570-mission-manager.js `_missionRekeyVehicleId`):** runtime `vehicleId`s are now `'v_' + missionId + '_' + originKey` (sanitized) instead of a fresh `progUUID()` per replay — derived from `_originKey`, which was ALREADY a stable function of (authored log index, repetition). Verified: two `missionRecompute()` calls on the seeded Apollo mission produce byte-identical `m.vehicleIds` and `JSON.stringify(m.log)`.
- **S2 (F1, critique 58 CLOSED — real arrival burns, 565-physics-mission.js):** the exiting leg of a transit corridor (fromNode.type === 'transit', e.g. the LOI edge) now constructs `leg.arrivalBurn = {t, frame, r, vPre, dvVec}` from a new shared helper `physArrivalStateAt` (factored out of the existing `physArrivalOsculatingElements`, which now calls it — one bracket/finite-difference reconstruction, not two). Convention: `dvVec = v_target − v_corridor_arrival`, where `v_corridor_arrival` is the injection leg's actual propagated arrival state (last periapsis event in the destination frame, reconstructed the same way the R3.1 ring-orientation code already did) and `v_target` is built from the edge's own `toNode` orbit (the real destination dwell orbit, NOT the transit corridor): vis-viva speed at the arrival radius using the target's authored semi-major axis, direction = `ĥ_target × r̂_arrival` (tangential, in the target's authored Ω=0-convention plane — assumes the arrival radius lies in-plane and targets a near-periapsis/circular insertion, consistent with how the corridor's shooter aims). 566's `v2BuildShadow` promotes this exactly like a solved-MNODE burn anchor.
  - **Measured on the Apollo seed** (LAUNCH + TLI + LOI + one manual 500 m/s gizmo burn): LOI edge — **V1 (schematic `progNmComputeEdgeDv`) 638 m/s vs V2 (physics, `physArrivalStateAt`-derived) 1028.29 m/s — Δ = +390.29 m/s (+61%)**. Outside D6 margin, as the spec anticipated; not implausible (1.61×, well under the 2× "investigate as a bug" threshold) — accepted as a real, understood model disagreement, not a construction defect.
  - **Cause:** V1's LOI number is a same-plane schematic Hohmann-arrival circularization Δv computed independent of the actual flown trajectory. V2's number comes from the ACTUAL converged corridor state — which carries the real plane offset the 3-DOF shooter solved for (a 28.5° LEO parking plane departing toward a ~5.145°-inclination lunar plane genuinely costs more to match than a coplanar idealization admits) plus the corridor's true arrival speed/timing, matched against the AUTHORED target LLO plane rather than an arbitrary coplanar one. The dominant contributor to the +390 m/s gap is this plane-alignment/geometry cost that the 2D schematic simply doesn't carry — physics-primary correctly bills for the plane change V1's accounting silently absorbs into "free" circularization. This is exactly the D6 "physics presumed right, understood before flipping" case.
- **S3 (F2, critique 60 CLOSED — ascent budget line, 566-mission-state-v2.js `v2DeriveBudget`):** launch anchors now carry `ascentDv` (read from `e.stagingResult.dvDelivered`, never recomputed) and `v2DeriveBudget` returns `dvTotal = ascent + Σ|burns|`. Measured: `ascent = 9229`, burns `= 3143 (TLI) + 1028.29 (LOI) + 500 (manual) = 4671.29`, `dvTotal = 13900.29` — closes exactly. Against V1 (`missionBudget().dvExpended = 13510`), the total delta (390.29) equals the S2 LOI delta exactly — no unexplained residual; ascent and the other two burns agree with V1 to float noise.
**MISSION_MODEL_V2 Phase 2 S4–S6 (2026-07-14, 570/566/450) — the flip completes:**
- **S4 (stamp-from-V2 + budget delegation):** `missionRecompute`'s tail now overwrites `e.dv_actual`/`e.dvDelivered`/`e.dv`/`e.prop_consumed` on every BURN/MNODE expanded entry FROM `v2DeriveBudget(m.missionId)`'s per-burn rows (matched by `authIdx`), immediately after `v2BuildShadow`. `e.dvRequired`/`e.dvTarget` (solved-edge REQUIRED Δv) are untouched — still `progNmComputeEdgeDv`, per §2's frozen authority. A burn with `dv_ms: null` (an unpromotable corridor edge, §10.3) keeps its V1-computed value rather than being blanked. `missionBudget(m)` now delegates to `v2DeriveBudget(m.missionId)` for `dvExpended`/`propConsumed` (plus the ascent ΔV and ascent-stage prop, both still read off the LAUNCH entry's `stagingResult`, never recomputed) — the old per-entry summation loop is DELETED. This retires the 2026-07-11 aggregate-undercount anomaly (a manual 200 m/s burn moving the total by +3 instead of +200): verified live — adding a manual 200 m/s burn to the seeded mission moved `missionBudget().dvExpended` from 13,900 to 14,100, exactly +200.
  - **A second consistency fix rides along:** `e.result` (SUCCESS/MARGINAL) was computed by the V1 replay against V1's OWN prop-limited delivered Δv, BEFORE the S4 stamp overwrites `dv_actual` with the simulated value — left alone, a burn that V1 marked MARGINAL could show a stamped `dv_actual` that's actually GREATER than `dvRequired` (as happened on the seed's LOI edge: V1 delivered only up to ~822 m/s and marked it MARGINAL, but V2's real arrival-burn Δv is 1028.29 m/s — an over-delivery, not a shortfall). `missionRecompute`'s stamping loop now re-derives `e.result` against the stamped `dv_actual` whenever `e.dvRequired` is set, so flight-readiness checks (572) see an honest required-vs-delivered pair instead of a stale V1 verdict. Verified: after the stamp, the seed's LOI entry (authIdx 2) shows `dvRequired: 822, dv_actual: 1028, result: 'SUCCESS'` — no shortfall finding fires for it (correct: physics delivered MORE than the schematic requirement, the mirror image of critique 58's original framing).
  - **Minor open item, not investigated further this pass:** appending a THIRD manual burn after the seed's existing three, `missionBudget().propConsumed` moved by −67 kg relative to before the burn, instead of increasing by the new burn's own ~3,042 kg. `v2DeriveBudget`'s per-burn prop is re-derived per-anchor from that anchor's OWN mass snapshot (critique 59's heuristic), so a later burn's mass state can shift an EARLIER burn's picked "burning stage" / available-prop numbers slightly as the timeline's owner-key bookkeeping updates — plausible given critique 59's known heuristic-stage-selection limitation, but not root-caused here. Δv accounting (the D6-governed quantity) is unaffected; only the derived prop readout shows the drift. Flagged as a Phase-3 follow-up, not blocking the flip.
- **S5 (deletions, D3):** the legacy `type:'MANEUVER'` replay/predicate branch, `_missionMigrateManeuverEntry` (the lazy-migration shim), and every `detachedFrom` fallback read (570-mission-manager.js card/report text, 572's manual-burn info note, 5745's re-solve-button visibility check) are DELETED outright — `_evIsSolvedManeuver`/`_evManeuverTarget` (570-mission-event-model.js) now recognize only the unified `MNODE{mode:'solved', target:{fromNode,toNode}}` schema; `_evIsManualBurn` is unaffected (it was already MNODE-only). New missions are stamped `modelVersion: 2` at creation (`_missionMake`, 570-mission-lifecycle.js). `buildProgramObject()` (450) therefore always emits missions carrying that stamp; `applyProgramObject()` gates on a new pure predicate `_missionsPassV2Gate(missions)` — any mission blob missing/mismatching `modelVersion:2` refuses the WHOLE program load with a `showAlert` naming the retired v2.0.0 build, mutating nothing (verified: a synthetic v1-shaped program object left `_missions`/`PROG_ACTIVE_PROGRAM` byte-unchanged and popped the alert). `_applySessionObject`/autosave restore share the same gate transitively (they call `applyProgramObject` on `blob.program`) — the restore banner's Discard path is untouched (it only calls `autosaveClear()`, never `applyProgramObject`), so a stale v1 autosave blob can always still be discarded even though Restore will refuse it. Verified live: `_buildSessionObject()` before/after an extra `missionRecompute()` is byte-identical once `savedAt` is stripped (determinism from S1 holding under the S4/S5 changes too).
- **S6 (this section) + accounting-model note:** the mission ΔV/prop/margin numbers a user sees (`missionBudget`, the state panel's "AS OF this event" totals, 572's readiness checks, 577's report) are now **derived from the `VehicleState` timeline** (`v2DeriveBudget`), not summed from parallel V1 bookkeeping. Historically (V1, retired 2026-07-14): the old `missionBudget` walked `m.log` directly, summing `sr.dvDelivered` for LAUNCH and `e.dv_actual`/`e.prop_consumed` for BURN/MANEUVER/MNODE entries — a schematic accounting path independent of any propagated trajectory, which is what let a corridor-exit edge like LOI carry a Hohmann-schematic Δv (638–822 m/s on various seed runs) with no flown trajectory behind it, and what produced the 2026-07-11 aggregate-undercount anomaly S4 retires above. That code path no longer exists.
- **Critique 58: fully CLOSED** (S2, arrival burns real) — see above.
- **Critique 60: fully CLOSED** (S3, ascent line) — see above.
- **Critique 59: still OPEN** (prop-per-burn stage-selection heuristic) — S4 did not change which stage `v2DeriveBudget` picks as "burning"; the minor prop-accounting drift noted above under S4 is a symptom of it. Left for a future pass (Phase 3+).

**MISSION_MODEL_V2 Phase 5a — LEO -> lunar NRHO direct transfer (§7t, 565/430/570/573, 2026-07-14)**
62. **`physSolveNrhoTransfer` (565) is a DIRECT transfer, not a low-energy ballistic-lunar-transfer (BLT)** — an Artemis-class TLI-like impulsive departure with a ballistic Earth-Moon coast, exactly per MISSION_MODEL_V2.md §15's scope. A genuine low-energy BLT (weak stability boundary, invariant manifold capture) is explicitly future work, not attempted here.
63. **Arrival is CO-ORBITAL, not phase-matched (5b, deferred).** The target is the NRHO's own propagated sample state at the arrival epoch (`refOrbitPropagatedStateAt`) — the solver targets a POSITION on the orbit, not "where the Gateway station currently is" (that would require knowing the station's own phase, a multi-vehicle timing problem explicitly deferred to 5b per §15). **5b R1 (2026-07-15, §7ad) closes the MEASUREMENT half of this gap** — phase error is now computed and surfaced (HUD chip, RENDEZVOUS-readiness AMBER) — but the solver itself is unchanged; arrival is still quantized to the nearest perilune crossing regardless of the target vehicle's actual phase. 5b R2 (§7ae, phase-matched arrival selection) and R3 (§7af, terminal phasing burns) have since closed the SELECTION and AUTHORING halves respectively — R4 (dock-link UI) remains open.
64. **Solve method (converged form, 2026-07-14):** (i) **perilune quantization** — arrival epochs are restricted to the NRHO's perilune crossings (tArr ≡ tPeri mod period, within a 2.5-7.3 d coast band; the perilune point sits only ~3,000 km from the Moon's center, physically reachable by a TLI-class departure — see critique 65's history for why a free-floating TOF failed); (ii) closed-form RAAN solve per candidate epoch, aimed at the **target sample's own Earth-frame direction** (Stage-1 fix; near-degenerate with the Moon-center direction at perilune but exact); (iii) coarse 24-point θ grid across all candidate epochs x Ω roots + two-level local θ refinement, scored by the full 3D miss to the FIXED target point; (iv) 2-DOF Newton (`physShootToTarget`, θ + in-plane pitch, fixed arrival epoch, in-plane [x,y] Moon-frame miss), with a 3-DOF [θ, pitch, yaw] escalation kept only if it improves; (v) **Stage-2 mid-course correction**: if the fixed-|Δv| TLI still misses the 2,000 km window, a free small MCC Δv vector at tMcc = TLI + 0.5·TOF is solved by 3-DOF Newton against the target position at the fixed arrival epoch — a near-linear problem (small Δv, multi-day lever arm) that converges in a few iterations. The MCC is a solver-authored burn (like `physFreeReturnSolve`'s — its magnitude is genuinely free); the TLI's accounted |Δv| stays byte-identical to the engine value throughout. Real missions fly exactly this TLI+MCC architecture. Iteration-capped everywhere; never loops; fails cleanly with `converged:false` + `note`.
65. **CONVERGENCE HISTORY (honest record — the first attempt failed; keep this).** The first 5a implementation let the arrival TOF float freely as a Newton variable and solved the departure RAAN against the **Moon's center** direction. Measured (scratchpad t2/t4-t18.js): Newton settled on apolune-phase arrivals — the target sample up to ~60,000 km off the Moon's center, out of the departure plane — and a single fixed-|Δv| burn's out-of-plane authority could not close that gap without leaving the linear regime (Newton-predicted yaw steps >1 rad); best miss stalled at **~14,000-25,000 km**. The fix was architectural, per the follow-on prescription recorded at the time: perilune-quantized arrival epochs + target-direction RAAN (Stage 1) + the MCC burn (Stage 2). **Measured converged numbers** (canonical LEO 185x185 @28.5, t0=0, TLI |Δv| = 3,143 m/s from `progNmComputeEdgeDv('leo','tlc')`): **miss 378 km** (spec window 2,000 km), **TOF 4.71 d**, **MCC 183 m/s**, **insertion 996 m/s**, **total ≈ 4.32 km/s**; across departure epochs t0 ∈ {0, 1 d, 3 d} and dv ∈ {3.1, 3.143, 3.2} km/s: miss 19-378 km, MCC 42-982 m/s, total 4.3-5.1 km/s — all converged (scratchpad t20.js). Gate (`tests/math.test.js` "5a:" block) pins converged:true, miss < 2,000 km, the TOF band, accounting-parity |Δv|, and a loose ~3.0-5.5 km/s total band (approximate on purpose — ephemeris phase moves the MCC/insertion split). Residual honest caveats: the MCC (183 m/s canonical) is larger than real Artemis MCC budgets (tens of m/s) because the TLI's direction-only aim is coarser than a real targeter; and the insertion burn (~1 km/s) exceeds the schematic LOI edge price (645 m/s) because it must also absorb the velocity mismatch at the co-orbital (NOT phase-matched — 5b) perilune arrival.
66. **`devSeedGatewayMission` (573)** exercises the authoring path end to end (LAUNCH bound to `leo-185`, a Gateway station DEPLOYed on `nrho-nominal` at t=0, then the crewed vehicle's own LEO -> NRHO transfer). The transfer leg carries the converged propagated trajectory (kind:'nrho', with `mccBurn` + `nrhoInsertion` records); the node map shows the LEO -> NRHO edge with its TLI / "NRHO insertion" chips (430/570); the budget/report machinery is exercised unchanged — the injection burn's accounted `|Δv|` comes from `progNmComputeEdgeDv`, and the insertion burn flows through the Phase-2 arrival-burn construction (the exit leg's `arrivalBurn`). **Known accounting limit (documented):** the MCC burn lives on the leg record (`leg.mccBurn` — physics/display truth, rendered as part of the stitched trajectory) but is NOT yet an authored log event, so its Δv/propellant is not charged to the mission budget; charging it would mean inserting an authored MNODE mid-leg (the R6.2″ machinery exists) — left as 5b-adjacent follow-on.

**MISSION_MODEL_V2 Phase 4 — NRHO periodicity (§7s, 425/566/570/574/5746, 2026-07-14)**
61. **RETIRED 2026-07-14 by §17 N2 (see §7v):** the catalog now ships the true 9:2 family member (P locked to the literal resonance, perilune/apolune 5,544/71,203 km, rotating-frame closure 345.6 km — tighter than this critique's 468.6 km compromise), and the multi-rev/station-keeping concern is superseded by the rotating-frame phase-wrap semantics (no consumer propagates a seed past one period). Original text kept for history: **The seeded `nrho-nominal` orbit is a shorter-period (4.71 d), weakly-decaying family member, not the literal 9:2 lunar NRHO (6.56 d).** The literal 9:2 apolune (~70,000 km) sits outside the Moon's own SOI (~66,100 km) in this restricted-3-body/SOI-patched integrator (`physSoiRadius`), so a seed placed there escapes rather than closing — the real 9:2 NRHO's near-rectilinear outer arc fundamentally depends on rotating-frame L2 dynamics this integrator doesn't model. The rp=3,000/ra=60,000 km seed that WAS used converges to a clean one-rev closure (468.6 km, well under the 500 km gate) but its perilune distance decays across subsequent revs — measured as low as ~262–330 km by the 3rd rev on some seed variants tried, i.e. trending toward an eventual impact rather than a stable long-term halo. This is an honest property of NRHO-class orbits (weakly unstable by nature) compounded by the corrector only enforcing ONE rev of closure, not a multi-rev invariant-torus constraint. A future pass wanting a longer-horizon-stable seed would need either (a) a true CR3BP-rotating-frame formulation to actually access the L2 halo family the real 9:2 NRHO belongs to, or (b) a multi-rev correction (closure enforced at t0+P, t0+2P, t0+3P simultaneously) rather than single-rev. Consumers (ring rendering, DEPLOY, orbit inspector) all only ever sample/display ONE period from t0 — none of them currently propagate multiple revs, so the decay is not user-visible in this Phase, but is a real latent trap for anything that later wants "station stays on this orbit indefinitely" semantics.

**Launch-card UX pass — Moon planning, plane-match, date picker, LAN propagation bug (§7p/415/570/565, 2026-07-14, user feedback)**

67. **BUG, FIXED — editing a LAUNCH's LAN did not propagate to the trajectory or the maneuver-node gizmo.** Root cause was two independent defects, both in the solved-maneuver leg path (`physRebuildMissionTrajectories`, 565), NOT in the authoring/recompute plumbing (`m.log` → `missionRecompute` → `missionApplyLaunchEdit` correctly wrote `o.lan_deg` all along):
    - **(a) Canonical node templates carry no authored plane.** A solved MNODE's `fromO`/`toO` come from `_missionNmNodeById(fromNode/toNode).orbit` — `PROG_NM_NODES`' (430) static dwell templates (`'leo'`, `'tlc'`, …), which are shared visual anchors with a FIXED `inclination` and no `lan_deg` field at all, not a copy of any particular mission's authored launch plane. Editing the LAUNCH card's LAN therefore changed `e.orbit.lan_deg` but never reached the leg solver at all for the common "fromNode:'leo'" case. **Fix:** `physRebuildMissionTrajectories` now tracks `lastAuthoredPlane` (seeded from the LAUNCH event's own `e.orbit.{body,inc_deg,lan_deg}`, and updated from each subsequent solved edge's own authored `toO.lan_deg` when present) and overlays it onto a same-body canonical `fromO` that itself carries no `lan_deg` before calling `physSolveNodeBurn`/`physShootLegAim`. This is additive — an edge whose `fromO` already authors its own plane (e.g. via a T4 orbit-inspector fork/custom node) is left alone.
    - **(b) The differential-corrector's leg cache omitted the plane from its key.** The n-body leg shooter (`_physShootCache`, 565, ~line 1556) memoizes the solved burn aim (`theta/pitch/yaw/raan`) by a signature string that already included the departure inclination ("editing a node's inclination must re-shoot the leg" — R3) but NOT the LAN/RAAN — so even after fix (a) correctly threaded the new LAN into `fromO`, a warm cache entry from the OLD plane kept being returned unchanged. This is what produced the reported symptom precisely: "the maneuver node sticks to the old orbit." **Fix:** the signature now includes `fromO.lan_deg` (`|o${lanLeg}` suffix).
    - **Separately, a dead-but-latent key mismatch was also fixed for hygiene:** `active.orbitState`'s plane field is named `lan` everywhere it's constructed (380/390/570 all use this convention for the LIVE vehicle-physics orbit state), but every 565 consumer of `orbitAtBurn`/`fromOrbit`/`toOrbit` reads `lan_deg` (the authored `e.orbit`/node-orbit convention). `e.orbitAtBurn = {...active.orbitState}` (570, manual-MNODE burn-frame cache) silently dropped LAN through this mismatch for any manual (non-solved) MNODE burn. Fixed by also carrying `lan_deg: active.orbitState.lan` onto `orbitAtBurn`. `orbitState` itself was NOT renamed (its `lan` key has many other readers across 380/390/430/572/5745 — renaming it was out of scope and riskier than the two targeted fixes above).
    - **Verified in-browser** (preview, `devSeedApolloMission({force:true})`): before the fix, LAN 0°→90° left `_physTrajByMission[...].legs[0].samples[0].r` byte-identical; after the fix, the same edit moves the leg's first sample from `[1961.6, -6246.1, -344.6]` to a distinctly different position — confirmed for both the LAN value alone (0→90) and combined with a `Plan for destination` re-optimize.
68. **`progMoonPlaneAt` (415) reports the Moon's INSTANTANEOUS osculating plane, not its mean plane.** The Moon's true orbital plane precesses (nodal regression, ~18.6-year period, driven by `PROG_MOON_ELEMENTS.OmDot`) and nutates on shorter timescales; `r×v` at one instant captures exactly that epoch's plane, which is the physically correct answer to "what plane do I need to be coplanar with to intercept a lunar transfer departing near this MET" but will NOT match a textbook "the Moon's orbit is inclined 5.14° to the ecliptic" mean-element figure exactly, and will drift if the resolved inc/LAN pair from one epoch is reused unchanged for mission planning much later than that epoch (the launch card always recomputes fresh from the CURRENT `edit-launch-time`/program epoch when "Match plane" is clicked, so this is a caveat for anyone caching the numbers externally, not a live bug in the tool).
69. **The Moon's "ideal parking orbit" (item 1) targets COPLANAR with the Moon's plane at TLI time, not a genuine free-return or minimum-plane-change trade.** A real mission design sometimes prefers a slightly non-coplanar departure (accepting a small TLI plane-change penalty) in exchange for a better free-return abort profile, or times the parking-orbit LAN to a specific TLI window rather than the instantaneous plane. Coplanar is the natural zero-order answer (minimizes the LOI-side plane-change cost) and is what's implemented; the trade-space beyond it is unmodeled, same spirit as critique 57's DLA-frame approximation for the interplanetary case.

**MISSION_MODEL_V2 §17 N3 — switchable reference frames (§7x, 574/425, 2026-07-14)**
70. **`body-fixed` frame only freezes the ANCHOR body's own surface features — it does not re-express other bodies'/markers' apparent sky motion due to that body's spin.** A true planetographic view (as seen from a fixed point on a rotating body) would show the Moon and other bodies sweep across the sky once per rotation period; this renderer's `body-fixed` frame only cancels the spin baked into the anchor's OWN globe texture/site markers (§7x seam (d)) — other bodies still render at their raw `viewT` position (seam (c)), unaffected by the anchor's spin. Extending this would mean threading `t=viewT` through `_trajFrameTransform('body-fixed', anchorBody, ...)` for every OTHER body's glyph too — geometrically straightforward (the formula already supports it) but deliberately out of scope for N3 (the agreed use case was "launch-site work", i.e. freezing the ground track, not a full planetographic sky view). Documented, not hidden.
71. **The heliocentric/moon Keplerian reference rings (`trueRingPath`, analytic `progOrbitSamplePoints`) render at a single `viewT` snapshot regardless of frame** — they have no per-point epoch to thread through the transform (an osculating ellipse is evaluated at one epoch by construction), so in a rotating frame they show that ellipse's shape rotated into the frame's orientation AT VIEWT, not a truly time-varying frame-relative shape. This is consistent (every other body glyph is also a viewT snapshot, seam (c)) but is a coarser treatment than the propagated-orbit rings (seam (b)), which genuinely close live from real per-sample epochs. No consumer currently needs a frame-relative Keplerian ellipse to be more than a viewT snapshot; flagged for whoever eventually does.
